# Internal Security Flashcards

> One flashcard per Act, institution, committee, scheme, operation, classification, landmark event, accord and framework in the repository Internal Security owners (basic + advanced), plus concept cards per theme.
> **Verification status:** built from the repository owners with claims checked against Wikipedia and other pages that loaded while building; confirmed items carry a ✅ source tag; unverified details are marked ⚠️. Time-sensitive items carry as-of dates from the owner. mha.gov.in, FATF and other official sites were mostly unreachable. IDs: IS-001–174, IS-201–441.
> Owner conflicts and uncertain items are flagged inside the cards.

---

## INTERNAL SECURITY — Foundations, Terrorism, LWE, North-East, J&K, Borders

> Fragment prefix **IS**. Source tags: `(✅ owner NN)` = repository owner (Basic/Advanced, NN = owner number); `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = fetched during this build; `⚠️` = unverified against a fetched source (memory or owner-only). Current figures carry an as-of date or are marked ⚠️. MHA / PRAHAAR / NATGRID pages could not be fetched (403 / 404).

### GROUP — Internal-Security Foundations and Governance (owner 01: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-001 — Internal security vs external security

**Front:** How does Indian doctrine distinguish internal security from external security, and which ministry owns each?

**Definition (learn):** Internal security is the security of the country within its borders: maintenance of peace, law and order and the upholding of sovereignty inside its territory. External security is protection against aggression by a foreign country.

**Breakdown:**
- Internal security: police-led, with armed police support where needed (`✅ owner 01`, citing Singh, PDF p. 1).
- External security: the armed forces' responsibility (`✅ owner 01`).
- Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) = internal security; Ministry of Defence = external security (`✅ owner 01`).
- The two domains are inter-related: neighbourhood events (Sri Lanka, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Nepal, Myanmar) have direct or indirect linkages with India's internal security (`✅ owner 01`; ⚠️ the "linkage" wording is the owner's paraphrase of Singh).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Internal security became a separate GS-III topic in the Civil Services Main Examination from 2013 (`✅ owner 01`, Singh PDF p. 2). Ministry of Home Affairs: nodal ministry (`✅ owner 01`).

**Links:** IS-002 (Kautilya fourfold), IS-003 (attributes), IS-005 (Seventh Schedule), IS-004 (eight-element doctrine).

**Trap:** Treating the two as separable silos. The owner's explicit trap: internal and external cannot be seen "in isolation from each other." Also: "external" does not mean "military" only; internal security also involves central forces under Union List Entry 2A.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Distinguish internal from external security and explain why India cannot treat them in isolation." Qualified conclusion: the two boundaries blur through proxies, cyber and finance, so the answer must name the actor and the domain, not only the ministry.

**Back:** *"Inside the wall, police and the Home Ministry hold; beyond it, the soldier and the Defence Ministry. Neighbours' trouble crosses the wall."*

### Flashcard IS-002 — Kautilya's fourfold threat classification

**Front:** What are the four categories of threat in Kautilya's classification as used in Indian internal-security discourse?

**Definition (learn):** A four-way typology of threats to a state: internal; external; internally aided external; externally aided internal.

**Breakdown:**
- Internal: originates and acts within the state (e.g., Left-Wing Extremism, organised crime).
- External: a foreign state acting directly (e.g., cross-border shelling/infiltration).
- Internally aided external: domestic actors help an external threat.
- Externally aided internal: insurgents or extremists receive foreign support.
- India's threat perception is "a mix of all four shades" (`✅ owner 01`, Singh PDF pp. 1-2).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Attributed in the owner to Kautilya's *Arthashastra* (`✅ owner 01`, `✅ owner 00`). ⚠️ The attribution was not confirmed in the fetched Wikipedia Arthashastra page (the fetched portion did not contain the classification).

**Links:** IS-001, IS-003, IS-004; owner 00 Section 2 (threat classification table).

**Trap:** Assigning the four labels by the *place* of the actor rather than by the *direction of aid*. "Internally aided external" is inside-out (domestic help to external hostile actor). "Externally aided internal" is outside-in. Also, an "internal" threat can still have external logistical links (LWE).

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Threat perception in India is a mix of all four shades." Discuss. Qualified conclusion: the classification helps diagnose the mix, but the state must still locate the actor's operational base, funding and recruitment separately.

**Back:** *"Inside-out and outside-in: the two cross-currents make four — pure in, pure out, and the two that help each other."*

### Flashcard IS-003 — Attributes of internal security

**Front:** List the attributes of internal security that a threat can undermine.

**Definition (learn):** The conditions a stable internal order must preserve: territorial integrity and sovereignty; domestic peace; law and order; rule of law and equality before law; freedom from fear; peaceful co-existence and communal harmony.

**Breakdown:**
- Six attributes (`✅ owner 01`, Singh PDF p. 2): territorial integrity/sovereignty; domestic peace; law and order; rule of law and equality before law; freedom from fear; peaceful co-existence and communal harmony.
- A Mains answer must name the attribute a specific threat undermines (e.g., LWE undermines rule of law and freedom from fear).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh, *Challenges to Internal Security of India* (book-period source, `✅ owner 00`).

**Links:** IS-001, IS-004, IS-007 (threat/vulnerability/capability).

**Trap:** A "law and order" only answer is incomplete. The attributes are a list of six; an answer that names only "law and order" misses the rights and social-harmony dimensions.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Name the attributes of internal security that communal violence undermines." Qualified conclusion: communal violence threatens peaceful co-existence first, and then law and order; the damage to rule of law is the one that outlasts the riot.

**Back:** *"Six pillars, not one: Territory, Peace, Order, Rule, Fearlessness, Harmony — TPOR-FH."*

### Flashcard IS-004 — Singh's eight-element internal-security doctrine

**Front:** What are the eight elements of India's internal-security doctrine (as stated by Singh)?

**Definition (learn):** A diagnostic grid for internal security: political; socio-economic; governance; police and security forces; Centre-State coordination; intelligence; border management; cyber security.

**Breakdown:**
- Political: distinguishes coercion (secessionism: "a heavy hand") from accommodation (regional and ethnic aspirations: "softer and sympathetic approach") (`✅ owner 01`, PDF p. 5).
- Socio-economic and governance: development and administrative reform.
- Police and forces; Centre-State coordination; intelligence (Multi Agency Centre cited as "a good beginning"); border management; cyber security (Singh: India "has just made a beginning") (`✅ owner 01`, PDF pp. 5-6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh's doctrine (book-period, `✅ owner 01`). Owner mapping: political → legal/political consensus (topics 02, 05); socio-economic → schemes (03, 04); border → BADP/BIM/CIBMS (06); cyber → CERT-In/NCIIPC (08).

**Links:** IS-003, IS-005, IS-006, IS-007, IS-008 (MAC).

**Trap:** The eight elements are hypotheses to test, not a checklist to recite. The doctrine does not itself say which element should lead in a given area. Also, it is not purely a police or security doctrine.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "India's internal-security doctrine must be political, socio-economic and governance-based, not merely a police and intelligence doctrine." Qualified conclusion: the doctrine already includes the non-kinetic elements, but the weakest element differs by threat; the answer must name which.

**Back:** *"Please Stop Guns, Police Can Improve Border Cyber: P-S-G-P-C-I-B-C, the eight spokes."*

### Flashcard IS-005 — Constitutional division: Seventh Schedule entries and Article 355

**Front:** Which Seventh Schedule entries and which Article govern the Centre-State division of internal-security powers?

**Definition (learn):** Police and public order are State subjects; the Union may deploy armed forces in a State only in aid of the civil power, and has a protective duty under Article 355.

**Breakdown:**
- State List Entry 1: public order (excluding Union armed-force use) (✅ Wikipedia: Seventh Schedule of the Constitution of India).
- State List Entry 2: police, including railway and village police, subject to Union List Entry 2A (✅ Wikipedia: Seventh Schedule).
- Union List Entry 2A: deployment of any armed force of the Union in any State "in aid of the civil power" (✅ Wikipedia: Seventh Schedule; ✅ owner 00/01).
- Union List Entry 8: Central Bureau of Intelligence and Investigation (✅ Wikipedia: Seventh Schedule).
- Union List Entry 9: preventive detention for reasons connected with defence, foreign affairs or the security of India (✅ Wikipedia: Seventh Schedule).
- Union List Entry 5 (arms, firearms, explosives) and Entry 21 (piracy on high seas): ⚠️ from owner 00; not verified in the fetched text.
- Article 355: the Union's duty to protect every State against external aggression and internal disturbance (`✅ owner 01`; ⚠️ article text not fetched).
- Punchhi Commission (as cited in Laxmikant, owner 00): a localised emergency short of Article 356 is justified under Article 355 read with Union List Entry 2A and State List Entry 1 (`✅ owner 00`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Seventh Schedule of the Constitution (1950). ⚠️ The insertion of Entry 2A by the 42nd Amendment (1976) was not confirmed in the fetched page; the owner says so, and Wikipedia's text mentions the 42nd Amendment only for omitted entries.

**Links:** IS-001, IS-006 (Centre-State coordination), IS-008 (Multi Agency Centre), IS-016 (NCTC federalism).

**Trap:** Reading "in aid of the civil power" as the Centre taking over policing. Entry 2A is aid, not replacement. Another trap: State List Entry 1 expressly excludes Union armed forces used in aid of civil power.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Centre-State coordination is a structural, not incidental, constraint on India's internal security response." Qualified conclusion: the Centre owes a duty under Article 355 but can act only through instruments that are State-facing; the constraint is constitutional design, not reluctance.

**Back:** *"State holds the Police and Peace (List II 1-2); the Union holds the Force in aid (2A), the Intelligence (8), and the Preventive hand (9); Article 355 is the duty."*

### Flashcard IS-006 — Centre-State coordination as a structural problem

**Front:** Why does Singh treat Centre-State coordination as a structural rather than administrative problem?

**Definition (learn):** Police and law and order sit with States, while intelligence, border guarding and several central agencies sit with the Centre; coordination must therefore run "from intelligence to operations."

**Breakdown:**
- Coordination exists "in all areas from intelligence to operations" and needs "an institutional framework which resolves all these centre-state coordination problems" (`✅ owner 01`, PDF p. 6).
- The constitutional asymmetry: the Union can deploy forces but only in aid of the civil power, and owes Article 355 duties (`✅ owner 00`, Section 6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period); Seventh Schedule (IS-005).

**Links:** IS-005, IS-008, IS-016.

**Trap:** "Centre-State coordination is a post-2010 problem" is wrong; Singh frames it as a structural feature of the federal division of policing powers (`✅ owner 01`, Advanced Section 8).

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — discuss Centre-State coordination as a structural constraint. Qualified conclusion: institutions such as MAC/SMAC reduce friction, but the sovereignty of the State police means cooperation is negotiated, not commanded.

**Back:** *"Police is the State's, forces are the Centre's: the bridge is coordination, and the bridge is built on consent."*

### Flashcard IS-007 — Threat, vulnerability, capability and consequence (risk model)

**Front:** Define threat, vulnerability, capability and consequence in the risk model used in the internal-security owners.

**Definition (learn):** Risk is the potential that a threat will exploit a vulnerability to cause harm. Threat = hostile actor/event with source, vector and target. Vulnerability = exploitable weakness. Capability = the state's actual means of prevention and response, not merely its legal power. Consequence = the adverse result when threats exploit vulnerabilities.

**Breakdown:**
- Threat: e.g., ISI-sponsored module (topic 05) or CPI (Maoist) cadre (topic 03).
- Vulnerability: an undemarcated or porous border stretch (topic 06).
- Capability: trained and equipped border-guarding manpower (topics 06, 12).
- Consequence: a successful attack, displacement or loss of legitimacy.
- Definitions from VisionIAS *Challenges to Internal Security through Communication Network*, PDF p. 14 (`✅ owner 00`, `✅ owner 01`). Owner tags the capability definition as analytical inference (⚠️).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Risk framework; source is a VisionIAS compendium, not an official text.

**Links:** IS-003, IS-004, IS-019 (intelligence-to-prosecution), owner 00 Section 4A.

**Trap:** Treating a threat and a vulnerability as the same. The policy levers act mainly on vulnerability and capability, because hostile intent is largely outside India's control.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Why is X a serious threat?" asks about convergence of threat, vulnerability and consequence. Qualified conclusion: most "what should be done" answers are really about reducing vulnerability and building capability.

**Back:** *"Threat knocks, Vulnerability opens, Capability answers, Consequence counts: T-V-C-C."*

### Flashcard IS-008 — Multi Agency Centre (MAC)

**Front:** What is the Multi Agency Centre, and what role did Singh assign to it?

**Definition (learn):** An intelligence-sharing platform for coordination among central and state agencies. Singh calls it "a good beginning" on intelligence coordination at the doctrine stage.

**Breakdown:**
- Cited by Singh as "a good beginning" (`✅ owner 01`, PDF p. 6).
- Owner 00 Section 6: MAC/SMAC as an information-sharing platform, not an operational command (`✅ owner 00`).
- ⚠️ Institutional status must be checked against the MHA Annual Report (owner 01 Section 8: book-period claim).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Post-26/11 revamp (`✅ owner 02`, Singh PDF pp. 15-16). Headquartered in MHA (⚠️ memory).

**Links:** IS-005, IS-006, IS-034 (NIA Act), IS-019.

**Trap:** Treating MAC as an investigating agency. It shares intelligence; it does not arrest, investigate or prosecute.

**Mains hook:** Use MAC as the example of cooperative federalism in intelligence sharing (15 marks on Centre-State coordination). Qualified conclusion: information sharing is necessary, but a lead is not evidence.

**Back:** *"MAC shares, never seizes: the Multi Agency Centre is a meeting room for intelligence, not a cell block."*

### Flashcard IS-009 — Official Secrets Act, 1923 (scope and PYQ route)

**Front:** What does the Official Secrets Act, 1923 cover, and how is it distinct from the RTI Act?

**Definition (learn):** A colonial-era statute on spying, prohibited places and wrongful communication of official information, distinct from the RTI Act's disclosure regime.

**Breakdown:**
- Covers prohibited-place, spying and wrongful-communication offences, not every government document (`✅ owner 01`, Section 12 routed safety note).
- It remains in force; it was not repealed by any ARC recommendation (`✅ owner 01`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Official Secrets Act, 1923. 2023 Prelims GS-I Q92 routed to this owner, key unavailable locally (`✅ owner 01`, PYQ integration 2018-2023).

**Links:** IS-010 (Arms Act), IS-011 (BNSS Zero FIR).

**Trap:** Confusing the OSA's prohibited-place offences with RTI-based disclosure. Being a secrecy statute does not make all government documents secret.

**Mains hook:** Use in governance-transparency answers: OSA is a security-secrecy law; RTI is a transparency law; a qualified conclusion requires separating national security from accountability.

**Back:** *"Secrets of the place, not of the paper: OSA guards the prohibited place and the spy, RTI opens the rest."*

### Flashcard IS-010 — Arms Act, 1959 (licensing, possession and dealing)

**Front:** What are the distinctions in the Arms Act, 1959 that the owner tests?

**Definition (learn):** A licensing framework for acquisition, possession and carrying of arms (section 3), and a separate framework for manufacture, sale, transfer and dealing (section 5).

**Breakdown:**
- Section 3: acquisition, possession, carrying (licence).
- Section 5: manufacture, sale, transfer and related dealing.
- A licensed arm, a prohibited arm and an intelligence or security offence are not interchangeable (`✅ owner 01`).
- ⚠️ Section numbers are owner-stated; not verified against India Code in this build.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Arms Act, 1959. 2023 Prelims GS-I Q92 routed (key unavailable locally).

**Links:** IS-009, IS-099 (foreign links), IS-146 (Myanmar border).

**Trap:** Treating possession and manufacture as the same offence. Sections 3 and 5 are separate.

**Mains hook:** Use in LWE or North-East answers: arms supply is a vulnerability; licensing and dealing controls are capability. Qualified conclusion: licensing controls help only where diversion is monitored.

**Back:** *"Three holds the licence, five makes the trade: Section 3 carries, Section 5 deals."*

### Flashcard IS-011 — Zero FIR under BNSS section 173

**Front:** What does BNSS, 2023, section 173 provide about Zero FIR and registration?

**Definition (learn):** Information about a cognisable offence may be given to an officer in charge irrespective of the area where the offence occurred; the station then transfers the case to the competent police station.

**Breakdown:**
- Zero FIR is a registration and access device, not a jurisdictional transfer of investigation (`✅ owner 01`).
- It does not make electronic reporting compulsory in every case.
- A preliminary inquiry has its own statutory threshold (`✅ owner 01`).
- BNSS replaced CrPC from 1 July 2024 (✅ Wikipedia: Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, in force 1 July 2024).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** BNSS, 2023, in force 1 July 2024. 2026 Prelims GS-I Q62 (Zero FIR and police jurisdiction) routed to owner 01; provisional key present locally, answer not inferred.

**Links:** IS-009, IS-033 (BNS 2023).

**Trap:** Thinking a Zero FIR must be registered electronically, or that a Zero FIR makes the registering station the investigating station. The fetched and owner sources both say the station transfers the case.

**Mains hook:** Use in rights and criminal-justice answers: Zero FIR widens access to justice; a qualified conclusion requires transfer and investigation capacity.

**Back:** *"Any station takes the first word, the right station takes the case: Zero writes, the proper one investigates."*

### Flashcard IS-012 — Foundations concept card: root causes and doctrine logic

**Front:** What are the root causes of internal insecurity in Singh's account, and how do they route to doctrine elements?

**Definition (learn):** Causes that create the conditions for a threat: unfriendly neighbours, poverty, unemployment, inequitable growth, widening gap between haves and have-nots, governance deficit, communal and caste divide, porous borders with tough terrain, and a poor criminal justice system that lets corruption enable organised crime.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh's root-cause list (`✅ owner 01`, PDF pp. 4-5).
- Routing to doctrine: governance deficit → governance element; poverty/inequality → socio-economic element; porous border → border management; criminal justice → police/forces element.
- Owner 00 root-cause matrix: governance deficit (01, 03, 04); development/resource grievance (03, 04); identity (04, 05, 09); sponsorship (02, 05); porous border (04-07); criminal-financial (08, 10, 11); digital (08, 09).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period); owner 00 matrix.

**Links:** IS-004, IS-007, IS-013 (development–extremism).

**Trap:** Saying root causes are only external (hostile neighbours). Singh lists poverty, unemployment and governance deficit as equally significant domestic causes.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Development is the solution to extremism." Discuss. Qualified conclusion: exclusion becomes a security risk only when an organised actor converts it into recruitment, coercion or parallel authority.

**Back:** *"Neighbours, poverty, jobs, gaps, governance, caste, border, courts: eight root causes, and every one is a doctrine element missing."*

### Flashcard IS-013 — Development-extremism causal thesis (conditional thesis)

**Front:** What is the correct conditional thesis linking poverty and exclusion to extremism?

**Definition (learn):** Exclusion, weak justice and administrative absence become security risks only when an organised actor converts them into recruitment, coercion or a parallel authority.

**Breakdown:**
- Do not say poverty automatically produces violence (`✅ owner 01`, Section 12).
- Causal chain: land or forest or service-delivery exclusion → local grievance; weak policing or justice → coercive parallel authority (`✅ owner 01`).
- Match measures: secure access, restore police and civil administration, deliver rights and services, protect accountability.
- Close: reduced violence is a necessary security result, not the end-state of legitimate governance (`✅ owner 01`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis; Singh's Maoist "vested interest in keeping poverty alive" (`✅ owner 01` Advanced, PDF p. 59).

**Links:** IS-012, IS-055 (LWE root causes), IS-056 (instrumental underdevelopment), IS-103 (identity vs resource-driven).

**Trap:** A scheme launch or road is not evidence that violence fell or grievance was resolved.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Connect development and extremism" (GS-III named phrase). Qualified conclusion: the feedback loop means development can be blocked by the threat itself, so sequencing must be area-specific.

**Back:** *"Poverty is the tinder, the organiser is the spark; remove the spark, and the tinder becomes development's problem."*

### Flashcard IS-014 — Coercion vs. political settlement (political element)

**Front:** How does Singh's political element distinguish responses by threat type?

**Definition (learn):** Secessionist or separatist demands are met with "a heavy hand" and stringent laws; regional aspirations and ethnic demands require a reasonably softer and sympathetic approach.

**Breakdown:**
- Coercion for secessionism (`✅ owner 01`, PDF p. 5).
- Accommodation for regional and ethnic demands (`✅ owner 01`).
- North-East is the clearest accommodation case (topic 04); J&K proxy war draws a harder posture (topic 05) (`✅ owner 01` Advanced Section 5).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh's doctrine (book-period).

**Links:** IS-004, IS-086 (instrument ladder), IS-110 (J&K proxy war).

**Trap:** "Heavy hand is the universal prescription." It is prescribed for secessionism only (`✅ owner 01`, Advanced Section 8).

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Distinguish the coercive and accommodative strands of India's internal-security doctrine." Qualified conclusion: the calibration depends on the actor and root cause; coercion without accountability can itself become a root cause.

**Back:** *"Secession gets the heavy hand, identity gets the open hand: Coerce the separatist, settle the aspirant."*

### Flashcard IS-015 — "One border, one force" principle

**Front:** What is the "one border, one force" principle and who recommended it?

**Definition (learn):** The principle that each border should be guarded by a single force, so that the force is not distracted from its guarding mandate by other internal-security duties.

**Breakdown:**
- Recommended by the Kargil Review Committee / Group of Ministers (`✅ owner 00`, Section 6).
- Recurring measures point across topics 04-07 (`✅ owner 00`).
- ⚠️ The recommendation is owner-stated; not verified in a fetched primary text in this build.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Kargil Review Committee (1999). ⚠️ Year is from memory.

**Links:** IS-148 (force-to-border mapping), IS-149 (Kargil to Group of Ministers).

**Trap:** Treating it as a binding statute. It is a committee recommendation.

**Mains hook:** Border-management answers: one force per border helps command clarity; the qualification is capacity and the status of any border-guarding reform.

**Back:** *"One border, one guard: one force, one line, no split duty."*

### Flashcard IS-016 — NCTC and federalism (cross-link to terrorism owner)

**Front:** Why was the National Counter Terrorism Centre contested, and what is the federalism objection?

**Definition (learn):** A proposed apex counter-terror body under IB's administrative control, opposed because its proposed search and arrest powers could be used without State Government involvement.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh: "As per the constitutional provisions, law and order is a state subject. The powers conferred on the NCTC to search, arrest individuals and carry out independent investigations without involving the State Government is viewed as an infringement" (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 18).
- Second objection: arrest powers for an intelligence body breach the principle of not granting arrest powers to intelligence agencies (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 18).
- ⚠️ NCTC's present status not verified; the owner says it was never implemented as originally conceived.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Post-26/11 (2008) proposal (`✅ owner 02`).

**Links:** IS-005, IS-006, IS-008 (MAC), IS-034 (NIA Act).

**Trap:** Saying NCTC is India's apex counter-terror body. Owner says it was not implemented as originally conceived.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "India's counter-terror architecture is cooperative-federal, not centralised, by constitutional necessity." Qualified conclusion: NCTC shows coordination needs can be real yet constitutionally unworkable when they bypass State policing.

**Back:** *"NCTC: the coordinator who wanted to arrest without asking: the federal check said no."*

### Flashcard IS-017 — Intelligence informs; it does not arrest (institutional boundary)

**Front:** What is the single most examinable institutional boundary in the internal-security owners?

**Definition (learn):** Intelligence agencies inform prevention and response. They do not arrest, investigate or prosecute. Intelligence-to-prosecution chain: prevention (intelligence) → containment (police/forces) → investigation (State police/NIA) → prosecution (special courts).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 00 Section 6: the four-stage chain (`✅ owner 00`, PDF pp. 19-21).
- Singh: the principle of not granting arrest power to intelligence agencies (`✅ owner 00`, PDF p. 18).
- Attachment-vs-conviction gap: arrests and attachments are not convictions (`✅ owner 00` Section 8).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh's counter-terror chain.

**Links:** IS-008, IS-016, IS-034 (NIA Act), IS-046 (four evidentiary levels).

**Trap:** Describing NIA as an intelligence agency or MAC as an investigating agency. Owner 00 specifically warns against institution conflation.

**Mains hook:** Any counter-terror question: name the chain stage and its owner. Qualified conclusion: intelligence is a lead, not admissible proof.

**Back:** *"Intelligence whispers, police contain, investigators prove, courts decide: the whisperer never cuffs."*

### Flashcard IS-018 — Statute vs capability (evidence discipline)

**Front:** Why does a statutory power not prove operational capacity, and what evidence discipline does the owner require?

**Definition (learn):** A statute confers authority; it does not create trained manpower, forensic capacity, court time or inter-agency trust. An answer must separate the section relied upon from dated reporting of practice and outcome.

**Breakdown:**
- Notification is not implementation; a ceasefire is not a settlement; an arrest is not a conviction (`✅ owner 01`, Advanced Section 6).
- Evidence unit: claim → named evidence → why it supports → limitation/status (`✅ owner 00`, Section 10A).
- Current-anchor rule: institutional status requires the MHA Annual Report edition (`✅ owner 01`, Section 9).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis; no fixed date.

**Links:** IS-007, IS-019, IS-044 (PRAHAAR).

**Trap:** Quoting a law as evidence of delivery. The owner's advanced trap is that evidentiary-level discipline is itself an answer-quality differentiator.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Laws exist; implementation is the problem." Discuss. Qualified conclusion: statutory power is necessary but evidence of throughput must come from dated official reporting.

**Back:** *"Power on paper is not power on the ground: write the section, then write the capacity."*

### Flashcard IS-019 — Intelligence to prosecution: the chain and its failure points

**Front:** Name the four stages of the counter-terror chain and the institution that owns each (as set out in the owners).

**Definition (learn):** Prevention (intelligence) → containment (police and security forces) → investigation (State police or NIA) → prosecution (Special courts).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 00 Section 6, PDF pp. 19-21 (`✅ owner 00`).
- Owner 02 table: IB/State police for prevention; CAPF/NSG for response; NIA/State police for investigation; Special NIA courts for prosecution (`✅ owner 02`, Section 10).
- Each stage needs a distinct tool; overbroad coercion can create recruitment vulnerability (`✅ owner 02`, Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh's four-function chain (`✅ owner 02`, Section 3).

**Links:** IS-017, IS-018, IS-033 (BNS), IS-034 (NIA Act), IS-036 (Special Courts).

**Trap:** Stage collapse. An arrest is containment or investigation, not conviction.

**Mains hook:** 15-20 marks: explain how to convert a chain into a rights-respecting response. Qualified conclusion: the chain works only if each stage has capacity and accountability.

**Back:** *"Watch, Stop, Probe, Judge: four links, four owners, one chain that breaks at any weak link."*

### Flashcard IS-020 — Foundations: Basic owner summary (Mains spine)

**Front:** What is the Mains answer spine for any internal-security question?

**Definition (learn):** Define the specific threat; identify root causes and the weakest doctrine element; name the actor type; cite the current institution; separate announced from implemented measures; name the binding constraint; offer a calibrated way forward; close by relating to the doctrine.

**Breakdown:**
- Eight-step spine from owner 00 Section 10 (`✅ owner 00`).
- Demand decoder: development–extremism, external actors, area management (`✅ owner 01` Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-004, IS-007, IS-013, IS-017.

**Trap:** Generic opening "India faces many internal-security challenges." Owner says naming the weak element wins marks.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — synthesis question on LWE, North-East and proxy terrorism comparison. Qualified conclusion: an integrated response reduces exploitable vulnerability without treating every dissenting citizen as a proxy.

**Back:** *"Name, root, actor, institution, status, constraint, way, close: N-R-A-I-S-C-W-C."*

### GROUP — Terrorism and Counter-Terror Architecture (owner 02: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-021 — Working definition of terrorism

**Front:** How does Singh define terrorism, and why is there no internationally agreed definition?

**Definition (learn):** "The planned, organised and systematic use of violence as a means of coercion for political or religious or ideological purposes" (`✅ owner 02`, Singh PDF p. 7).

**Breakdown:**
- Planned, organised, systematic violence; purpose is coercion for political, religious or ideological ends (`✅ owner 02`).
- No internationally agreed definition: "a 'terrorist' in one country may be viewed as a 'freedom fighter' in another" (`✅ owner 02`).
- Owner 02 flags the categories: hinterland, J&K militancy, North-East insurgency, LWE (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 9).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period). Statutory definition: UAPA "terrorist act" (IS-029) and BNS Section 113 from 1 July 2024 (IS-033).

**Links:** IS-022, IS-024, IS-029, IS-033.

**Trap:** Treating "terrorism" as a single category. The owner separates it from insurgency and Naxalism (IS-022). Also, the "freedom fighter" line means definitional disputes are political, not a reason to avoid defining.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Define terrorism and explain why no global consensus exists." Qualified conclusion: a working definition built on purpose and method (coercion for political, religious or ideological ends) allows law to act without resolving every political dispute.

**Back:** *"Planned, organised, systematic, coercive, for a cause: P-O-S-C-C; one man's terrorist, another's freedom fighter."*

### Flashcard IS-022 — Terrorism vs insurgency vs Naxalism

**Front:** Distinguish terrorism, insurgency and Naxalism as used in Singh's account.

**Definition (learn):** Terrorism is the general category of planned political violence. Insurgency is an act of rebellion and armed struggle with public support for the insurgents. Naxalism is the use of violence to destabilise the state through communist guerrilla warfare.

**Breakdown:**
- Terrorism: general category (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 22).
- Insurgency: "an act of rebellion and armed struggle... with public support for the insurgents" (`✅ owner 02`).
- Naxalism: "to destabilise the state through communist guerrilla warfare" (`✅ owner 02`). Owner 02 Section 6 frames it as capturing state power.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-021, IS-024, IS-052 (LWE definition), IS-103 (identity vs resource-driven).

**Trap:** Using "insurgency" and "terrorism" interchangeably. Insurgency's distinguishing feature is public support; terrorism's is coercion without necessarily local base.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Distinguish terrorism, insurgency and Naxalism." Qualified conclusion: the labels overlap in practice (a Maoist group can use terror tactics), so the classification should be made by objective and base, not by a single tactic.

**Back:** *"Terror strikes, insurgency holds ground with people, Naxal goes guerrilla for the state: T, I, N."*

### Flashcard IS-023 — State actor, non-state actor and the proxy logic

**Front:** Explain the proxy concept, deniability and the difference between an external state actor and a non-state actor in Singh's account.

**Definition (learn):** An external state actor is a government directly or indirectly engaged in terrorism. A non-state actor acts without direct government association, but may have de facto state support, which gives the sponsor deniability.

**Breakdown:**
- Terrorism in Kashmir is "a direct manifestation of state policy of Pakistan and ISI influence" (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 8).
- LeT and Indian Mujahideen claim to be non-state actors but have de facto support from Pakistan (`✅ owner 02`).
- "None of the so called 'non-state actors', like the LeT, could have operated with impunity without active funding, logistical and military support from Pakistan" (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 8).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-002 (externally aided internal), IS-024, IS-026, IS-050 (J&K owner 05).

**Trap:** Treating state-sponsored and non-state categories as mutually exclusive. Owner 02 says use proxy/deniability framing. Also, not every non-state group is independent of a state.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Non-state actors are frequently proxies for state sponsors seeking deniability." Discuss with reference to terrorism in India. Qualified conclusion: deniability is a means, not proof that the sponsor and the group share legal personality. Attribute a current incident only to a dated competent-agency finding.

**Back:** *"The proxy wears the mask, the sponsor keeps the hands clean: deniable front, sponsor's ISI."*

### Flashcard IS-024 — Categories of terrorism in India

**Front:** What are the four categories of terrorism in India in Singh's framework?

**Definition (learn):** Hinterland terrorism; J&K militancy; North-East insurgency; Left-Wing Extremism.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh's categories (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 9). The last three are developed as dedicated topics 03, 04 and 05.
- Owner 02 Section 12: organise manifestations by hinterland/module, J&K proxy, North-East insurgency and LWE-linked violence.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-021, IS-022, IS-023, IS-049 (PYQ routes).

**Trap:** Using a chronology of attacks rather than the categories to answer "how has terrorism manifested in India." Owner 02 warns about this.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "How has terrorism manifested in India? Elaborate with contemporary examples." Qualified conclusion: the categories are analytical; each needs its own actor, base and institutional route.

**Back:** *"Hinterland, Kashmir, Northeast, Red corridor: H-K-N-R, four doors into one house of fear."*

### Flashcard IS-025 — Sleeper cell

**Front:** What is a sleeper cell?

**Definition (learn):** A dormant agent or cell placed in a target country or organisation, activated only on order.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh's definition (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 12).
- Operational value to a sponsor is deniability and timing control.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-023, IS-026, IS-039 (26/11 owner context).

**Trap:** Treating every radicalised individual as a sleeper cell. A sleeper cell requires a dormant, activatable link. Evidence must come from an investigation, not from profile.

**Mains hook:** Use in online-radicalisation answers (topic 09). Qualified conclusion: dormancy makes early detection the key; it also raises civil-liberties risks if detection is profiling.

**Back:** *"Asleep until the order wakes it: the sleeper waits, the handler decides."*

### Flashcard IS-026 — Terror funding sources (state, extortion, FICN, hawala)

**Front:** What funding sources does Singh record for militancy, and which categories rely on them more?

**Definition (learn):** Funding through state sponsorship and private resources: drugs and contraband money, donations, charities, diaspora and NGO funding, extortion and taxation, counterfeit currency (FICN), hawala channels and organised-crime linkage.

**Breakdown:**
- J&K militancy as "a classical case of state-sponsored and financed terrorism," with ISI using drug money, donations, charities, Kashmiri diaspora and NGO funds (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 13).
- Hinterland, North-East and LWE draw more heavily on domestic extortion, taxation and smuggling (`✅ owner 02`, ⚠️ owner analytical inference).
- Funding flows are developed in IS-064 (topic 10 terror finance) (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period); FICN and hawala as labelled in owner 02 diagram.

**Links:** IS-023, IS-024, IS-046, IS-050 (complexity chain).

**Trap:** Using funding as proof of sponsorship. A funding channel shows a route, not an attribution.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on terror financing. Qualified conclusion: finance disruption works only with proof of flow and conviction, not just freezing or attachment.

**Back:** *"Money from drugs, donations, diaspora, forged notes and hawala: the purse of terror has many pockets."*

### Flashcard IS-027 — TADA (1985-1995)

**Front:** What was TADA, when was it in force, and why did it lapse?

**Definition (learn):** The Terrorist and Disruptive Activities (Prevention) Act, the first anti-terrorism statute in India to define and counter terrorist activities, which lapsed after widespread allegations of abuse.

**Breakdown:**
- First enacted 1985; came into effect 24 May 1985 (`✅ Wikipedia: TADA`).
- Re-enacted in 1987 (assented 3 September 1987) after lapse and an ordinance (`✅ Wikipedia: TADA`).
- Renewed in 1989, 1991 and 1993; allowed to expire in 1995 (`✅ Wikipedia: TADA`).
- Abuse indicators (as reported by Wikipedia, citing a 1994 figure): more than 76,000 arrested by 30 June 1994; about 25% cases dropped; about 35% reached trial; ~95% of those acquitted; under 2% convicted (`✅ Wikipedia: TADA`). ⚠️ Treat these as secondary-source figures; not from a primary dataset.
- Supreme Court: *Arup Bhuyan v. State of Assam* held that mere membership of a banned organisation does not make a person punishable under the Act (`✅ Wikipedia: TADA`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** TADA 1985 (re-enacted 1987); expired 1995 (`✅ owner 02`, Singh PDF pp. 16-17).

**Links:** IS-028 (POTA), IS-029 (UAPA), IS-031 (UAPA 2019).

**Trap:** Saying TADA is still in force. It expired in 1995. Also the 1985/1987 date confusion: enactment 1985, re-enactment 1987.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Anti-terror laws widen investigative power and invite misuse." Discuss with TADA and POTA as precedents. Qualified conclusion: each expansion of power needs an accountability safeguard, or the law loses legitimacy.

**Back:** *"Eighty-five in, ninety-five out: TADA came with a wide net and left with a bad name."*

### Flashcard IS-028 — POTA (2002-2004)

**Front:** What was POTA, when was it enacted and repealed, and what made its confession provision contentious?

**Definition (learn):** The Prevention of Terrorism Act, 2002, enacted after the December 2001 Parliament attack and repealed in 2004.

**Breakdown:**
- Passed 26 March 2002 in a joint session after the Rajya Sabha defeated the Bill; assent 28 March 2002 (`✅ Wikipedia: POTA 2002`).
- Repealed 21 September 2004 by ordinance; Repeal Act assented 21 December 2004 (`✅ Wikipedia: POTA 2002`).
- Confessions made to a police officer were admissible as evidence, unlike ordinary Indian law (`✅ Wikipedia: POTA 2002`).
- Owner 02: enacted after the December 2001 Parliament attack (`✅ owner 02`, Singh PDF pp. 16-17).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** POTA 2002; repealed 2004.

**Links:** IS-027, IS-029, IS-040 (2001 Parliament attack).

**Trap:** "POTA is still in force" or "POTA was passed in 2004." It was repealed in 2004.

**Mains hook:** Use with TADA in the rights-accountability spine. Qualified conclusion: the repeal shows that misuse concerns can change the law, but the next law must still meet the necessity case.

**Back:** *"Two-zero-zero-two in, two-zero-zero-four out: POTA trusted the confession, and the Repeal Act trusted nothing."*

### Flashcard IS-029 — UAPA, 1967: enactment and amendment lineage

**Front:** When was the Unlawful Activities (Prevention) Act enacted, and what is its amendment history?

**Definition (learn):** India's principal anti-terror statute, designed as the special law against unlawful activities and terrorist acts.

**Breakdown:**
- Act No. 37 of 1967; assent 30 December 1967 (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`).
- Amendments: 1969, 1972, 1986, 2004, 2008, 2012 (per owner 02, `✅ owner 02`), and 2019 (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA` infobox lists 1969, 1972, 1986, 2004, 2008, 2019 and mentions 2012 in History).
- Owner 02 counts six pre-2019 amendments; the 2019 one is the post-book amendment.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** UAPA 1967; 2019 Amendment (IS-031).

**Links:** IS-030, IS-031, IS-032, IS-033, IS-047 (forum choice).

**Trap:** Saying UAPA's amendment history ends in 2012. The 2019 amendment is the one most likely to be tested.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "The UAPA amendments have widened state power." Qualified conclusion: each widening has been paired with a due-process debate; widening is the security case, safeguards are the rights case.

**Back:** *"Sixty-seven to nineteen: the Act was born in 1967 and grew by amendment; the last big growth was 2019."*

### Flashcard IS-030 — UAPA 2012 widening (economic offences) and bail under section 43D(5)

**Front:** What did the 2012 UAPA amendment add, and why is section 43D(5) the operative rights question?

**Definition (learn):** The 2012 amendment widened "terrorist act" to include economic offences such as fund-raising and counterfeiting. Section 43D(5) restricts bail where the case diary shows reasonable grounds to believe the accusation is prima facie true.

**Breakdown:**
- 2012: economic offences within "terrorist act" (`✅ owner 02`, PDF; `✅ owner 02` advanced Section 5).
- Section 43D(5): bail may not be granted if reasonable grounds exist for believing the accusation is prima facie true (`✅ owner 02`).
- Wikipedia: pre-charge-sheet period extended to 180 days, against the usual 60 to 90 days; 2021 Supreme Court ruling that violation of speedy trial can justify bail (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** UAPA 2012 amendment; Section 43D(5).

**Links:** IS-029, IS-031, IS-046 (designation vs prosecution), IS-047 (forum choice).

**Trap:** Thinking detention length is the core rights question. Owner 02 says bail is the operative deprivation of liberty.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "UAPA's bail regime and the rights of the accused." Qualified conclusion: arrest and conviction can diverge sharply in time, so bail is the proportionality test.

**Back:** *"Forty-three D, subsection five: no bail when the case looks true, and the time in custody is the real sentence."*

### Flashcard IS-031 — UAPA 2019: designation of individuals as terrorists

**Front:** What did the UAPA (Amendment) Act, 2019 change about designation?

**Definition (learn):** The Central Government may designate an individual, not only an organisation, as a terrorist, on grounds such as committing, preparing for, promoting or being involved in terrorism.

**Breakdown:**
- Before 2019 only organisations could be labelled terrorist (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`).
- Designation is an executive notification under the Fourth Schedule with a statutory review or denotification route (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ The Fourth Schedule reference is owner-stated; Wikipedia does not mention the schedule.
- Designation is not a judicial finding or conviction (`✅ owner 02`, trap).
- Critics say it allows designation without a formal judicial process (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** UAPA (Amendment) Act, 2019. Parliament passed the NIA Bill on 17 July 2019 (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`).

**Links:** IS-029, IS-030, IS-046 (four evidentiary levels), IS-032.

**Trap:** Designation equals guilt. It is executive, reviewable and not a conviction. Also, the individual designation power is a 2019 innovation, not 2012.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "The 2019 amendment shifted UAPA's centre of gravity from organisations to individuals." Qualified conclusion: the security case is that organisational bans are defeated by renaming; the due-process case is that the consequence arrives before adjudication.

**Back:** *"Organisations banned, now people named: the list grew a name, and the name needs a review, not a verdict."*

### Flashcard IS-032 — UAPA 2019: NIA Inspector-rank investigation and DG NIA approval

**Front:** What changed in investigation authority under UAPA 2019 for NIA cases?

**Definition (learn):** NIA officers of Inspector rank or above may investigate UAPA cases; in NIA-investigated cases, property seizure or attachment requires approval by the Director General, NIA.

**Breakdown:**
- Inspector rank or above (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`; `✅ owner 02`). Previously Deputy Superintendent or ACP (`✅ Wikipedia: UAPA`).
- DG NIA approval for seizure/attachment, replacing the State DGP (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ Not verified in a fetched primary text.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** UAPA 2019.

**Links:** IS-031, IS-034 (NIA Act 2008), IS-035 (NIA 2019).

**Trap:** Treating the rank change as a change in NIA's jurisdiction. The rank change concerns who can investigate, not which offences NIA covers.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on federal investigation: DG NIA approval moves attachment authority from State to Centre in NIA cases. Qualified conclusion: capacity and review are what make this a rights-safe reform.

**Back:** *"Inspector can probe, the DG must sign the seizure: rank lowered the gate, the top signs the paper."*

### Flashcard IS-033 — Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023: terrorist act and organised crime

**Front:** What did the BNS (in force 1 July 2024) add about terrorism and organised crime?

**Definition (learn):** For the first time, the general penal code defines a terrorist act (owner: Section 113) and organised crime (owner: Section 111). UAPA remains the special law.

**Breakdown:**
- BNS in force 1 July 2024, replacing the IPC of 1860 (`✅ Wikipedia: BNS`).
- Owner 02: BNS s.113 (terrorist act) and s.111 (organised crime) (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ Wikipedia fetch did not state the section numbers; verify in India Code.
- Owner 02: an officer not below the rank of Superintendent of Police decides whether a case proceeds under BNS or UAPA (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ Not verified in a fetched primary text.
- BNSS replaced CrPC; BSA replaced the Evidence Act on the same date (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** BNS, BNSS and BSA, 2023; in force 1 July 2024.

**Links:** IS-029, IS-047 (forum choice), IS-011 (BNSS s.173).

**Trap:** Saying BNS repealed UAPA. Both operate concurrently.

**Mains hook:** 10 or 15 marks: "Does the BNS terror provision make UAPA unnecessary?" Qualified conclusion: concurrent statutes shift discretion to the investigating officer; guidelines and review are needed.

**Back:** *"One-one-three in the general code, UAPA the special: two laws, one choice, and the choice is the question."*

### Flashcard IS-034 — National Investigation Agency Act, 2008

**Front:** When was the NIA Act enacted, and what is NIA's jurisdiction?

**Definition (learn):** Central law creating the National Investigation Agency to investigate scheduled offences, including terror-related offences, anywhere in India with concurrent jurisdiction.

**Breakdown:**
- NIA Act, 2008; presidential assent 31 December 2008 (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`).
- NIA formally constituted 20 January 2009 (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`).
- Concurrent jurisdiction for terror-related offences (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 15).
- Schedule at enactment: offences against sovereignty, bomb blasts, hijacking, attacks on nuclear installations, counterfeit currency, human trafficking, narcotics, organised crime, Atomic Energy/WMD Act violations (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 18).
- Headquarters: CGO Complex, New Delhi (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`).
- NIA operates on scheduled offences at State request or Centre's direction (`✅ owner 02` Advanced Section 4).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NIA Act 2008 (assent 31 Dec 2008); NIA constituted 20 Jan 2009 (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`); created after 26/11 (`✅ owner 02`).

**Links:** IS-035, IS-036, IS-032, IS-008 (MAC), IS-016 (NCTC).

**Trap:** Calling NIA an intelligence agency. It is an investigating agency. Also: "NIA Act 2008, NIA created 2008" — formal constitution was 2009.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "NIA is a federal investigating agency that does not displace State police." Qualified conclusion: concurrent jurisdiction plus State request makes it cooperative federalism.

**Back:** *"Two thousand eight the law, two thousand nine the agency: NIA probes, not spies, and it asks before it walks in."*

### Flashcard IS-035 — NIA (Amendment) Act, 2019

**Front:** What did the NIA (Amendment) Act, 2019 add?

**Definition (learn):** Amendment extending NIA's jurisdiction to scheduled offences committed outside India against Indian citizens or interests, adding scheduled offences, and allowing designation of Special Courts.

**Breakdown:**
- Scheduled offences abroad against Indian citizens or Indian interests, probed under Indian law, treaties and the law of the country of offence (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`; examples: Sri Lanka Easter bombings, Kabul gurdwara attack).
- New scheduled offences: human trafficking, cyber terrorism, prohibited arms, counterfeit currency, explosive substances (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`; `✅ owner 02`). Explosive Substances Act, 1908 (`✅ owner 02`).
- Sessions Courts may be designated as Special Courts (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ Wikipedia did not confirm this specific change.
- Parliament passed the Bill on 17 July 2019 (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NIA (Amendment) Act, 2019.

**Links:** IS-034, IS-036, IS-031, IS-032.

**Trap:** Thinking the extraterritorial power lets NIA act on any foreign crime. It covers scheduled offences against Indian citizens or interests abroad.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Expanded NIA reach abroad: sovereignty or security?" Qualified conclusion: reach follows the victim's Indian nexus; the operating limit is the host state's law and treaties.

**Back:** *"Abroad against Indians, the scheduled list grew: NIA crosses borders, bounded by treaty and host law."*

### Flashcard IS-036 — Special NIA Courts (Sections 11 and 22)

**Front:** How are Special NIA Courts constituted, and what powers do they have?

**Definition (learn):** Courts notified under sections 11 and 22 of the NIA Act to try scheduled offences, presided over by a judge appointed on the recommendation of the High Court's Chief Justice, with powers of a Court of Session and day-to-day trial.

**Breakdown:**
- Notified under sections 11 and 22 (`✅ owner 02`). ⚠️ Section numbers owner-stated; not fetched.
- Day-to-day trials; appeal to High Court (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 18).
- Wikipedia: "there are 38 Special NIA Courts" (`✅ Wikipedia: NIA`, count as-of date not given). ⚠️ Count is an undated, unverified figure; verify from NIA or MHA.
- States may also appoint Special Courts (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NIA Act, 2008; amendment 2019 (IS-035).

**Links:** IS-034, IS-035, IS-019 (prosecution stage).

**Trap:** Quoting the owner's book-period count as current. Owner 02 asks for verification.

**Mains hook:** Use in prosecution-capacity answers. Qualified conclusion: specialised courts speed trials, but throughput needs dated data.

**Back:** *"Eleven and twenty-two open the doors, sessions powers, day trials; count them fresh."*

### Flashcard IS-037 — NATGRID (National Intelligence Grid)

**Front:** What is NATGRID, when was it proposed, and what is its operational status?

**Definition (learn):** An intelligence-sharing grid linking multiple agency databases so that authorised security agencies can access them. Proposed after 26/11.

**Breakdown:**
- Proposed after 26/11 (`✅ owner 02`, Singh PDF pp. 15-16).
- Singh (book-period): "yet to become operational" (`✅ owner 02`).
- VisionIAS 2024 describes it as an integrated intelligence master database structure linking databases from several security agencies (`✅ owner 02`, *Communication Network* PDF p. 27). ⚠️ Treat as secondary.
- ⚠️ Wikipedia's National Intelligence Grid page returned 404; current status not verified.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Post-26/11 proposal (2008); status unverified.

**Links:** IS-008, IS-017, IS-019.

**Trap:** Treating NATGRID as an investigating agency or as the same as NIA. It is a data-sharing intelligence tool (`✅ owner 02`, trap).

**Mains hook:** Use in intelligence-coordination answers. Qualified conclusion: database linkage is a capability; its legal access safeguards decide whether it is a rights risk.

**Back:** *"Grid for data, not for arrest: NATGRID links the files, and the files never make the arrest."*

### Flashcard IS-038 — NSG and the four post-26/11 hubs

**Front:** Describe the National Security Guard and its regional hubs.

**Definition (learn):** A central armed police force for crisis response, raised in 1984, with a regional-hub network expanded after 26/11 so that a response does not depend on distant Manesar.

**Breakdown:**
- Raised 16 October 1984 after Operation Blue Star; National Security Guard Act, 1986 (`✅ Wikipedia: NSG`).
- Parent ministry: Ministry of Home Affairs (`✅ Wikipedia: NSG`).
- Manesar: operational headquarters (`✅ Wikipedia: NSG`).
- Owner 02: four new hubs after 26/11 at Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Hyderabad (`✅ owner 02`, PDF p. 16).
- Wikipedia: Mumbai (26 SCG); Chennai (27 SCG, set up 2009); Kolkata (29 SCG, 2009); Hyderabad (28 SCG); Gandhinagar (30 SCG, operationalised July 2018, fifth hub) (`✅ Wikipedia: NSG`). Owner's list omits Gandhinagar.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NSG 1984; NSG Act 1986; hubs 2008-2018.

**Links:** IS-039 (26/11), IS-044 (PRAHAAR response capacity), IS-004 (police and forces element).

**Trap:** Saying NSG is part of the Army. It is an MHA-controlled armed police force. Also, Gandhinagar is a fifth hub not in the owner list.

**Mains hook:** Use as the response-capability example in terrorism answers. Qualified conclusion: hubs cut response time; prevention and intelligence still decide whether the response is needed.

**Back:** *"Manesar commands, five hubs respond: the Guard came late to Mumbai, so the hubs were spread."*

### Flashcard IS-039 — 26/11 Mumbai attacks (2008) and the institutional response

**Front:** What did the 26/11 attacks trigger in India's counter-terror architecture?

**Definition (learn):** The November 2008 Mumbai attacks exposed coordination failures and led to creation or strengthening of NIA, NATGRID, MAC (revamped), a proposed NCTC, four new NSG hubs and a revamped Coastal Security Scheme.

**Breakdown:**
- Mumbai Police and NSG were applauded, yet the response "brought to light serious flaws in coordination" (`✅ owner 02`, PDF pp. 14-15).
- Institutions: NIA (IS-034), NATGRID (IS-037), MAC (IS-008), NCTC (IS-016), NSG hubs (IS-038), Coastal Security Scheme (`✅ owner 02`, PDF pp. 15-16).
- Attack dates and tolls are not stated in the owner; ⚠️ not added here.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 26/11 (2008).

**Links:** IS-034, IS-037, IS-038, IS-016, IS-008, IS-044 (PRAHAAR).

**Trap:** Saying NCTC was created after 26/11. It was proposed; its federal objections meant it was not created as conceived.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Crisis events drive institutional design rather than long-term planning." Discuss with 26/11. Qualified conclusion: post-crisis institutions help, but the test is their operational integration.

**Back:** *"Twenty-six eleven taught coordination: a city under siege, a dozen institutions born, one lesson on the wire."*

### Flashcard IS-040 — December 2001 Parliament attack

**Front:** Why does the 2001 Parliament attack matter in the counter-terror timeline?

**Definition (learn):** The December 2001 attack on the Parliament complex, which the owner cites as the trigger for POTA.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 02 says POTA was enacted after the December 2001 Parliament attack (`✅ owner 02`, PDF pp. 16-17).
- ⚠️ Casualty figures and perpetrator details are not in the owner or fetched source; omitted.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** December 2001; POTA 2002 (IS-028).

**Links:** IS-028, IS-027.

**Trap:** Dating POTA to the 2001 attack itself. POTA was enacted in 2002.

**Mains hook:** Use in legal-evolution answers. Qualified conclusion: crisis-led laws often outlive their emergency, which is why sunset and review matter.

**Back:** *"Oh-one in Parliament, oh-two in law: the crisis came first, the statute followed."*

### Flashcard IS-041 — 1993 Mumbai serial blasts (book-period chronology)

**Front:** What place does the 1993 Mumbai attack hold in Singh's chronology?

**Definition (learn):** The 1993 Mumbai serial blasts appear first in Singh's book-period list of important terrorist attacks, running from 1993 Mumbai to 2014 Patna.

**Breakdown:**
- Listed as a book-period chronology item (`✅ owner 02`, PDF pp. 10-11).
- Owner warns not to cite this list as current or complete (`✅ owner 02`).
- ⚠️ Casualty and accused details not verified; omitted.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1993.

**Links:** IS-039, IS-040.

**Trap:** Citing a book-period attack list as the current or complete record.

**Mains hook:** Use only as historical context in an "evolution of terrorism" answer. Qualified conclusion: use chronology to show pattern, not to claim current status.

**Back:** *"Ninety-three opens the list: history for context, not for the present tense."*

### Flashcard IS-042 — 2025 Pahalgam attack: investigation status

**Front:** What is the correct way to describe the 2025 Pahalgam case in a Mains answer?

**Definition (learn):** The 2025 Pahalgam terror-attack case was transferred by MHA to NIA. NIA's later material describes an alleged Pakistan-linked conspiracy and local facilitation.

**Breakdown:**
- Use "NIA is investigating" or "has chargesheeted," not "proved" or "convicted" (`✅ owner 02` Section 8).
- Do not add casualty figures, identities or attribution beyond the dated NIA record (`✅ owner 02`).
- ⚠️ Date (April 2025) and details from memory; not verified in a fetched source.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2025; NIA investigation (`✅ owner 02`).

**Links:** IS-034, IS-046, IS-023 (proxy logic), IS-044 (PRAHAAR).

**Trap:** Converting a chargesheet into a conviction, or an alleged sponsor into an adjudicated fact.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on contemporary manifestation (PYQ 2025 Q9). Qualified conclusion: investigative stage facts can be stated; adjudication requires court outcome.

**Back:** *"Investigating is not convicting: NIA chargesheets, the court decides, and the Mains answer reads the stage."*

### Flashcard IS-043 — NIA's 2025 Jammu-Kashmir module searches

**Front:** What do NIA's 2025 Jammu and Kashmir module searches illustrate?

**Definition (learn):** Searches illustrating investigation of alleged infiltration, logistical facilitation and drone or arms-delivery channels.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 02 uses them as stage illustrations (`✅ owner 02`, Section 8).
- Searches, seizures and charges are disruption or investigation stages, not a judicial finding (`✅ owner 02`).
- ⚠️ Specific dates and counts not verified.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2025 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-023, IS-026, IS-044 (drone threat).

**Trap:** Treating a search as conviction or a charge as proof.

**Mains hook:** Use in cross-border and drone-threat answers. Qualified conclusion: disruption is necessary, but adjudicated outcome is the test of effectiveness.

**Back:** *"Search the house, seize the drone, file the charge: each is a stage, none is the verdict."*

### Flashcard IS-044 — PRAHAAR (MHA, 23 February 2026)

**Front:** What is PRAHAAR, and what does it formalise?

**Definition (learn):** MHA's National Counter-Terrorism Policy and Strategy, described as India's first comprehensive national counter-terror policy document, organised around prevention, response, aggregating capacities, human rights and rule of law, attenuating conditions, international alignment and recovery/resilience.

**Breakdown:**
- Seven elements (`✅ owner 02`, Section 8).
- Explicitly recognises cyberattacks, drone-based attacks and dark web or cryptocurrency misuse (`✅ owner 02`).
- Works through existing coordination mechanisms (MAC, Joint Task Force on Intelligence, NIA, State units); no new agency (`✅ owner 02`).
- ⚠️ MHA page returned 403; the 23 February 2026 date and element list are owner-stated only, not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 23 February 2026 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-008, IS-034, IS-037, IS-045, IS-019.

**Trap:** Treating PRAHAAR as a new agency or as an operational statute. It is a policy and strategy framework.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Critically examine PRAHAAR as a policy-coordination framework." Qualified conclusion: policy architecture helps coordination, but its practical test is whether prevention work receives resources comparable to response.

**Back:** *"Seven petals, one policy, no new agency: PRAHAAR coordinates what exists."*

### Flashcard IS-045 — UNSC Counter-Terrorism Committee Delhi Declaration (2022)

**Front:** What is the UNSC CTC Delhi Declaration and why is it relevant to India's counter-terror policy?

**Definition (learn):** A declaration adopted at a special UN Security Council Counter-Terrorism Committee meeting hosted by India in 2022, on countering the use of new and emerging technologies for terrorist purposes.

**Breakdown:**
- Cited alongside GCTF's Berlin Memorandum on unmanned aerial systems and FATF's 2018 virtual-asset guidance (`✅ owner 02`, *Communication Network* PDF p. 23).
- ⚠️ Adoption year and host details from owner; not fetched.
- IR owns the diplomatic negotiation; the internal-security effect is domestic (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-044, IS-167 (UAV), IS-049.

**Trap:** Treating a UN declaration as binding domestic law.

**Mains hook:** Use in technology-and-terror answers. Qualified conclusion: international soft-law shapes norms; domestic enforcement needs statute and capacity.

**Back:** *"Delhi declares, the world listens: emerging tech, soft law, domestic rules still needed."*

### Flashcard IS-046 — Four evidentiary levels: designation, arrest, charge-sheet, conviction

**Front:** Why must designation, arrest, charge-sheet and conviction be kept apart?

**Definition (learn):** Four distinct stages in counter-terror law: executive designation (UAPA Fourth Schedule), arrest (investigative act), charge-sheet (prosecutorial step), and conviction (judicial finding beyond reasonable doubt).

**Breakdown:**
- Designation is executive and reviewable; conviction requires a Special Court trial to the criminal standard (`✅ owner 02` Advanced Section 8).
- Arrest and charge-sheet are not proof (`✅ owner 02`).
- Rights critique: the first carries reputational consequences without the second's threshold (`✅ owner 02` Advanced Section 2).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** UAPA 2019 designation power (IS-031).

**Links:** IS-031, IS-030, IS-032, IS-042.

**Trap:** The "single most common rights-analysis error" in this topic: conflating designation with conviction (`✅ owner 02`).

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on UAPA's rights calculus. Qualified conclusion: the proportionality test is evidentiary level plus bail, not the label alone.

**Back:** *"Designate, arrest, charge, convict: four doors, and only the last is the verdict."*

### Flashcard IS-047 — Forum-choice problem: BNS 113 vs UAPA after 1 July 2024

**Front:** What is the forum-choice problem created by BNS Section 113 alongside UAPA?

**Definition (learn):** The same conduct can attract BNS s.113 (general law, State-police-led) or UAPA (special law with NIA concurrent jurisdiction, stricter bail under s.43D(5) and sanction under s.45). An officer not below SP rank makes the choice.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 02 Advanced Section 2: forum choice determines bail regime, sanction requirement and investigating agency (`✅ owner 02`).
- Owner flags that this discretion needs guidelines (`✅ owner 02`).
- ⚠️ Section 45 sanction requirement is owner-stated; not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** BNS, 1 July 2024.

**Links:** IS-033, IS-030, IS-029.

**Trap:** Thinking BNS replaced UAPA. Both operate.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Concurrent special and general laws create discretion that needs guidelines." Qualified conclusion: the discretion is the federal and rights question after 2024, not "who investigates."

**Back:** *"Two laws, one officer's pen: the choice decides bail, sanction and agency."*

### Flashcard IS-048 — Prevention vs response: measurement asymmetry

**Front:** Why is prevention under-resourced compared with response?

**Definition (learn):** Prevention produces an absence of incidents and is hard to evidence. Response produces countable outputs such as arrests, seizures and hubs opened.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 02 Section 3: prevention vs response measured differently (`✅ owner 02`).
- PRAHAAR's attenuating-conditions element is the prevention half (`✅ owner 02`).
- Owner 01 Advanced: this asymmetry, not indifference, is the usual reason prevention is under-resourced (`✅ owner 01`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-044, IS-019, IS-039.

**Trap:** Treating an absence of attacks as proof that prevention works.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Prevention is better than cure, but prevention is harder to measure." Qualified conclusion: use proxy indicators, and fund community and counter-radicalisation work alongside response.

**Back:** *"Silence is hard to count: prevention gets no ribbon, response gets the photo."*

### Flashcard IS-049 — Terrorism PYQ routes (2019, 2021, 2025)

**Front:** Which PYQ routes does the terrorism owner cover, and what is the answer structure for each?

**Definition (learn):** 2019 GS-III Q19 (UAPA and NIA Act amendments, 15 marks); 2021 GS-III Q20 (terrorism complexity, causes, linkages, nexus and eradication, 15 marks); 2025 GS-III Q9 (manifestation and counter-measures, 10 marks).

**Breakdown:**
- 2019: UAPA/NIA 15-mark spine with security rationale and due-process qualification (`✅ owner 02`).
- 2021: complexity chain (ideology, sponsorship, finance, logistics, propaganda) linked to IS-050, topic 10, topic 11 and topic 09 (`✅ owner 02`).
- 2025 Q9 verbatim: "Terrorism is a global scourge. How has it manifested in India? Elaborate with contemporary examples. What are the counter measures adopted by the State? Explain." (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQs 2019, 2021, 2025.

**Links:** IS-022, IS-024, IS-044, IS-050.

**Trap:** Using chronology of attacks for 2025 Q9 and missing the policy route (PRAHAAR) and the categories.

**Mains hook:** Answer 2025 Q9 with four categories, the chain, PRAHAAR and a dated example. Qualified conclusion: counter-terror is a prevention-to-prosecution chain, and legal power must be matched by capacity.

**Back:** *"Nineteen, twenty-one, twenty-five: three routes into one terror question; define, map, measure, qualify."*

### Flashcard IS-050 — Terrorism complexity chain: ideology to fear

**Front:** Draw the causal chain for complex terrorism and explain why each link needs a distinct tool.

**Definition (learn):** Ideology or grievance or external sponsorship → recruitment → finance, logistics and technology → attack and propaganda → fear and legitimacy loss.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 02 Section 12 chain (`✅ owner 02`).
- Each link needs its own tool; overbroad coercion can create recruitment vulnerability (`✅ owner 02`).
- Verdict prefers prevention and legitimate prosecution to eradication by force alone (`✅ owner 02`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-023, IS-026, IS-048, IS-049.

**Trap:** Treating "eradication" as the realistic end-state. Owner 02's verdict prefers prevention.

**Mains hook:** 15 or 20 marks — "Terrorism's complexity and nexus demand eradication." Qualified conclusion: force reduces the chain's output; prevention and legitimacy reduce the input.

**Back:** *"Grievance feeds recruits, recruits fund attacks, attacks breed fear: cut the feed, not just the shooter."*

### GROUP — Left-Wing Extremism and Integrated Response (owner 03: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-051 — Naxalbari origin and the term "Naxalism"

**Front:** Where and when did Naxalism begin, and who led it?

**Definition (learn):** The term derives from Naxalbari in Darjeeling district, West Bengal, where a peasant armed uprising began in 1967 under Charu Majumdar and Kanu Sanyal.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 says the movement began in May 1967 (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 51). ⚠️ Conflict: Wikipedia's Naxalbari uprising article gives 1967 with the first specific date as 3 March 1967 (armed peasants seized paddy); Kharibari and Phansidewa are named as the areas where committees gained foothold (`✅ Wikipedia: Naxalbari uprising`). Use "1967" in the answer; do not state May as verified.
- Charu Majumdar as "leader and ideologue"; Kanu Sanyal listed as a commander/leader (`✅ Wikipedia: Naxalbari uprising`).
- Owner 03 lists Naxalbari, Khoribari and Phansidewa police-station areas (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1967, Darjeeling district, West Bengal.

**Links:** IS-052 (LWE definition), IS-053 (CPI Maoist), IS-054 (Maoist doctrine).

**Trap:** Dating Naxalbari to the wrong month, or treating Naxalism as the same thing as LWE. Naxalism is the origin term; LWE is the broader current umbrella.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Trace the origin of Naxalism and explain why LWE is now the preferred term." Qualified conclusion: the movement's origin is a peasant-land grievance, but its current form is an organised armed insurgency; the label has moved, the grievance has not fully.

**Back:** *"Nineteen sixty-seven, Naxalbari bloom: the peasant spark that became the Red corridor's name."*

### Flashcard IS-052 — LWE: definition and the "people's government" aim

**Front:** Define Left-Wing Extremism as used in the owner.

**Definition (learn):** The broader, current umbrella term for Naxalism or Maoism-inspired violence aimed at capturing political power by force to install a "people's government" (`✅ owner 03`, PDF pp. 51, 56).

**Breakdown:**
- Maoists capture state power through armed insurgency, mass mobilisation and strategic alliances; Mao's "Protracted People's War" (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 52).
- "Bearing of arms is non-negotiable" (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 52).
- Naxalites do not seek secession; they aim to capture political power (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period). MHA's 2015 National Policy and Action Plan (IS-058).

**Links:** IS-051, IS-053, IS-054, IS-022 (terror vs insurgency vs Naxalism).

**Trap:** Describing LWE as a secessionist movement. Owner 03 says it differs from J&K and North-East militancy on exactly this point.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Explain LWE and how it affects people." Qualified conclusion: LWE is a challenge to state power, not a border question; so the response is governance-led as much as force-led.

**Back:** *"Power, not a province: LWE wants the state's seat, not a border."*

### Flashcard IS-053 — CPI (Maoist): formation and status

**Front:** When and how was CPI (Maoist) formed, and what is its legal status?

**Definition (learn):** The principal LWE outfit, formed in 2004 by merger of the People's War Group and the Maoist Communist Centre of India.

**Breakdown:**
- Formed 21 September 2004; merger of CPI (ML) People's War and MCCI; public announcement 14 October 2004 (`✅ Wikipedia: CPI (Maoist)`).
- Formed 2004 (`✅ owner 03`, PDF pp. 53-54).
- Banned as a terrorist organisation under UAPA; Wikipedia gives the ban date as 22 June 2009 (`✅ Wikipedia: CPI (Maoist)`). Owner says it is in the First Schedule of UAPA (`✅ owner 03`). ⚠️ First Schedule not confirmed by fetch.
- Owner's "People's War Group (Andhra Pradesh) and MCCI (Bihar)" details: ⚠️ state locations from owner, not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2004 formation; 2009 UAPA ban.

**Links:** IS-029 (UAPA), IS-051, IS-052, IS-034 (NIA Act).

**Trap:** Thinking CPI (Maoist) is listed only because of recent operations. The ban dates back to 2009 per Wikipedia.

**Mains hook:** Use in legal-measures answers: a UAPA-listed organisation gives UAPA and NIA hooks. Qualified conclusion: the listing is a legal tool; its effect depends on investigation and prosecution capacity.

**Back:** *"Oh-four merged, oh-nine banned: the People's War and the Centre joined, and the law followed."*

### Flashcard IS-054 — Maoist five-phase spread strategy

**Front:** Name the five phases of the Maoist area-expansion method.

**Definition (learn):** Preparatory, perspective, guerrilla, base and liberated phases, the movement's own systematic method of area expansion.

**Breakdown:**
- Preparatory → Perspective → Guerrilla → Base → Liberated (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 52).
- Parallel taxation and parallel governance are part of the base-area logic (`✅ owner 03` Section 4 and advanced Section 3).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-052, IS-056 (instrumental underdevelopment), IS-059 (clear-hold-develop).

**Trap:** Treating the five phases as a current map of territory. They describe method, not present status.

**Mains hook:** Use to explain why holding matters: the movement's base phase depends on administrative absence. Qualified conclusion: five-phase logic explains why the holding stage is the decisive counter-measure.

**Back:** *"Prepare, perspective, guerrilla, base, liberate: P-P-G-B-L, a ladder of territory."*

### Flashcard IS-055 — Root causes of LWE: land, forest and governance

**Front:** List the root causes that Singh groups under LWE.

**Definition (learn):** Land and forest grievances, extortion economy, governance deficit and social alienation as causal conditions for LWE.

**Breakdown:**
- Evasion of ceiling laws; encroachment on government and community lands; non-regularisation of traditional land rights; land acquisition without compensation; disruption of the tribal jal-jangal-zameen relationship; unemployment; poverty; infrastructure deficit; governance deficit; social exclusion and alienation (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 59).
- LWE is most intense in mineral-rich areas (coal, iron, bauxite, manganese, nickel, copper), providing scope for extortion from government and corporate projects (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 58).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-012 (root causes), IS-013 (conditional thesis), IS-056, IS-058 (FRA/PESA).

**Trap:** Saying LWE is caused only by poverty. Owner 03 lists land, forest and governance root causes alongside poverty.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Determinants of LWE in eastern India." Qualified conclusion: land-rights failure creates the grievance; the mineral-belt economy creates the extortion; governance absence allows both to persist.

**Back:** *"Ceilings, lands, rights, jal-jangal-zameen, mines, and absent officials: six doors that open to the Red."*

### Flashcard IS-056 — Instrumental underdevelopment (Singh's development-obstruction critique)

**Front:** What does Singh say about the Maoists' interest in keeping poverty alive?

**Definition (learn):** The Maoists have a vested interest in keeping poverty alive because it enables them to expand territory, and they do not allow district administration to build roads or improve electricity and water.

**Breakdown:**
- Verbatim gist (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 59): "Maoists have vested interest in keeping poverty alive because it enables them to expand their territory. They don't allow district administration to do any development work like building roads and improving electricity and water supply."
- Underdevelopment is both a cause and a maintained condition (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 2).
