**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-013, IS-055, IS-057, IS-054.

**Trap:** Thinking development alone will end LWE because the movement claims to fight for the poor. Singh shows the movement obstructs development.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "LWE thrives on the persistence of underdevelopment it also helps sustain." Examine. Qualified conclusion: development must be paired with security and trust restoration, because development delivery itself is targeted.

**Back:** *"Roads blocked, schools closed, poverty kept: the rebel needs the poor to stay poor."*

### Flashcard IS-057 — LWE: civilian impact (four heads)

**Front:** How are people affected by LWE, and how should the answer structure this?

**Definition (learn):** Civilian impact falls under four heads: coercion and violence; blocked roads, schools and health access; land, forest and livelihood insecurity; and weakened panchayat and justice presence.

**Breakdown:**
- Denial of development and basic services; displacement and livelihood loss; extortion burden; exposure to both extremist and state-security coercion; disruption of local governance (`✅ owner 03` Section 10).
- Panchayats forced to resign (book-period illustration; `✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 55).
- Owner 03 Section 12 spine: four heads for the 10-mark answer.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis; book-period illustration.

**Links:** IS-052, IS-055, IS-059, IS-066 (2025 Q10).

**Trap:** Describing civilian impact only as "violence." The owner requires the development, land and governance heads too.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "How are people affected by LWE?" Qualified conclusion: the cost is both coercion and absence of state, so relief must restore both security and services.

**Back:** *"Coerced, cut off, dispossessed, unrepresented: four heads of the civilian cost."*

### Flashcard IS-058 — National Policy and Action Plan to address LWE (2015)

**Front:** What are the three prongs of MHA's National Policy and Action Plan to address LWE?

**Definition (learn):** MHA's standing counter-LWE policy frame (2015): a security response, a development response, and ensuring the rights and entitlements of local communities.

**Breakdown:**
- Three prongs (`✅ owner 03`, Section 4; 📰 owner 03 Section 4 current anchor).
- Owner notes the third prong is most often dropped by candidates (`✅ owner 03`).
- ⚠️ Current operative status and any successor framing not verified; owner says verify from the MHA Annual Report.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2015 MHA policy.

**Links:** IS-059, IS-060 (FRA), IS-061 (PESA), IS-066.

**Trap:** Presenting the 2015 plan as the latest policy without checking. Also dropping the rights prong.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "The security-development-rights triad in LWE." Qualified conclusion: the rights prong is a security instrument in this theatre, because FRA and PESA failures produce grievance.

**Back:** *"Secure, develop, entitle: the third prong is the one that gets dropped."*

### Flashcard IS-059 — Clear, hold, develop

**Front:** Why does the "hold" stage decide the outcome in LWE?

**Definition (learn):** Security forces clear an area; permanent administrative and police presence holds it; schemes and rights delivery develop it. Most reversals occurred at the holding stage when forces moved on before civil administration arrived.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 Section 3 point 6 (`✅ owner 03`; ⚠️ owner analytical inference).
- Indicator: districts with a functioning police station, panchayat and service-delivery presence, rather than the number of districts cleared (`✅ owner 03`).
- Owner advanced: clearing is a force capability; holding is administrative presence; developing is a delivery capability (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 2).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-054, IS-057, IS-062 (fortified police stations), IS-058.

**Trap:** Counting cleared districts as evidence of resolution.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Security operations can clear, but only administration holds." Qualified conclusion: durability depends on the hold stage; the indicator must measure presence, not operations.

**Back:** *"Clear the road, hold the ground, develop the trust: the middle word decides the war."*

### Flashcard IS-060 — Forest Rights Act, 2006 (FRA)

**Front:** What is the Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006, and why does it matter for LWE?

**Definition (learn):** The statute recognising rights of forest-dwelling communities, cited by Singh as part of LWE's development strategy through coordination and implementation in affected States.

**Breakdown:**
- Cited as a rights-based instrument (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 63).
- Implementation quality, title pace and community forest rights are the practical gap (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 4).
- An enactment is not proof of title recognition or trusted delivery (`✅ owner 03` Section 12 bank).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** FRA 2006 (`✅ owner 03`; ⚠️ year not fetched).

**Links:** IS-058, IS-061 (PESA), IS-055, IS-065 (FRA implementation gap).

**Trap:** Treating FRA as resolving the land grievance once enacted. Owner 03 lists this as a key misconception.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "FRA on paper does not equal forest rights in practice." Qualified conclusion: title distribution and trust are the practical test.

**Back:** *"Two-thousand-six promised the forest; the title is the only proof it arrived."*

### Flashcard IS-061 — PESA, 1996 (Gram Sabha consultation in Fifth Schedule areas)

**Front:** What does the Panchayats (Extension to the Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996 provide?

**Definition (learn):** PESA extends panchayat provisions to Fifth Schedule areas and vests Gram Sabhas with consultative rights, including over land acquisition and minor minerals.

**Breakdown:**
- Gram Sabha consultation before land acquisition and over minor minerals (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 4; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Fifth Schedule framework is the constitutional core of the governance deficit cause (`✅ owner 03`).
- Owner 03 Advanced: PESA supplies the procedure by which communities can assert FRA rights (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PESA, 1996.

**Links:** IS-060, IS-058, IS-055.

**Trap:** Naming FRA without PESA. Owner 03 calls that naming the entitlement without the procedure.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on tribal rights and mineral extraction: PESA's Gram Sabha consent is the procedure. Qualified conclusion: procedural rights are only as good as their enforcement and the State's willingness to apply them.

**Back:** *"Nineteen ninety-six, the Gram Sabha speaks first: PESA is the procedure behind the promise."*

### Flashcard IS-062 — Security Related Expenditure scheme and LWE scheme cluster

**Front:** Name the central scheme cluster for LWE-affected States and the caution on figures.

**Definition (learn):** A cluster of central schemes for LWE-affected States, including Security Related Expenditure (SRE), Special Infrastructure Scheme (SIS), Special Central Assistance (SCA) for most affected districts, the Road Requirement Plan and its successor, the LWE Mobile Tower Project, Civic Action Programme (CAP) and Fortified Police Stations.

**Breakdown:**
- Book-period subset (`✅ owner 03`, PDF pp. 60-61).
- Eligibility depends on the district list; de-listing changes resource position as well (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 2).
- ⚠️ Current scheme names, coverage and allocations not verified; owner says verify before citing any figure.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Book-period scheme list; current MHA source needed.

**Links:** IS-059, IS-063 (district classification), IS-064 (CRPF units).

**Trap:** Quoting an allocation or a scheme name as current without a dated source.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Schemes are not outcomes." Qualified conclusion: scheme outlay is an input; the output test is holding presence and trusted delivery.

**Back:** *"SRE, SIS, SCA, roads, towers, CAP, fortified stations: a scheme cluster, verify every rupee."*

### Flashcard IS-063 — LWE-affected district lists: classification is not completion

**Front:** Why is de-listing a district not proof that root causes are resolved?

**Definition (learn):** The LWE-affected and most-affected lists are administrative classifications that drive scheme eligibility and force deployment; de-listing records a security-metric change, not completed land, forest or service-delivery reform.

**Breakdown:**
- Keep three claims separate: classification, security outcome and development outcome (`✅ owner 03` Section 7).
- Owner 03 Advanced: argument for tapering development support in newly de-listed districts (`✅ owner 03`, ⚠️ owner analysis).
- Owner 03 Section 8: 2024 MHA baseline of 73% violence decline, 86% deaths, 38-district formulation (`✅ owner 03`); do not re-date it.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** MHA 2024 baseline (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-059, IS-062, IS-065.

**Trap:** Equating de-listing with success. Also re-dating the 2024 baseline to 2026.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — "The 2026 elimination target." Qualified conclusion: announced target, reported metric and resolved grievance are three different evidentiary levels.

**Back:** *"Off the list is not out of trouble: classification, security and development are three different claims."*

### Flashcard IS-064 — CRPF CoBRA battalions, Bastariya Battalion and Greyhounds model

**Front:** Name the specialised anti-LWE units and the design precedent that Singh cites.

**Definition (learn):** Operation Greyhound (Andhra Pradesh) is a specialised, trained state police force credited with curbing Naxalism in undivided Andhra Pradesh. Later analogues include CRPF's CoBRA (jungle warfare) battalions and locally recruited units such as the Bastariya Battalion.

**Breakdown:**
- Greyhounds: "a professional way to handle the naxal menace" (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 64).
- Benefit partly lost when Naxalites moved to adjoining States lacking the force (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 4).
- CoBRA and Bastariya Battalion: ⚠️ owner-stated; not verified in this build.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Greyhounds (Andhra Pradesh); CoBRA (CRPF); Bastariya Battalion (⚠️ year not verified).

**Links:** IS-066 (2025 Q10), IS-065 (trijunction), IS-062.

**Trap:** Saying "central forces" generically. Owner 03 says name the specific unit.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Specialised forces versus general police in LWE." Qualified conclusion: a specialised force works when neighbouring States can replicate it; otherwise the threat relocates.

**Back:** *"Greyhounds chased, the Maoist ran next door: a dog is good, a region needs a pack."*

### Flashcard IS-065 — Operation Greyhound limitation and the inter-State trijunction

**Front:** Why does the Dandakaranya trijunction matter for LWE coordination?

**Definition (learn):** The Dandakaranya region spans Chhattisgarh, Odisha, Maharashtra and Andhra Pradesh. Poor coordination among State police at inter-State trijunctions allows pressure in one State to be relieved by movement into another.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh names the trijunction problem (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 4; PDF pp. 61-62).
- Administrative hurdles: poor infrastructure and manpower; absence of routine administration enabling parallel taxation; inter-State boundary exploitation; lack of professionalism between central and State forces; differing surrender and talks policies across States (`✅ owner 03`, PDF pp. 61-62).
- Four States are named in the owner's Advanced Prelims item (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-064, IS-067, IS-006 (Centre-State coordination).

**Trap:** Listing the trijunction without the four State names, or treating one-State success as structural resolution.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "A single-State success does not resolve a multi-State insurgency." Qualified conclusion: inter-State coordination is a standing constraint; a district count cannot measure it.

**Back:** *"Pressed in Andhra, flown to Odisha: the Maoist uses the border's seam."*

### Flashcard IS-066 — 2025 Q10 and 2020 GS-III LWE PYQ routes

**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2025 Q10 LWE question and the 2020 determinants question?

**Definition (learn):** 2025 Q10 (10 marks): define LWE and its impact; list measures; assess the 2026 elimination statement. 2020 GS-III (15 marks): determinants of LWE in eastern India and differentiated strategy for government, civil administration and security forces.

**Breakdown:**
- 2025 Q10 verbatim: "The Government of India recently stated that Left Wing Extremism (LWE) will be eliminated by 2026. What do you understand by LWE and how are the people affected by it? What measures have been taken by the government to eliminate LWE?" (`✅ owner 03`; local paper text).
- Structure: definition, civilian impact, three-part strategy, current anchor, clear-hold-develop (`✅ owner 03` Section 9).
- 2020: organise causes first, then allocate roles to political executive, civil administration and security forces (`✅ owner 03` Section 9).
- 2018 and 2022 routes also owned by this topic (`✅ owner 03`, PYQ integration 2018-2023).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQs 2018, 2020, 2022, 2025.

**Links:** IS-052, IS-057, IS-058, IS-059, IS-067.

**Trap:** Repeating the government's "eliminated by 2026" claim as fact.

**Mains hook:** Use the three-track spine and the qualified status ladder. Qualified conclusion: the government's objective is stated; the outcome is reported and must be graded.

**Back:** *"Define, affect, measure, assess: the 2026 claim is a promise to grade, not a fact to repeat."*

### Flashcard IS-067 — LWE current-status ladder (December 2025 reply and June 2026 PIB)

**Front:** What is the current-status ladder for LWE figures, and how should the answer grade them?

**Definition (learn):** A ladder of evidentiary levels: the 2024 MHA baseline; the December 2025 MHA parliamentary reply; and PIB's June 2026 "Naxal-Free India" explainer.

**Breakdown:**
- MHA December 2025 reply: 89% decline in LWE violence, 91% decline in deaths since 2010, 218 incidents in 2025 (`✅ owner 03`; 📰 owner-stated; not fetched).
- PIB June 2026: "effectively free from Left-Wing Extremism" after 31 March 2026; not literal zero incidents (`✅ owner 03`; 📰 owner-stated; not fetched).
- 2024 baseline: 73% violence decline, 86% deaths, 38-district formulation (`✅ owner 03`; not to be re-dated).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** December 2025; June 2026 (owner-stated; ⚠️ not fetched because MHA and PIB pages were not reachable in this build).

**Links:** IS-063, IS-066, IS-068 (deadline gap).

**Trap:** Converting "effectively free" into "zero incidents," or re-dating the 2024 baseline to 2026.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Assess the claim that LWE will be eliminated by 2026." Qualified conclusion: the official qualified wording is the best honest summary; root-cause resolution is a separate test.

**Back:** *"Eighty-nine, ninety-one, two-eighteen; then 'effectively free': read the qualifier, not the headline."*

### Flashcard IS-068 — Deadline-achievement gap and violence decline vs root-cause resolution

**Front:** Why must a violence decline and a root-cause resolution be evaluated separately?

**Definition (learn):** A falling violence metric shows security change in the reporting period; root-cause resolution requires land, forest, governance and rights outcomes.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 Advanced Section 5: violence decline does not confirm grievance resolution (`✅ owner 03`).
- Owner 00 Section 8: deadline-achievement gap; do not convert the target into a literal zero (`✅ owner 00`).
- Reversibility: security gains reverse unless development and rights keep pace (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 1).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-067, IS-059, IS-060.

**Trap:** Stating that a decline by a stated date confirms root causes resolved.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "A decline in LWE-related violence is not the same as the resolution of its root causes." Critically examine. Qualified conclusion: decline is necessary, not sufficient; durability needs the hold and rights stages.

**Back:** *"Fewer shots are not fewer grievances: the gun falls faster than the land is restored."*

### Flashcard IS-069 — Surrender versus rehabilitation

**Front:** Distinguish surrender from rehabilitation in LWE policy.

**Definition (learn):** Surrender is a one-time act with an immediate incentive package. Rehabilitation is a multi-year process of skilling, livelihood, protection from reprisal and social reintegration.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 basic trap (`✅ owner 03`, Section 7).
- State surrender policies vary widely; Maoists have exploited differences (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 5; PDF p. 62).
- A surrender count is a countable event; rehabilitation outcomes are rarely measured (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 5).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-065, IS-064, IS-067.

**Trap:** Reporting surrenders as evidence of rehabilitation success.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Surrender is not rehabilitation." Qualified conclusion: rehabilitation requires protection and livelihood; without them, surrender is reversible.

**Back:** *"Surrender is the door, rehabilitation is the house: count the doors, then visit the houses."*

### Flashcard IS-070 — Psychological operations as a third strategic pillar

**Front:** What is the third pillar in Singh's LWE strategy?

**Definition (learn):** Psychological operations, separate from security and development: delegitimise the movement, manage media and public perception, and have administration engage civil society, NGOs and the public to restore trust.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh's three-part strategy: development (infrastructure, land rights, FRA), security (professional dominance, State-police primacy, specialised forces), psychological operations (PDF pp. 63-64) (`✅ owner 03`).
- Owner 03 Advanced: trust restoration as its own track (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 64).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-058, IS-059, IS-069.

**Trap:** Treating trust as a by-product of security or development.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Trust restoration is a security instrument." Qualified conclusion: without trust, security action reproduces alienation that fuels recruitment.

**Back:** *"Win the mind after the ground: the third pillar is the trust that outlasts the operation."*

### Flashcard IS-071 — Security-first vs development-first sequencing

**Front:** How should security and development be sequenced in LWE areas?

**Definition (learn):** Singh's text implies security should precede development, while also warning that security without root-cause work will fail. The two claims must be reconciled as sequenced, not as either-or.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh: "security needs to be established before development can come" (`✅ owner 03` Advanced, PDF p. 63).
- Doctrine does not resolve sequencing; area-specific judgement needed (`✅ owner 01` Advanced Section 6).
- Security action must be rights-bound to avoid alienation (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-059, IS-070, IS-013, IS-004.

**Trap:** Thinking the doctrine prescribes an order. It does not; the owner says sequencing is area-specific.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Security before development in LWE areas." Discuss. Qualified conclusion: sequencing is conditional; the hold stage must start early.

**Back:** *"Secure first, but not alone: the sequence bends to the district, never to the slogan."*

### Flashcard IS-072 — Naxalism is not merely a law-and-order issue

**Front:** What does Singh say about LWE and law and order?

**Definition (learn):** Singh states that Naxalism is "not merely a law and order issue... it is directly related to under-development" (`✅ owner 03`, PDF p. 62).

**Breakdown:**
- Governance and development failures are root causes, not side issues (`✅ owner 03` Section 7).
- The trap: LWE is purely law and order (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-013, IS-055, IS-056.

**Trap:** Treating LWE as purely a law-and-order problem.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on the development-security debate. Qualified conclusion: law and order is necessary but not sufficient.

**Back:** *"Not just order, not just roads: LWE is under-development's shadow."*

### Flashcard IS-073 — Prelims facts: Naxalism origin and LWE statutory hooks

**Front:** Which statutory and historical facts about LWE are likely Prelims targets?

**Definition (learn):** 1967 origin in Naxalbari; CPI (Maoist) formed 2004; UAPA listing; no secession aim; "Political power grows out of the barrel of a gun" slogan.

**Breakdown:**
- Naxalbari, 1967 (`✅ owner 03`; ✅ Wikipedia: Naxalbari uprising).
- CPI (Maoist) 2004; UAPA ban 2009 (`✅ Wikipedia: CPI (Maoist)`).
- Slogan and "Protracted People's War" (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1967; 2004; 2009.

**Links:** IS-051, IS-053, IS-052, IS-054.

**Trap:** Dating UAPA listing to 2004 or to the formation year.

**Mains hook:** Use in LWE introduction, not as a full answer. Qualified conclusion: the legal listing and the origin date are background, not the answer.

**Back:** *"Sixty-seven, oh-four, oh-nine: the origin, the merger, the ban."*

### Flashcard IS-074 — Naxalbari, Dandakaranya and the Red Corridor (classification caution)

**Front:** What is the "Red Corridor" and why is its extent not to be assumed?

**Definition (learn):** A book-period term for the contiguous mineral-rich belt (parts of Jharkhand, Chhattisgarh, Odisha and adjoining States) most affected by LWE violence.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 defines it as book-period; current extent must be verified from MHA (`✅ owner 03`, Section 2).
- Dandakaranya trijunction (four States) is the structural case (IS-065).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Book-period term.

**Links:** IS-065, IS-063, IS-067.

**Trap:** Assuming a fixed red-corridor map from the book.

**Mains hook:** Use in geography-linked answers only with a dated official district list.

**Back:** *"A corridor is a map of a moment: verify the red before you paint the corridor."*

### Flashcard IS-075 — LWE advanced concept: security metrics vs root causes (Mains thesis)

**Front:** What is the advanced thesis for LWE?

**Definition (learn):** LWE's officially reported decline reflects convergence of security, development and trust tracks in the most-affected districts, but the movement's instrumental interest in underdevelopment and inter-State exploitation makes security gains reversible unless root causes move too.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 03 Advanced Section 1 and 11 (`✅ owner 03`).
- Three-track model: security (clear and hold), development, psychological (`✅ owner 03` Advanced Section 3).
- Advanced Prelims trap: Greyhounds alone did not end Naxalism (`✅ owner 03`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-068, IS-064, IS-065, IS-070.

**Trap:** Treating single-State success as structural.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — "Evaluate LWE's elimination by 2026." Qualified conclusion: the convergence test, not the target date, decides durability.

**Back:** *"Three tracks, one trust: converge or reverse."*

### GROUP — North-East Insurgency and Peace Processes (owner 04: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-076 — Historical isolation of the North-East (British exclusion policy)

**Front:** How did historical isolation shape North-East identity and later insurgency?

**Definition (learn):** Under British policy, the tribal areas were excluded from outside contact while Christian missionary access was permitted; the tribal population had little contact with India's freedom struggle.

**Breakdown:**
- Virtual absence of political, cultural, social, geographical, religious or business contact with the rest of India; "India's freedom struggle had very little impact on the tribals" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF pp. 31-32).
- Foundational condition for identity-based insurgency (`✅ owner 04`).
- Missionary influence noted (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 31).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** British colonial period (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-077, IS-078, IS-103 (identity vs resource-driven).

**Trap:** Treating the North-East as a homogeneous bloc. Historical isolation differed by community.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Explain how historical isolation contributed to identity-based insurgency." Qualified conclusion: isolation created the identity gap; the later driver was the state's uneven integration, which the answer must evidence separately.

**Back:** *"Walled off by policy, missionary-lit: the isolated hills kept their identity and lost their contact."*

### Flashcard IS-077 — 1960 Assamese official-language decision and "Assamisation" reaction

**Front:** What was the 1960 language decision and how did the tribal districts react?

**Definition (learn):** The 1960 decision to make Assamese the sole official language triggered strong reaction in tribal districts, fearing loss of identity through "Assamization."

**Breakdown:**
- "Immediate and strong reaction in the tribal districts" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 32).
- Led to statehood demands; the All Party Hill Leaders Conference (1960) demanded separate statehood (`✅ owner 04`, PDF pp. 32-33).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1960 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-076, IS-079 (statehood), IS-078.

**Trap:** Describing the reaction as a purely Naga or purely Mizo event. The owner records it in tribal districts generally.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on language and identity in the North-East. Qualified conclusion: language policy can convert cultural anxiety into political demand when no accommodation exists.

**Back:** *"Nineteen-sixty, one language: the hills heard 'Assamese only' as 'Assam only.'"*

### Flashcard IS-078 — Sixth Schedule of the Constitution

**Front:** Which areas does the Sixth Schedule cover, under which articles, and through which bodies?

**Definition (learn):** A constitutional schedule providing self-rule and autonomy for tribal areas through Autonomous District and Regional Councils with law-making, judicial and revenue powers.

**Breakdown:**
- Articles 244(2) and 275(1) (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 32; ⚠️ article numbers not confirmed by fetch).
- States: Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram (`✅ Wikipedia: Sixth Schedule`).
- Autonomous District Councils listed on Wikipedia: Bodoland Territorial Council, North Cachar Hills, Karbi Anglong (Assam); Garo Hills, Jaintia Hills, Khasi Hills (Meghalaya); Chakma, Lai, Mara (Mizoram); Tripura Tribal Areas (Tripura) (`✅ Wikipedia: Sixth Schedule`).
- "Self-rule, autonomy and decentralization" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 32).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Sixth Schedule (1950 Constitution); owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-079, IS-080 (Article 371A and 371C), IS-086, IS-091.

**Trap:** Saying the Sixth Schedule covers the whole North-East. It covers four States' tribal areas; Nagaland and Manipur run through 371A and 371C. Also: Regional Councils are not in the fetched Wikipedia summary.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Autonomy instruments are not interchangeable." Qualified conclusion: the Sixth Schedule is structural accommodation; it can create boundary frictions between groups.

**Back:** *"Four States, ten councils: the Sixth gives the hills their own law, not a separate flag."*

### Flashcard IS-079 — Statehood accommodation timeline (Nagaland to Mizoram and Arunachal)

**Front:** Give the dates on which the North-Eastern States were created as accommodation.

**Definition (learn):** The post-Independence fragmentation of Assam into separate States, used as a primary accommodation tool.

**Breakdown:**
- Nagaland 1963; Meghalaya, Manipur, Tripura 1972; Mizoram 1987; Arunachal Pradesh (formerly NEFA) 1987 (`✅ owner 04`, PDF pp. 32-33).
- Owner 04 Section 3: statehood as "a historical accommodation pattern, not a current negotiation."
- ⚠️ Dates not fetched; from owner, consistent with general knowledge.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1963, 1972, 1987.

**Links:** IS-077, IS-078, IS-093 (DoNER).

**Trap:** Dating Mizoram's statehood to 1972 or Meghalaya's to 1963. Meghalaya was 1972 (with Manipur and Tripura).

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Statehood as a peace instrument." Qualified conclusion: statehood addressed group-level demands but generated successor demands for other communities.

**Back:** *"Sixty-three Nagaland, seventy-two three more, eighty-seven for Mizoram and Arunachal: the hills got states one step at a time."*

### Flashcard IS-080 — Article 371A (Nagaland) and Article 371C (Manipur hill areas)

**Front:** Which constitutional instrument protects Nagaland and Manipur's hill areas?

**Definition (learn):** Nagaland's protections run through Article 371A (customary law, land and resources; Assembly concurrence for named central laws). Manipur's hill areas are protected under Article 371C.

**Breakdown:**
- Article 371A for Nagaland (`✅ owner 04`, Advanced Section 2).
- Article 371C for Manipur hill areas (`✅ owner 04`).
- Different instrument from the Sixth Schedule (`✅ owner 04`, trap).
- ⚠️ Article text not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Articles 371A and 371C.

**Links:** IS-078, IS-086, IS-101 (Manipur 2023).

**Trap:** Saying Nagaland has Sixth Schedule status. Article 371A is its instrument.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Sixth Schedule status" demands are demands for a different instrument. Qualified conclusion: accords promising autonomy must specify the instrument they intend.

**Back:** *"Three-seven-one-A for Naga custom, three-seven-one-C for Manipur's hills: the Sixth is not the only key."*

### Flashcard IS-081 — AFSPA, 1958: Sections 3, 4 and 6

**Front:** What do Sections 3, 4 and 6 of the Armed Forces (Special Powers) Act, 1958 provide?

**Definition (learn):** The Act lets the Governor or the Centre declare an area "disturbed" (Section 3); confers use-of-force, arrest and search powers on officers in such areas (Section 4); and bars prosecution of those acting under it without prior Central Government sanction (Section 6).

**Breakdown:**
- Enacted 1958; Act No. 28 of 1958; 11 September 1958 (`✅ Wikipedia: AFSPA`).
- Section 3: declaration by State Governor or Union Territory Administrator; Act 7 of 1972 extended it to the Central Government (`✅ Wikipedia: AFSPA`).
- Section 4 powers: firing after due warning, destroying arms dumps and fortified hideouts, arrest without warrant, entry and search, stopping and searching vehicles (`✅ Wikipedia: AFSPA`).
- Section 6: prosecution only with "prior sanction of the Central Government" (`✅ Wikipedia: AFSPA`, quoting Hegde commission).
- J&K has a separate statute: Armed Forces (Jammu and Kashmir) Special Powers Act, 1990 (`✅ owner 04`; ⚠️ not fetched).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** AFSPA 1958.

**Links:** IS-082, IS-083, IS-084, IS-086.

**Trap:** Saying the Centre alone declares disturbed areas, or that Section 6 gives absolute immunity (see IS-082). Section 3 power also rests with Governors.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — AFSPA's operational necessity vs accountability. Qualified conclusion: the sanction filter is where accountability and operational protection meet; a reformed filter might serve both.

**Back:** *"Three declares, four empowers, six protects: the sanction gate is the one door to prosecution."*

### Flashcard IS-082 — AFSPA judicial frame: Naga People's Movement (1997) and EEVFAM (2016)

**Front:** What did the Supreme Court hold in the two AFSPA judgments?

**Definition (learn):** In the Naga People's Movement of Human Rights case (1997), the Court upheld AFSPA while making the Army's "Do's and Don'ts" binding and requiring proportionality. In EEVFAM (2016), the Court held that there is no absolute immunity and allegations of excessive force are investigable.

**Breakdown:**
- *Naga People's Movement of Human Rights v. Union of India* (1997): upheld constitutionality; "Do's and Don'ts" binding (`✅ owner 04`). ⚠️ Not verified in the fetched Wikipedia AFSPA page.
- *Extra Judicial Execution Victim Families Association (EEVFAM) v. Union of India* (2016): no absolute immunity; investigation open; ruling dated 8 July 2016 (`✅ Wikipedia: AFSPA`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1997; 8 July 2016.

**Links:** IS-081, IS-083, IS-084.

**Trap:** Saying AFSPA was struck down in 2016. It was held to give no absolute immunity; the Act stands.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "The judiciary has affirmed both necessity and accountability." Qualified conclusion: judicial affirmation of both does not resolve the practical tension; the sanction filter is the bottleneck.

**Back:** *"Ninety-seven upheld the Act, twenty-sixteen denied the immunity: the law stands, the shield does not."*

### Flashcard IS-083 — AFSPA: operational necessity vs accountability (Singh's two-sided position)

**Front:** How does Singh frame the AFSPA debate?

**Definition (learn):** Singh argues that a deployment without search and seizure powers is meaningless, and concludes that AFSPA's removal is not advisable now, while stressing that forces must be accountable for human rights violations.

**Breakdown:**
- "If the armed forces are deployed without the power of search and seizure, then their deployment is meaningless" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 47).
- "The removal of AFSPA now is not advisable" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 47).
- "Must also be wary of human rights violations and must be accountable for their actions and behaviour towards the public" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 47).
- Irom Sharmila's decade-long hunger strike cited as evidence of depth of grievance (`✅ owner 04`, Advanced Section 5).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-081, IS-082, IS-084.

**Trap:** Presenting AFSPA as settled one-sidedly. Owner 04 insists on two-sided argument.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "AFSPA: necessity or excess?" Qualified conclusion: operational necessity and accountability are not opposed; their balance depends on investigation and remedy.

**Back:** *"Search is the power, accountability is the price: Singh holds both in one breath."*

### Flashcard IS-084 — Disturbed-area notification vs AFSPA repeal (2026 notifications)

**Front:** What is the difference between a disturbed-area notification and AFSPA's repeal, and what do the 2026 notifications cover?

**Definition (learn):** AFSPA applies only where a disturbed-area notification is in force. Withdrawing or reducing that notification is an executive act that ends operation in those districts without repealing the Act.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 04 Current anchor: MHA 2026 notifications effective 1 April to 30 September 2026 continued the disturbed-area notification in nine Nagaland districts plus specified police-station areas, and in Tirap, Changlang and Longding districts plus specified Namsai police-station areas in Arunachal Pradesh (`✅ owner 04`). ⚠️ Not fetched; as-of date from owner only.
- Manipur notification must be read by its exact police-station exclusions (`✅ owner 04`).
- Reduction in footprint is never equal to repeal (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1 April to 30 September 2026 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-081, IS-083, IS-086.

**Trap:** Equating a reduced footprint with repeal, or citing the 2026 notification as permanent.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks on AFSPA implementation. Qualified conclusion: footprint reduction is an executive notification outcome, not a final peace verdict.

**Back:** *"Notify, reduce, reinstate: the Act stays on the book while the map shrinks."*

### Flashcard IS-085 — Suspension of Operations (SoO): ceasefire with ground rules

**Front:** What is a Suspension of Operations agreement and what does it not do?

**Definition (learn):** A cessation-of-hostilities arrangement with ground rules (designated camps, arms in monitored storage, no movement or extortion), renewable and revocable; it is neither disarmament nor a political settlement.

**Breakdown:**
- Government has signed SoO agreements with most North-East groups, historically "numbering 17-18" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 44; book-period count).
- Its value depends on monitoring capacity (`✅ owner 04` Advanced Section 4).
- Rung 1 of the instrument ladder (IS-086).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period); owner 04.

**Links:** IS-086, IS-090, IS-091.

**Trap:** SoO equals disarmament. Owner 04 lists it as a top trap.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on peace accords. Qualified conclusion: SoO manages violence; it leaves the political demand open.

**Back:** *"Camp, arms stored, no levy: a truce with rules, not a treaty."*

### Flashcard IS-086 — Peace-process instrument ladder (SoO, framework, settlement, implementation)

**Front:** What are the four rungs of the North-East peace process and why does the rung matter?

**Definition (learn):** Rung 1: SoO or ceasefire. Rung 2: framework agreement recording principles. Rung 3: Memorandum of Settlement or accord with specified commitments. Rung 4: implementation (council elections, Sixth Schedule amendment, disbursed package, cadre reintegration).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 04 Section 4A table (`✅ owner 04`).
- Same State can sit on different rungs at once (Nagaland framework since 2015; Assam multiple settlements; Manipur rung-1 SoO) (`✅ owner 04` Advanced Section 2).
- Implementation is the rung most criticised (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-085, IS-087, IS-088, IS-089, IS-090, IS-091.

**Trap:** Counting signatures as settlement. "A signed accord means the conflict is resolved" is a named trap.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Signing an accord and settling a conflict are different achievements." Discuss. Qualified conclusion: the map must show the rung for each entry; implementation is the test.

**Back:** *"Truce, framework, settlement, delivery: four rungs, and the top one is the one that is hard to climb."*

### Flashcard IS-087 — NSCN (IM) Framework Agreement, 3 August 2015

**Front:** What is the NSCN (IM) Framework Agreement, and what is its legal status?

**Definition (learn):** A framework agreement signed with NSCN (IM) on 3 August 2015 that records agreed principles for a future settlement, leaving competencies, territory and timelines open.

**Breakdown:**
- Signed 3 August 2015 (`✅ owner 04`, Section 6).
- Not a final settlement (`✅ owner 04`).
- Owner 04 Section 4A: framework is rung 2.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 3 August 2015.

**Links:** IS-086, IS-088, IS-091.

**Trap:** Calling it a final Naga settlement. Owner 04 names this trap.

**Mains hook:** Use in peace-process mapping. Qualified conclusion: the framework is an open negotiation, not a closed deal.

**Back:** *"Fifteen, August three: principles signed, details still in the room."*

### Flashcard IS-088 — Bodo Accord (27 January 2020)

**Front:** What is the Bodo Accord of 2020, and what does owner 04 record about it?

**Definition (learn):** A third Bodo Accord signed with NDFB factions and ABSU, a memorandum of settlement on the Bodo question in Assam.

**Breakdown:**
- Dated 27 January 2020 (`✅ owner 04`, Section 4A).
- Rung 3: memorandum of settlement (`✅ owner 04`).
- Implementation status must be verified from the MHA Annual Report 2024-25 (`✅ owner 04`).
- ⚠️ Not fetched; signatories as owner states.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 27 January 2020.

**Links:** IS-078 (BTC, Sixth Schedule), IS-086, IS-091.

**Trap:** Claiming implementation complete. Owner 04 says signature is not resolution.

**Mains hook:** Use as the example of a settlement whose outcome depends on implementation and boundary issues. Qualified conclusion: the accord's durability depends on the Bodoland council's operation and the non-Bodo population's trust.

**Back:** *"Twenty-twenty, January the twenty-seventh: signed, and the test begins."*

### Flashcard IS-089 — Karbi Anglong Agreement (4 September 2021)

**Front:** What is the Karbi Anglong Agreement, and what rung does it occupy?

**Definition (learn):** An agreement with Karbi armed groups in Assam dated 4 September 2021, a rung-3 settlement instrument.

**Breakdown:**
- Dated 4 September 2021 (`✅ owner 04`, Section 4A).
- Owner's classification: settlement, rung 3 (`✅ owner 04`).
- ⚠️ Not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 4 September 2021.

**Links:** IS-078, IS-086, IS-090.

**Trap:** Dating it to 2020 or using its date to infer implementation.

**Mains hook:** Use with the Bodo accord to show Assam's multi-settlement landscape. Qualified conclusion: each settlement needs its own implementation record.

**Back:** *"Twenty-one in September, Karbi's door: the agreement is dated, the delivery is still due."*

### Flashcard IS-090 — UPF and KNO tripartite SoO: revised ground rules (2025-2026)

**Front:** What is the status of the UPF and KNO tripartite SoO in Manipur?

**Definition (learn):** A rung-1 ceasefire arrangement with the United People's Front (UPF) and Kuki National Organisation (KNO), with revised ground rules signed 4 September 2025 and valid to 3 September 2026.

**Breakdown:**
- Revised ground rules signed 4 September 2025, valid until 3 September 2026 (`✅ owner 04` Section 8).
- Evidence that Manipur's process is at the ceasefire stage, not settlement (`✅ owner 04`).
- MHA April 2026 peace-process update is the owner's cited current source; ⚠️ not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 4 September 2025; 3 September 2026.

**Links:** IS-085, IS-086, IS-101 (Manipur 2023).

**Trap:** Presenting the SoO as a Manipur settlement. The owner classes it as rung 1.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on Manipur's peace process. Qualified conclusion: a rung-1 instrument cannot carry a settlement claim; its renewal status must be checked on the answer date.

**Back:** *"Two-five September rules, two-six September end: a truce on a calendar, not a peace."*

### Flashcard IS-091 — Accord map 2019-2024 (illustrative past-decade list)

**Front:** Which accords and agreements does owner 04 list for 2019-2024?

**Definition (learn):** An illustrative map of Government of India agreements in the North-East, each to be verified against the MHA Annual Report 2024-25 for date, signatories and implementation.

**Breakdown:**
- 2019: agreement with NLFT (SD), Tripura (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2020: Bru-Reang permanent-settlement agreement (Tripura); third Bodo Accord (IS-088) (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2022: Assam-Meghalaya inter-State boundary agreement (inter-State, not an insurgent accord); agreement with Adivasi and tea-garden-community armed groups (Assam) (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2023: DNLA (Assam); UNLF (Manipur, first with a Meitei armed group); pro-talks ULFA Memorandum of Settlement (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2024: tripartite agreement with TIPRA Motha (Tripura) (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2015 NSCN (IM) framework (IS-087); 2025 UPF/KNO SoO (IS-090).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2019-2024 (owner-stated; ⚠️ not fetched).

**Links:** IS-086, IS-087, IS-088, IS-089, IS-090.

**Trap:** Mixing an inter-State boundary agreement with an insurgent accord.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Map the peace accords of the past decade." Qualified conclusion: the map is a list of instruments with different rungs; the status column matters more than the count.

**Back:** *"Nineteen to twenty-four: nine instruments, many rungs; the count is easy, the status is the answer."*

### Flashcard IS-092 — Pre-decade accords: Shillong (1975), Assam (1985), Mizo (1986), BTC (2003)

**Front:** Name the pre-decade landmark accords and their significance.

**Definition (learn):** Shillong Accord (1975), Assam Accord (1985), Mizo Peace Accord (1986), Bodoland Territorial Council accord (2003), given as one line of context in owner 04.

**Breakdown:**
- Shillong Accord, 1975 (`✅ owner 04`, Section 4A).
- Assam Accord, 1985 (`✅ owner 04`).
- Mizo Peace Accord, 1986: "the standard success case, an insurgency converted into electoral politics" (`✅ owner 04`).
- Bodoland Territorial Council accord, 2003 (`✅ owner 04`).
- ⚠️ Not fetched; owner-stated.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1975, 1985, 1986, 2003.

**Links:** IS-086, IS-088, IS-091.

**Trap:** Presenting the Mizo accord as a template without its implementation record. Owner says implementation followed signature.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Mizoram as the success case." Qualified conclusion: the success followed implementation; the template is not automatic.

**Back:** *"Seventy-five, eighty-five, eighty-six, oh-three: four landmarks, and only one turned guns into votes."*

### Flashcard IS-093 — DoNER (Department of Development of North Eastern Region)

**Front:** When was DoNER created and when did it become a ministry?

**Definition (learn):** The development ministry for the North-East focused on regional infrastructure.

**Breakdown:**
- Created 2001; full ministry in 2004 (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 45).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2001; 2004.

**Links:** IS-094, IS-095, IS-079.

**Trap:** Dating DoNER to 2004 as creation.

**Mains hook:** Use in development-security answers. Qualified conclusion: infrastructure helps only where accords and administration deliver.

**Back:** *"Two-thousand-one the department, two-oh-oh-four the ministry: DoNER grew up in four years."*

### Flashcard IS-094 — North Eastern Council

**Front:** When was the North Eastern Council established and restructured?

**Definition (learn):** A regional planning body established by Act of Parliament in 1971 and restructured in 2002.

**Breakdown:**
- Established 1971 by Act of Parliament; restructured 2002 as the regional planning body (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 45).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1971; 2002.

**Links:** IS-093, IS-095.

**Trap:** Confusing it with DoNER. The Council is a planning body; DoNER is a ministry.

**Mains hook:** Use in federal-planning answers. Qualified conclusion: planning bodies coordinate; they do not settle political demands.

**Back:** *"Seventy-one planned, oh-two reshaped: the Council plans, the Ministry funds."*

### Flashcard IS-095 — North-East fiscal devolution mechanisms (book-period)

**Front:** Which fiscal mechanisms does Singh record for the North-East, and why should they be verified?

**Definition (learn):** The 10% Gross Budgetary Support earmarking, Special Category status and the Non-Lapsable Central Pool of Resources.

**Breakdown:**
- Named in owner 04 from Singh (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 45).
- ⚠️ Current arrangements not verified; owner says verify from a dated official source.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Book-period.

**Links:** IS-093, IS-094.

**Trap:** Quoting book-period fiscal percentages as current.

**Mains hook:** Use only as context in a federal-fiscal answer, with a dated source.

**Back:** *"Ten per cent, special status, a pool that does not lapse: check the rules before you count the money."*

### Flashcard IS-096 — Free Movement Regime (FMR) and the 8 February 2024 scrapping decision

**Front:** What is the Free Movement Regime and what did MHA announce on 8 February 2024?

**Definition (learn):** A provision allowing tribal communities to move up to 16 km across the India-Myanmar border without passport or visa, historically justified by shared ethnicity and trade. MHA announced its decision to scrap it on 8 February 2024.

**Breakdown:**
- 16 km; formalised within Act East policy in 2018 (`✅ owner 04`, VisionIAS PDF p. 24; ⚠️ formalisation year not fetched).
- Reasons: illegal immigration, Manipur 2023 ethnic tensions, cross-border insurgent misuse, narcotics surge (`✅ owner 04`).
- Announcement only; operational status requires a formal notification (`✅ owner 04`).
- ⚠️ Wikipedia FMR page returned 404.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 8 February 2024 (announcement).

**Links:** IS-101, IS-097, IS-098.

**Trap:** Treating the announcement as operational regime. Also: the FMR decision was internal-security driven, not purely IR.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "How internal identity conflict reshapes border policy." Qualified conclusion: the decision is a compound response; implementation must be verified.

**Back:** *"Sixteen kilometres, one announcement: the free walk is announced, not yet fenced."*

### Flashcard IS-097 — Assam Rifles' dual mandate on the Myanmar border

**Front:** What does VisionIAS record about Assam Rifles' battalion deployment and mandate?

**Definition (learn):** Assam Rifles has deployed more battalions to counter-insurgency than to border guarding on the Myanmar border, creating a "one force, one mandate" design question.

**Breakdown:**
- Manned the border since 2002 with help from the Indian Army (`✅ owner 04`, VisionIAS PDF p. 25).
- 31 of 46 battalions on counter-insurgency vs 15 on border guarding, per the source's figures (`✅ owner 04`; ⚠️ source figures, not verified against MHA).
- "Functioning more like a counter-insurgency force rather than a border guarding force" (`✅ owner 04`).
- Way ahead: single border-guarding mandate or another force such as the BSF (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2002 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-015 (one border one force), IS-096, IS-098.

**Trap:** Assuming Assam Rifles' current function is border guarding. The owner lists this as an advanced trap.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Border management needs one mandate." Qualified conclusion: mandate clarity is a capability reform, not only a boundary question.

**Back:** *"Thirty-one in insurgency, fifteen at the line: the rifles are shooting at the wrong target."*

### Flashcard IS-098 — Vibrant Villages Programme (2022) as an accommodation tool

**Front:** What role does the Vibrant Villages Programme play in North-East peace?

**Definition (learn):** A border-village infrastructure and development programme (2022) that also reduces alienation in North-East border districts.

**Breakdown:**
- Framed as border infrastructure; also an accommodation tool (`✅ owner 04`, Advanced Section 4; ⚠️ owner-stated).
- Cross-referenced to topic 06 (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-097, IS-096, IS-099.

**Trap:** Presenting border-village schemes as settlement instruments.

**Mains hook:** Use in border-development answers. Qualified conclusion: village schemes build presence, not settlement.

**Back:** *"Villages in the margin, lights on the line: development as a border's soft power."*

### Flashcard IS-099 — Myanmar as the biggest foreign link

**Front:** Why is Myanmar the biggest foreign link for North-East insurgents?

**Definition (learn):** Shared ethnicity, long difficult-terrain border with Arunachal Pradesh, Nagaland, Manipur and Mizoram.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh: Myanmar "the biggest foreign link" (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 45).
- Bangladesh, Nepal, Bhutan and China also historically served as transit or shelter points (`✅ owner 04`, PDF pp. 45-46; book-period).
- VisionIAS: narcotics surge from Myanmar (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-096, IS-097, IS-103.

**Trap:** Treating all cross-border links as Myanmar-only.

**Mains hook:** Use in cross-border answers. Qualified conclusion: Myanmar is the main link; the border is multi-state, and transit uses differ.

**Back:** *"Same faces, same hills, long line: Myanmar is the family across the fence."*

### Flashcard IS-100 — 2012 Bodo-Muslim clashes and the SMS exodus

**Front:** What were the 2012 Bodo-Muslim clashes and what effect did social media have?

**Definition (learn):** Clashes in Kokrajhar and Chirang (Assam), driven by fears of demographic change tied to migration, amplified by provocative SMS messages that triggered an exodus of North-East people from southern states.

**Breakdown:**
- Displacement "more than four lakh" people; many lives claimed (`✅ owner 04`, Singh PDF p. 47). Owner uses displacement scale, not casualty figures.
- SMS circulation triggered the exodus from southern States (`✅ owner 04`, PDF pp. 47-48).
- Spread across lower Assam (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2012 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-099, IS-101, topic 09 social-media owner (not in this fragment).

**Trap:** Quoting a casualty figure not in the source.

**Mains hook:** 10 or 15 marks — "Identity conflict and information amplification." Qualified conclusion: misinformation can convert a local clash into a national displacement; the response must include digital-channel control with rights safeguards.

**Back:** *"Two-twelve, Kokrajhar's fear, a phone's message: the rumour crossed the state line faster than the police."*

### Flashcard IS-101 — Manipur 2023 ethnic violence (context)

**Front:** Why is the 2023 Manipur conflict a key reference point in North-East internal security?

**Definition (learn):** An inter-ethnic conflict (Meitei-Kuki tensions) that the owner records as a driver of the 2024 FMR decision and of the UPF/KNO SoO.

**Breakdown:**
- Cited as a reason in the 2024 FMR decision (`✅ owner 04`, Section 8).
- Article 356 and Entry 2A instruments applied in Manipur (`✅ owner 04`; ⚠️ current status must be verified).
- Owner records "Meitei-Kuki tensions" (`✅ owner 04` Advanced).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2023.

**Links:** IS-096, IS-090, IS-102.

**Trap:** Stating current constitutional status of Manipur without a Gazette or MHA source.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Inter-ethnic conflict as an internal-security threat." Qualified conclusion: inter-ethnic conflict needs reconciliation design as well as security.

**Back:** *"Twenty-three's flame in the valley and hills: the conflict that reshaped the border."*

### Flashcard IS-102 — Article 356 as the residual instrument vs Union List Entry 2A

**Front:** How does the escalation ladder run from central forces to President's Rule?

**Definition (learn):** Where State law-and-order machinery fails, the Union's options escalate from central-force deployment under Union List Entry 2A (aid to civil power, State government intact) to President's Rule under Article 356 (State executive superseded).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 04 Advanced Section 4 (`✅ owner 04`).
- President's Rule is time-bound and reversible (`✅ owner 04`).
- Verify current constitutional status from Gazette or MHA (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Article 356; Entry 2A.

**Links:** IS-005 (Seventh Schedule), IS-101.

**Trap:** Treating Entry 2A deployment as President's Rule.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "When should the Centre escalate in a State?" Qualified conclusion: escalation must follow the least intrusive instrument that restores law and order.

**Back:** *"Aid first, takeover last: 2A helps the State stand; 356 replaces it."*

### Flashcard IS-103 — Identity-driven vs resource-driven insurgency

**Front:** Why does the North-East need political-structural tools while LWE needs governance and land tools?

**Definition (learn):** North-East insurgency is predominantly identity or autonomy driven; LWE is resource and land driven. The primary durable tool therefore differs.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 04 Advanced Section 2 (`✅ owner 04`).
- Political-structural tools: Sixth Schedule, statehood, autonomous councils, accords (`✅ owner 04`).
- LWE: land, forest and governance (IS-055).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-055, IS-076, IS-099.

**Trap:** Applying the LWE toolset to the North-East or the reverse.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — "Compare LWE and North-East insurgency." Qualified conclusion: the threat type sets the tool; the same doctrine calibrates differently.

**Back:** *"Identity needs a constitution, resource needs a register: match the tool to the grievance."*

### Flashcard IS-104 — Composite strategy (talks, action, development, autonomy)

**Front:** What is Singh's composite strategy for the North-East?

**Definition (learn):** The Government's combined approach: talks with groups renouncing violence, strict action against continuing violence, development and connectivity, and political or structural autonomy tools.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 04 Section 2 (`✅ owner 04`, PDF p. 44).
- Parallel tracks, not sequential (`✅ owner 04` Advanced Section 2).
- Calibrated group by group (`✅ owner 04`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-085, IS-086, IS-078, IS-081.

**Trap:** Presenting the strategy as one uniform process.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Balance talks and force in the North-East." Qualified conclusion: accommodation and calibrated security are complementary.

**Back:** *"Talk to the willing, act on the violent, build the road, and grant the council: four hands at one table."*

### Flashcard IS-105 — 2025 Q19 and 2019 Q20 North-East PYQ routes

**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2025 Q19 map question and the 2019 Q20 India-Myanmar border question?

**Definition (learn):** 2025 Q19 (15 marks): challenges and accord map for the past decade. 2019 Q20 (15 marks): India-Myanmar border security challenges and North-East insurgency.

**Breakdown:**
- 2025 Q19 verbatim: "What are the major challenges to internal security and peace process in the North-Eastern States? Map the various peace accords and agreements initiated by the government in the past decade." (`✅ owner 04`).
- 2025 route: challenge families; instrument-ladder map; composite strategy (`✅ owner 04` Section 12).
- 2019 route: cross-border ethnic ties, terrain, sanctuary and drug-route vulnerability; Assam Rifles, ICPs and intelligence; FMR decision as announcement (`✅ owner 04` Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQs 2019 and 2025.

**Links:** IS-086, IS-091, IS-097, IS-096.

**Trap:** Treating FMR as the whole 2019 answer.

**Mains hook:** Answer the 2025 map with visible rungs; the 2019 answer with the lawful-mobility and trust conclusion.

**Back:** *"Nineteen, twenty-five: challenges first, accords mapped by rung, and the border answered with trust."*

### GROUP — Jammu-Kashmir and Cross-Border Terrorism (owner 05: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-106 — Instrument of Accession (26 October 1947)

**Front:** When did Maharaja Hari Singh sign the Instrument of Accession, and which subjects did it cover?

**Definition (learn):** The instrument by which the Maharaja acceded J&K to India on the subjects of defence, external affairs and communications.

**Breakdown:**
- Signed 26 October 1947 (`✅ owner 05`; `✅ Wikipedia: Instrument of Accession`).
- Accepted by Mountbatten on 27 October 1947 (`✅ Wikipedia: Instrument of Accession`).
- Subjects: defence, external affairs, communications (`✅ owner 05`; Wikipedia confirms defence and external relations generally, not communications separately for J&K).
- Finance is not a separate accession subject (`✅ owner 05`; Wikipedia does not list finance among the subjects).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 26 and 27 October 1947.

**Links:** IS-107 (UNSC Resolution 47), IS-108 (LoC), IS-113 (2019 reorganisation).

**Trap:** Saying the accession covered all subjects, or treating the accession as the end of the dispute. The owner says the dispute is unresolved.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Accession and its unresolved dispute." Qualified conclusion: the instrument settled legal title for India in the owner's account, but the territorial and proxy dimension remains open.

**Back:** *"Twenty-six October, three subjects: defence, foreign, communications; the rest was never handed over."*

### Flashcard IS-107 — UNSC Resolution 47 (21 April 1948)

**Front:** What did UNSC Resolution 47 envisage, and why has it remained infructuous?

**Definition (learn):** The UN resolution of 21 April 1948 which envisaged Pakistan securing withdrawal of tribesmen and Pakistani nationals, followed by force reduction and a plebiscite stage.

**Breakdown:**
- Process "has remained infructuous till date since Pakistan has refused to withdraw its forces from PoK" (`✅ owner 05`, PDF pp. 24-25).
- Pakistan's withdrawal is the precondition for later stages (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 7).
- ⚠️ Resolution text not fetched in this build.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 21 April 1948.

**Links:** IS-106, IS-109 (PoK), IS-124 (referendum argument).

**Trap:** Stating the plebiscite was rejected by India. The owner says the precondition (Pakistani withdrawal) is what stalled the process.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Why has the UN plebiscite not occurred?" Qualified conclusion: the stalled step is Pakistan's withdrawal; the answer must say so, not infer a unilateral Indian choice.

**Back:** *"Forty-seven says first withdraw, then reduce, then vote: the first step never came."*

### Flashcard IS-108 — Line of Control (LoC): establishment and purpose

**Front:** When was the LoC established and what is India's stated objective for it?

**Definition (learn):** The Line of Control is the ceasefire line that replaced the earlier ceasefire line after the 1971 war; India's objective is to maintain the status quo and seek its conversion into a recognised international border.

**Breakdown:**
- Ceasefire of 17 December 1971; ratified through the Simla (Shimla) Agreement; the LoC name agreed; established 2 July 1972 (`✅ Wikipedia: Line of Control`).
- Origin: roughly the 1949 ceasefire line (`✅ Wikipedia: Line of Control`).
- India's objective: maintain the status quo and convert LoC into an international border (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 25).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 17 December 1971; Simla Agreement; 2 July 1972.

**Links:** IS-115 (2003 understanding), IS-120 (ceasefire violations), IS-117 (UNMOGIP).

**Trap:** Dating the LoC to 1971 (the war) rather than 1972 (established). Also the LoC is a ceasefire line, not an international border.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "The LoC is a line of control, not a border." Qualified conclusion: its status is contested; security management proceeds on the line as it exists.

**Back:** *"Seventy-one's ceasefire, seventy-two's line: a control, not yet a border."*

### Flashcard IS-109 — Pakistan-occupied Kashmir (PoK) / "Azad Kashmir"

**Front:** What is PoK, and what do the two countries call it?

**Definition (learn):** The area from which invading Pakistani-backed tribesmen were not driven out in 1947-48. India's name is Pakistan-occupied Kashmir; Pakistan calls it Azad Kashmir.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 2 (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 24).
- Non-withdrawal is the key obstacle under UNSC Resolution 47 (IS-107).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1947-48.

**Links:** IS-107, IS-108, IS-124.

**Trap:** Using the two names interchangeably without noting the political naming difference.

**Mains hook:** Use as the territorial anchor in a J&K answer. Qualified conclusion: the naming difference marks the dispute, so use neutral description with source attribution.

**Back:** *"Two names, one line: India says occupied, Pakistan says free; the map says contested."*

### Flashcard IS-110 — Low-intensity proxy war after 1965 and 1971

**Front:** Why did Pakistan shift to low-intensity warfare in Kashmir?

**Definition (learn):** After losing the 1965 and 1971 wars, Pakistan adopted low-intensity warfare: supporting terrorist and separatist activity in Kashmir rather than direct conventional war.

**Breakdown:**
- Shift after losing the two main wars in 1965 and 1971 (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 25).
- Punjab template: the Khalistan playbook later replicated in Kashmir with a similar ISI-support model (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 3, PDF pp. 8-9, 26).
- 1989 insurgency partly triggered by a disputed 1987 state election, then escalated with Afghan-war veterans (`✅ owner 05`, PDF pp. 25-26).
- Afghan-war spillover: mujahideen entering the Valley reinforced the JKLF-led insurgency (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 3).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1965, 1971, 1987, 1989 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-023 (proxy logic), IS-111, IS-112, IS-122.

**Trap:** Treating the 1987 election as the root cause. Owner says it was a trigger, and the deeper condition was ISI's readiness to exploit any legitimacy gap.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Pakistan's Kashmir strategy has adapted from conventional war to proxy militancy." Qualified conclusion: the adaptation is the proxy logic; attribution must be graded by evidence.

**Back:** *"Lost in sixty-five and seventy-one, switched to a thousand cuts: the proxy is the second war."*

### Flashcard IS-111 — ISI-linked militant groups in J&K: LeT, JeM and Hizbul Mujahideen

**Front:** Which militant groups does Singh name as ISI-linked infiltrators in J&K?

**Definition (learn):** Lashkar-e-Taiba, Jaish-e-Mohammed and Hizbul Mujahideen, which infiltrated across the LoC with training, funding and weapons from Pakistan's ISI.

**Breakdown:**
- Named as historically the most prominent (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 26).
- Owner 05 Section 6 notes they are book-period groups; current status must be verified.
- Shared proxy-actor logic with LeT in IS-023.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-023, IS-110, IS-122.

**Trap:** Treating book-period groups' strength or leadership as current. Owner 05 warns about this.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on proxy structure. Qualified conclusion: group names are historical examples; the mechanism is what persists.

**Back:** *"Taiba, Mujahideen, Hizbul: named in the book, dated by the book, verified by the agency."*

### Flashcard IS-112 — Kashmiri Pandit exodus and rehabilitation

**Front:** What happened to the Kashmiri Pandits in the 1990s and what is the rehabilitation problem?

**Definition (learn):** Nearly four lakh Kashmiri Pandits were forced to flee the Valley in the 1990s, creating acute demographic change and an unresolved rehabilitation problem.

**Breakdown:**
- "Nearly four lakh" (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 26).
- Singh frames the exodus as a step toward "ethnic cleansing" altering the Valley's demographic and political character (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 3).
- Displaced Pandits "continue to live in temporary refugee camps"; safe return failure called "one of the stark failures of successive governments" (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 5, PDF p. 26).
- MHA 26 February 2026 relief status (IS-125).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1990s; 2008 rehabilitation package (IS-118).

**Links:** IS-118, IS-125, IS-126.

**Trap:** Treating the Pandit problem as the same type as North-East insurgency. Owner 05 says keep analytically distinct.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on rehabilitation and trust. Qualified conclusion: rehabilitation packages address material loss; legitimacy and safe return are separate outcomes.

**Back:** *"Four lakh left, camps remain: the return is the test, not the package."*

### Flashcard IS-113 — Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019 (operational consequence)

**Front:** What did the J&K Reorganisation Act, 2019 create, and why does it matter operationally for internal security?

**Definition (learn):** The Act created two Union Territories, Jammu and Kashmir (with a legislature) and Ladakh (without one). The J&K Assembly cannot legislate on public order and police, which remain with the Union.

**Breakdown:**
- Assent 9 August 2019; Lok Sabha 6 August 2019; Rajya Sabha 5 August 2019; effective 31 October 2019 (`✅ Wikipedia: J&K Reorganisation Act 2019`).
- Ladakh covers Leh and Kargil; the residuary J&K is the other UT (`✅ Wikipedia: J&K Reorganisation Act 2019`).
- Assembly may legislate on State List matters "except 'public order' and 'police'", which "will remain as the law-making powers of the union government" (`✅ Wikipedia: J&K Reorganisation Act 2019`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2019 Act; effective 31 October 2019.

**Links:** IS-114, IS-115, IS-129 (Article 370 boundary).

**Trap:** Saying the Assembly controls the police. Also: the constitutional debate on Article 370 belongs to Polity.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Analyse the operational consequences of the 2019 reorganisation." Qualified conclusion: coordination is Lieutenant Governor and Union centred; the trade-off is speed against local buy-in.

**Back:** *"Two UTs, and police stays with the Union: the Assembly law-makes, but not on order or police."*

### Flashcard IS-114 — Lieutenant Governor-Union chain vs Centre-State chain (J&K)

**Front:** Why does J&K's security coordination differ structurally from a State's?

**Definition (learn):** Because police and public order sit outside the Assembly's competence, the operational chain runs through the Lieutenant Governor and the Union, not a Chief Minister and State government.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 2 and Advanced Section 2 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Coordination is intra-executive rather than Centre-State (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 2).
- Still true after Assembly elections (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 8).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2019 Act (IS-113).

**Links:** IS-113, IS-006 (Centre-State coordination), IS-129.

**Trap:** Assuming an elected Assembly reverts J&K to a normal State chain.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — operational coordination post-2019. Qualified conclusion: the statute fixes the chain; the political assessment is separate.

**Back:** *"Elected Assembly, appointed chain: the police run through the Lieutenant Governor's table."*

### Flashcard IS-115 — LoC ceasefire understanding (2003) and DGMOs' reaffirmation (25 February 2021)

**Front:** What is the status of the 2003 LoC ceasefire understanding and the 25 February 2021 DGMO statement?

**Definition (learn):** A military-to-military understanding on the LoC: a November 2003 ceasefire agreement, reaffirmed by the two Directors General of Military Operations in a joint statement of 25 February 2021.

**Breakdown:**
- November 2003 LoC ceasefire (`✅ Wikipedia: Line of Control`, which gives no exact day).
- Reaffirmation on 25 February 2021 by DGMOs (`✅ owner 05`; ⚠️ not confirmed by fetched Wikipedia text).
- Not a treaty or settlement; does not alter infiltration (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** November 2003; 25 February 2021.

**Links:** IS-108, IS-120, IS-122.

**Trap:** Treating a reaffirmed ceasefire as a settlement or as the end of infiltration.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "A ceasefire on the LoC is a humanitarian gain, not resolution." Examine. Qualified conclusion: the ceasefire reduces one category of harm; other metrics need separate evidence.

**Back:** *"Two-thousand-three ceasefire, two-thousand-twenty-one reaffirmation: quiet on the line, not closure."*

### Flashcard IS-116 — Armed Forces (Jammu and Kashmir) Special Powers Act, 1990

**Front:** How does the J&K special powers statute relate to AFSPA 1958?

**Definition (learn):** The J&K-specific special-powers statute, separate from the 1958 AFSPA, with parallel structure: disturbed-area declaration, enabling powers and prior sanction for prosecution.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 (`✅ owner 05`; ⚠️ not fetched in this build).
- Parallel to AFSPA 1958 sections 3, 4 and 6 (IS-081).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1990.

**Links:** IS-081, IS-082, IS-083.

**Trap:** Treating J&K as covered by AFSPA 1958.

**Mains hook:** Use in rights comparisons. Qualified conclusion: two parallel statutes need the same accountability logic.

**Back:** *"Ninety for Kashmir, fifty-eight for the North-East: two statutes, one sanction."*

### Flashcard IS-117 — UNMOGIP (UN Military Observer Group in India and Pakistan)

**Front:** What does UNMOGIP do?

**Definition (learn):** The UN observer group that supervises the ceasefire line and reports violations.

**Breakdown:**
- Continues to supervise the ceasefire line and report violations (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 24).
- IR owns the institutional detail; this topic notes operational relevance only (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated; ⚠️ founding year not verified.

**Links:** IS-107, IS-108.

**Trap:** Treating UNMOGIP as an enforcement force.

**Mains hook:** Use only as context in a LoC answer.

**Back:** *"Observers watch the line; they do not hold it."*

### Flashcard IS-118 — Development and rehabilitation packages for J&K (book-period)

**Front:** Which book-period development and rehabilitation initiatives does owner 05 list?

**Definition (learn):** The Prime Minister's Reconstruction Plan (2004), the UDAAN skill-development initiative, and a 2008 rehabilitation package for Kashmiri Pandit migrants.

**Breakdown:**
- `✅ owner 05`, PDF pp. 27, 30 (book-period).
- Owner 05 Advanced Section 4 describes them as "development alongside security."
- Verify current names and allocations from a dated official source.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2004; 2008 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-112, IS-125, IS-126.

**Trap:** Quoting book-period packages as current.

**Mains hook:** Use in hearts-and-minds answers, with a status note.

**Back:** *"Reconstruction in 2004, skills as UDAAN, a Pandit package in 2008: the schemes are dated; the outcomes need checking."*

### Flashcard IS-119 — Village Defence Committees / Village Defence Guards

**Front:** What are Village Defence Guards and what trade-off do they carry?

**Definition (learn):** The community self-defence model originating in J&K (VDCs) and renamed Village Defence Guards, functioning under the district SP or SSP to provide residents of remote hilly villages with weapons and arms training.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh: VDC referenced as a precedent for LWE areas (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 64).
- Renamed VDG; SP/SSP supervision (`✅ owner 05`; VisionIAS PDF p. 30).
- Trade-off: local deterrence and early warning, against weakened command accountability (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 4).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated; ⚠️ start year not fetched.

**Links:** IS-064 (LWE units), IS-112, IS-120.

**Trap:** Presenting armed civilians as an unqualified measure.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on community defence. Qualified conclusion: supervision decides whether the model helps or harms.

**Back:** *"Guards with guns in hills: local eyes, but the SP holds the leash."*

### Flashcard IS-120 — Ceasefire violations as an ISI instrument

**Front:** Why does Singh treat LoC ceasefire violations as deliberate rather than routine friction?

**Definition (learn):** Singh says ceasefire violations are planned by the ISI to keep the J&K issue alive, to internationalise it, to keep Indian forces engaged and to aid infiltration.

**Breakdown:**
- Quote: "planned by the ISI mainly to keep the J&K issue alive, to try to internationalise it, keep the Indian forces engaged and to aid the infiltration of the terrorists" (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 29).
- A deliberate tactic, distinct from routine border-management friction (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 2).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-108, IS-115, IS-122.

**Trap:** Calling ceasefire violations routine friction.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "A quiet LoC is not the end of cross-border terror." Qualified conclusion: cross-LoC firing is one metric; infiltration is another.

**Back:** *"Firing as a tool: keep the issue alive, keep forces busy, cover the infiltration."*

### Flashcard IS-121 — Post-2008 crowd mobilisation and stone-pelting

**Front:** What post-2008 tactical shift does Singh document in Kashmir?

**Definition (learn):** An ISI strategy shift toward crowd mobilisation to defame Indian security forces and internationalise the Kashmir issue, with stone-pelting becoming a routine feature of street protest.

**Breakdown:**
- "In the last 4-5 years" (book-period, post-2008) (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 26).
- Stone-pelting as a routine feature (`✅ owner 05`).
- Information and legitimacy contest (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 3).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Post-2008 (book-period).

**Links:** IS-110, IS-122, IS-124.

**Trap:** Treating stone-pelting as spontaneous and unconnected. Owner 05 says it is part of a deliberate tactic.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Pakistan's Kashmir strategy has continuously adapted." Qualified conclusion: legitimacy contest needs communication as well as security response.

**Back:** *"Post-2008, the crowd became the weapon: stone and sound, not only guns."*

### Flashcard IS-122 — Four J&K metrics that must not be merged

**Front:** What are the four J&K metrics that answers routinely merge?

**Definition (learn):** LoC ceasefire violations; infiltration attempts versus successful infiltration; local recruitment into militancy; and hinterland incidents. Each is reported separately and moves independently.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 10 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Infiltration, tunnelling and drone-borne deliveries are separate metrics (`✅ owner 05` Section 7).
- Use the metric you rely on, with a date (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-120, IS-115, IS-123.

**Trap:** Asserting general improvement or deterioration without naming the metric.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — "Compare the LoC, infiltration, recruitment and hinterland metrics." Qualified conclusion: a quiet LoC is one gain; the others need evidence.

**Back:** *"Four gauges, four needles: LoC, infiltration, recruits, hinterland; never read one as the other."*

### Flashcard IS-123 — Attribution as a graded judgement

**Front:** What evidence grades the attribution of a proxy-war incident to a sponsor?

**Definition (learn):** Attribution runs along a gradient: material recovered, communication intercepts, cadre origin, training-camp provenance, financing trail and state-institution linkage.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Advanced Section 2 (`✅ owner 05`).
- An answer that names the evidence basis is analysing; "Pakistan-sponsored" alone is an assertion (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-023, IS-122, IS-019 (chain).

**Trap:** Stating sponsorship as fact without a dated competent-agency finding.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on proxy attribution. Qualified conclusion: attribution is graded; evidence determines the level.

**Back:** *"Show the evidence ladder before the verdict: material, intercepts, origin, camp, money, state."*

### Flashcard IS-124 — Referendum argument (closed, not open, policy question)

**Front:** Why does Singh consider a referendum meaningless?

**Definition (learn):** Singh argues a referendum is "meaningless" given the demographic change since 1947 and Pakistan's non-withdrawal from PoK, which is the UNSC Resolution 47 precondition.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 5 (`✅ owner 05`, PDF p. 29).
- Reasoning remains analytically useful independent of the 2019 change (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-107, IS-109, IS-113.

**Trap:** Presenting the book's view as the state's current official position without checking.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Is a plebiscite still a viable answer?" Qualified conclusion: the precondition is unmet; the argument is Singh's, not a binding rule.
