**Back:** *"Withdraw first, vote later, and the demographics moved: a referendum on a moved map is meaningless."*

### Flashcard IS-125 — MHA 26 February 2026 relief and rehabilitation status for Kashmiri migrants

**Front:** What current anchor does owner 05 use for Kashmiri migrant rehabilitation?

**Definition (learn):** An MHA relief and rehabilitation status note dated 26 February 2026, cited as the current operational anchor for trust, rehabilitation and return.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 8 (`✅ owner 05`; 📰 owner-stated, not fetched).
- Use as a dated implementation input, not as proof of safe return (`✅ owner 05` Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 26 February 2026 (owner-stated; ⚠️ not verified in fetched source).

**Links:** IS-112, IS-118, IS-126.

**Trap:** Treating package delivery as evidence of return or resolved conflict.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — hearts and minds. Qualified conclusion: rehabilitation status is an input; return and legitimacy are outcomes.

**Back:** *"A dated note is a dated fact: use it as the input, not the verdict."*

### Flashcard IS-126 — 2023 "Hearts and Minds" PYQ route (four tracks)

**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2023 J&K hearts-and-minds question?

**Definition (learn):** 2023 GS-III (10 marks): "Winning of 'Hearts and Minds' in terrorism-affected areas is an essential step in restoring the trust of the population. Discuss the measures adopted by the Government in this respect as part of the conflict resolution in Jammu and Kashmir."

**Breakdown:**
- Four tracks: civilian security and proportionate force; local participation and grievance redress; service and livelihood and rehabilitation; accountability and remedy (`✅ owner 05` Section 12).
- Not a scheme list or force-operations list (`✅ owner 05`).
- Current rehab status used only as an input (IS-125).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2023 (`✅ owner 05`, PYQ integration).

**Links:** IS-121, IS-125, IS-118, IS-119.

**Trap:** Listing schemes without evaluating effectiveness.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — hearts and minds. Qualified conclusion: trust is an operational capability; its measure is participation and remedy, not outlay.

**Back:** *"Security, voice, service, remedy: four tracks, one trust."*

### Flashcard IS-127 — 2019 over-ground workers (OGW) PYQ route

**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2019 OGW question?

**Definition (learn):** 2019 GS-III (10 marks): examine the role of over-ground workers in assisting terrorist organisations in insurgency.

**Breakdown:**
- OGW defined as a non-combatant facilitator whose alleged role must be proved (`✅ owner 05` Section 12).
- Possible functions: information and reconnaissance, shelter and logistics, communication, recruitment and propaganda, finance (`✅ owner 05`).
- Response: community intelligence and protection; evidence-led investigation; lawful financial tracing; prosecution on admissible evidence (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2019 (`✅ owner 05`).

**Links:** IS-023, IS-026, IS-019.

**Trap:** Labelling ordinary political dissent as OGW activity. Owner 05 names this trap.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — OGW. Qualified conclusion: indiscriminate labelling destroys the local reporting counter-terror needs.

**Back:** *"Facilitator, not fighter; proof, not label."*

### Flashcard IS-128 — 2018 CPEC cross-subject route (sovereignty and security leg)

**Front:** How should the 2018 CPEC question be answered from the internal-security side?

**Definition (learn):** The 2018 GS-III question on CPEC as an OBOR subset and India's strategic objections. This topic supplies the sovereignty and security leg; IR and Economy own connectivity and diplomacy.

**Breakdown:**
- Corridor passes through territory claimed by India and administered by Pakistan (`✅ owner 05` Section 12).
- Can alter strategic logistics in a contingency; links external infrastructure to the J&K proxy-security context (`✅ owner 05`).
- Owner routes the marks as "10 marks, 150 words" in the PYQ table but "15 marks" in Section 12 (`✅ owner 05`). ⚠️ Inconsistency in owner text; verify the paper's mark value.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2018 (`✅ owner 05`).

**Links:** IS-106, IS-113, IS-114.

**Trap:** Manufacturing an incident or merging the CPEC question with the hearts-and-minds question.

**Mains hook:** Use the bounded sovereignty and security leg. Qualified conclusion: strategic logistics are a contingency concern, not a verified current troop outcome.

**Back:** *"Corridor through contested ground: the sovereignty and the logistics are the security leg."*

### Flashcard IS-129 — Article 370 boundary (operational vs constitutional)

**Front:** What belongs to this topic and what belongs to Polity regarding Article 370?

**Definition (learn):** This topic covers only the operational internal-security consequences of the change in J&K's status. Article 370's constitutional text, litigation history and the December 2023 Supreme Court judgment belong to Polity.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 4 and Section 13 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Book-period debate on removal (Singh PDF pp. 28-29) is historical evidence, not current law (`✅ owner 05`).
- Owner 00 Section 9 boundary routing (`✅ owner 00`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis; December 2023 judgment cited by owner (⚠️ not fetched).

**Links:** IS-113, IS-114, IS-124.

**Trap:** Presenting Singh's pre-2019 debate as the present legal position.

**Mains hook:** Use in J&K answers: name the boundary and proceed with operational analysis.

**Back:** *"The Article is Polity's; the operations are ours."*

### Flashcard IS-130 — Kashmiri Pandit rehabilitation as an unresolved boundary case

**Front:** Why is Pandit rehabilitation a durable policy gap distinct from the 2019 reorganisation?

**Definition (learn):** Displaced Pandits still live in temporary camps, and the unresolved return is a problem that predates 2019 and is analytically separate from it.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Section 5 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Material loss vs political and emotional legitimacy concerns (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1990s onward (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-112, IS-125, IS-113.

**Trap:** Attributing the Pandit problem to the 2019 change.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Rehabilitation without legitimacy." Qualified conclusion: packages are necessary and insufficient.

**Back:** *"Packages pay the rent; only return pays the home."*

### Flashcard IS-131 — Pakistan's Kashmir strategy: continuous adaptation (Mains thesis)

**Front:** What is the advanced thesis on Pakistan's Kashmir strategy and India's response?

**Definition (learn):** Pakistan's strategy adapted from conventional war to proxy militancy and then to an information and legitimacy contest; India's response combined security with development, rehabilitation and, since 2019, a centralised structure.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Advanced Section 1 and 11 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Causal chain: defeat 1965-71 → Punjab replication → 1987 trigger → Afghan spillover → demographic engineering → post-2008 tactical shift → 2019 response (`✅ owner 05` Advanced Section 3).
- Each response carries its own trade-off (`✅ owner 05`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-110, IS-121, IS-122, IS-123.

**Trap:** Treating the strategy as static, or as proven without graded attribution.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Trace the evolution of Pakistan's Kashmir strategy." Qualified conclusion: adaptation is the pattern; the current toolkit needs a dated verification.

**Back:** *"Lost the wars, changed the weapons: war to proxy to crowd to narrative."*

### Flashcard IS-132 — LoC ceasefire as humanitarian gain vs terror resolution (advanced thesis)

**Front:** How should a quiet LoC be evaluated in a Mains answer?

**Definition (learn):** A ceasefire on the LoC reduces firing and civilian harm, a genuine gain, but it does not resolve cross-border terrorism, which has separate metrics.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 05 Advanced Section 12, question 4 (`✅ owner 05`).
- Infiltration and hinterland activity can move in opposite direction (`✅ owner 05` Section 7).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-115, IS-120, IS-122.

**Trap:** Reading a reaffirmed ceasefire as the end of the proxy war.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — exact question from owner 05. Qualified conclusion: humanitarian gain is real; counter-infiltration and hinterland work continue.

**Back:** *"A quiet line is good news for villagers, not a verdict on the war."*

### GROUP — Border Management and Border Area Development (owner 06: Basic and Advanced)

### Flashcard IS-133 — Border security vs border management

**Front:** How do border security and border management differ?

**Definition (learn):** Border security is only the defence of the border. Border management is the broader term that includes regulation, development of border areas and bilateral mechanisms.

**Breakdown:**
- VisionIAS definition (`✅ owner 06`, PDF p. 4).
- Department of Border Management, MHA, manages land and coastal borders, guarding, infrastructure and BADP (`✅ owner 06`, PDF p. 4).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Department of Border Management (MHA; created after Group of Ministers recommendations, owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-134, IS-135, IS-149.

**Trap:** Treating the two as synonyms. Owner 06 flags this in its first trap.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Distinguish border security from border management." Qualified conclusion: security is necessary but not sufficient; management needs regulation and development too.

**Back:** *"Security defends the line; management runs the whole border."*

### Flashcard IS-134 — Four-element border management framework

**Front:** What are the four elements of India's border-management approach?

**Definition (learn):** Guarding, regulation, development of border areas, and bilateral institutional mechanisms for resolving disputes.

**Breakdown:**
- Singh's framing: "(a) guarding, (b) regulation, (c) development of border areas, and (d) constituting bilateral institutional mechanisms for resolving disputes" (`✅ owner 06`, PDF p. 126).
- Used to route every neighbour-specific challenge (`✅ owner 06` Section 1).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh (book-period).

**Links:** IS-133, IS-150, IS-151, IS-140.

**Trap:** Using only guarding as the answer. Owner 06 asks for all four.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — general border-management question. Qualified conclusion: the four elements interlock; weakness in one undermines the other three.

**Back:** *"Guard, regulate, develop, negotiate: four hands on one border."*

### Flashcard IS-135 — India's land and coastal border totals (MHA, as reproduced by VisionIAS)

**Front:** What are India's land-border and coastline totals?

**Definition (learn):** The MHA total of 15,106.7 km of land border shared with seven countries, and 7,516.6 km of coastline including island territories.

**Breakdown:**
- Seven countries including the claimed 106 km with Afghanistan (`✅ owner 06`; VisionIAS PDF p. 4).
- Coastline 7,516.6 km (`✅ owner 06`).
- ⚠️ Figures as reproduced in owner from VisionIAS; not fetched from MHA. Owner 00 notes the Singh figure of "nearly 15,000 km shared with six countries" as earlier.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Undated MHA figure as reproduced; ⚠️ verify as-of date.

**Links:** IS-136, IS-138, IS-146, IS-147.

**Trap:** Quoting the "six countries" figure from Singh as current. Owner 00 flags the difference.

**Mains hook:** Use in introductions only with a dated source. Qualified conclusion: total length is a planning figure; each border has a distinct challenge.

**Back:** *"Fifteen thousand one hundred and six point seven, seven countries, and the coast adds seven thousand five hundred and sixteen."*

### Flashcard IS-136 — India-Pakistan border: three segments (Radcliffe Line, LoC, AGPL)

**Front:** Name the three segments of the India-Pakistan border and their approximate lengths.

**Definition (learn):** The Radcliffe Line (about 2,308 km, Gujarat to parts of Jammu), the Line of Control (about 776 km), and the Actual Ground Position Line (about 110 km, NJ 9842 to Indira Col, Siachen). Total Pakistan border 3,323 km (owner).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 3.1 (`✅ owner 06`; VisionIAS PDF p. 11; Singh PDF pp. 121-122).
- Each segment has different guarding force and dominant threat: BSF and smuggling/tunnelling on the Radcliffe Line; Army and infiltration on LoC; Army and altitude on AGPL (`✅ owner 06`).
- Radcliffe Line published 17 August 1947; drawn by Cyril Radcliffe (`✅ Wikipedia: Radcliffe Line`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 17 August 1947 (publication).

**Links:** IS-108 (LoC), IS-137 (Sir Creek), IS-135.

**Trap:** Merging the three segments into one "Pakistan border". Owner 06 asks for segment-wise analysis.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Examine the conflicting issues on the Pakistan border." Qualified conclusion: segment-wise management is the analytic route; each has a different guard and threat.

**Back:** *"Radcliffe runs the plain, LoC the mountain, AGPL the glacier: three lines, three guards."*

### Flashcard IS-137 — Sir Creek: unsettled tidal estuary

**Front:** Why is Sir Creek unresolved, and what doctrines do the two sides invoke?

**Definition (learn):** A 96 km tidal estuary dividing Gujarat from Sindh. India invokes the Thalweg (mid-channel) doctrine; Pakistan claims the eastern-bank line.

**Breakdown:**
- 96 km length (`✅ Wikipedia: Sir Creek`; `✅ owner 06`).
- India relies on 1925 map and mid-channel pillars installed 1924 (`✅ Wikipedia: Sir Creek`).
- Pakistan relies on the 1914 Bombay Presidency resolution's paragraph 9 (`✅ Wikipedia: Sir Creek`).
- Twelve rounds of talks 1997-2012; joint map agreed in 2008; no breakthrough (`✅ Wikipedia: Sir Creek`).
- Pakistan proposes international arbitration; India refuses, citing the Simla Agreement (`✅ Wikipedia: Sir Creek`).
- EEZ stake: owner cites about 250 square miles (VisionIAS PDF p. 11; ⚠️ not in fetched text).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1914; 1925; 1924; 2008; 1997-2012.

**Links:** IS-136, IS-160, IS-142 (contrast with settled cases).

**Trap:** Saying Sir Creek is settled. It is unresolved. Also: Thalweg is contested on tidal versus non-tidal applicability.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Why do some boundaries stay open?" Qualified conclusion: where parties do not accept adjudication, technical boundaries remain open; Sir Creek is the converse of the Bangladesh settlement.

**Back:** *"Nineteen-fourteen says the east bank, nineteen-twenty-five says the middle: ninety-six kilometres of unsettled water."*

### Flashcard IS-138 — India-China border: disputed and undemarcated LAC

**Front:** What is the status of the India-China border?

**Definition (learn):** The entire 3,488 km boundary is disputed; the Line of Actual Control has no mutually agreed alignment along certain stretches, with disputes in Aksai Chin (west) and Arunachal Pradesh (east).

**Breakdown:**
- 3,488 km (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF p. 122; VisionIAS PDF pp. 6-9).
- McMahon Line reference (`✅ owner 06`).
- Himalayan terrain limits trans-border movement but tension recurs (`✅ owner 06`).
- ITBP mans most of the China border; Vikas Battalions under Cabinet Secretariat in some sectors (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 2).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-139, IS-140, IS-172, IS-148.

**Trap:** Treating the China border as a demarcated border with occasional violations. Owner 06 says it is disputed and largely undemarcated.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — China border issues separately from Pakistan. Qualified conclusion: the China problem is dispute resolution and military posture, not fencing.

**Back:** *"Thirty-four eighty-eight, none of it agreed: the line is drawn in the mind, not on the ground."*

### Flashcard IS-139 — Galwan (2020) and Tawang (2022) as test cases

**Front:** What do the 2020 Galwan clash and the 2022 Tawang face-off show?

**Definition (learn):** Two recent India-China episodes on undemarcated or differently perceived LAC stretches that show escalation risk even without firearms.

**Breakdown:**
- Galwan: "fought with sticks and clubs, not guns" (per VisionIAS; `✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 5, PDF p. 8).
- Tawang (Yangtse) face-off, 2022 (`✅ owner 06`).
- Followed by disengagement agreements (`✅ owner 06`).
- ⚠️ Casualty and toll figures not included; not verified.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2020; 2022.

**Links:** IS-138, IS-140, IS-160.

**Trap:** Treating the dispute-resolution mechanisms as having prevented these clashes. Owner 06 says they did not.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks on China border. Qualified conclusion: crisis channels reduce risk; they do not settle the boundary.

**Back:** *"Clubs in the valley, a face-off in Tawang: no shots, no settlement."*

### Flashcard IS-140 — Border Dispute Settlement Mechanism (China) and its limits

**Front:** What agreements form the India-China border dispute settlement mechanism, and what are their limits?

**Definition (learn):** A series of five agreements (1993, 1996, 2005, 2012, 2013) giving confidence-building and consultation mechanisms along the LAC.

**Breakdown:**
- Years 1993, 1996, 2005, 2012, 2013 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 9).
- Limits: these did not prevent Galwan or Tawang (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 4).
- Signing is not boundary demarcation (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1993-2013.

**Links:** IS-138, IS-139, IS-141.

**Trap:** Treating these agreements as prevention rather than de-escalation.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on the China border. Qualified conclusion: de-escalation channels are useful; demarcation is a separate, unfinished task.

**Back:** *"Five agreements, zero demarcation: channels for calm, not lines on the map."*

### Flashcard IS-141 — India-Bangladesh border: longest, porous, densely settled

**Front:** What are the dominant challenges on the India-Bangladesh border?

**Definition (learn):** The longest land border at 4,096 km, with illegal migration, cattle smuggling and trans-border crime as dominant challenges, running through West Bengal, Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram.

**Breakdown:**
- 4,096 km (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF pp. 122-123).
- Densely populated and cultivated up to the border; 54 trans-boundary rivers (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 21).
- Outstanding issues now principally river-water sharing (Teesta) and migration (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated; ⚠️ river count not fetched.

**Links:** IS-142, IS-143, IS-134.

**Trap:** Describing the Bangladesh border as unsettled on territory. Owner 06 corrects Singh on enclaves and the maritime boundary.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Bangladesh as the contrast case." Qualified conclusion: settled territory does not end porous regulation issues.

**Back:** *"Longest and most crowded: migration and water, not the map, are the open questions."*

### Flashcard IS-142 — 2015 Land Boundary Agreement and the 100th Constitutional Amendment Act (2015)

**Front:** What did the 100th Constitutional Amendment Act (2015) give effect to?

**Definition (learn):** The Land Boundary Agreement with Bangladesh, which exchanged enclaves and resolved the adverse-possession problem.

**Breakdown:**
- Given effect by the 100th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2015 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 21). ⚠️ Wikipedia's 100th Amendment page returned 404; not fetched.
- Settled enclave and adverse-possession problem (`✅ owner 06` correction of Singh).
- Owner 06 calls it a controlled example of boundary settlement by instrument (`✅ owner 06` Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2015.

**Links:** IS-141, IS-143, IS-160.

**Trap:** Saying the enclave problem is unresolved, as the book states.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Boundary disputes can be settled." Qualified conclusion: India's own settled case shows the route: arbitration plus constitutional instrument.

**Back:** *"Twenty-fifteen amendment, enclaves exchanged: the amendment closed a book's open question."*

### Flashcard IS-143 — 2014 UNCLOS arbitral award (India-Bangladesh maritime boundary)

**Front:** How was the India-Bangladesh maritime boundary settled?

**Definition (learn):** The maritime boundary was fixed by a 2014 UNCLOS arbitral award in proceedings Bangladesh instituted in 2009.

**Breakdown:**
- Arbitral award 2014; proceedings instituted 2009 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 21).
- Owner 06 Section 8 trap: book's view of "undelimited" maritime boundary is outdated (`✅ owner 06`).
- ⚠️ Not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2014; 2009.

**Links:** IS-142, IS-141, IS-137.

**Trap:** Repeating the book's "undelimited" maritime claim.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — settled boundaries. Qualified conclusion: adjudication can close a maritime question as well as a land question.

**Back:** *"Two-thousand-nine asked, two-thousand-fourteen answered: the sea line has a verdict."*

### Flashcard IS-144 — India-Nepal border: open border since 1950 treaty

**Front:** What is the status of the India-Nepal border?

**Definition (learn):** A 1,751 km open border since the 1950 Treaty of Peace and Friendship, historically misused for insurgent and terrorist transit.

**Breakdown:**
- 1,751 km; open since 1950 Treaty (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF pp. 123-124).
- Historical transit misuse by Sikh and Kashmiri militants (1980s), ULFA, NDFB, KLO and ISI-linked groups (`✅ owner 06`).
- Guarded by SSB (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1950; 1,751 km.

**Links:** IS-145, IS-148, IS-134.

**Trap:** Treating an open border as a lack of control. Openness is a treaty design; control is regulation.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Open border, open threats." Qualified conclusion: regulation of misuse, not closure, is the practical instrument.

**Back:** *"Nineteen-fifty opened the gate; regulation, not a wall, keeps it honest."*

### Flashcard IS-145 — India-Bhutan border: mostly demarcated

**Front:** What is the status of the India-Bhutan border?

**Definition (learn):** A 699 km border mostly demarcated, except at the China tri-junction, historically used by North-East groups (KLO, ULFA, NDFB) for camps before being flushed out.

**Breakdown:**
- 699 km (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF p. 124).
- SSB guards it (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-144, IS-148, IS-099 (foreign links).

**Trap:** Treating Bhutan as a source of insurgent havens rather than a former transit or camp location.

**Mains hook:** Use in foreign-links answers. Qualified conclusion: the threat was camps, now reduced; the tri-junction remains a demarcation issue.

**Back:** *"Six ninety-nine, mostly drawn; the old camps were flushed, the tri-junction still waits."*

### Flashcard IS-146 — India-Myanmar border: 1,643 km, Golden Triangle edge, Assam Rifles since 2002

**Front:** What are the defining features of the India-Myanmar border?

**Definition (learn):** A 1,643 km border, rugged and weakly demarcated in pockets, with the Golden Triangle's edge facilitating drug trafficking. Assam Rifles has manned it since 2002 with some Army assistance.

**Breakdown:**
- 1,643 km; rugged; weakly demarcated pockets (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 25).
- Assam Rifles since 2002 (`✅ owner 06`).
- FMR scrapping decision 8 February 2024 (IS-096 and IS-158).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2002 (Assam Rifles); 2024 (FMR decision).

**Links:** IS-096, IS-097, IS-099, IS-158.

**Trap:** Describing the border as fully fenced or fully sealed.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — India-Myanmar border (PYQ 2019). Qualified conclusion: terrain, ethnic continuity and narcotics make regulation and trust as important as guarding.

**Back:** *"Sixteen forty-three, golden edge: drugs at the rim, Assam Rifles on the rim since two-thousand-two."*

### Flashcard IS-147 — Afghanistan claimed border (106 km)

**Front:** How does MHA count the Afghanistan border?

**Definition (learn):** MHA includes a claimed 106 km border with Afghanistan in its seven-country total, while the operational analysis covers six administered sectors.

**Breakdown:**
- Included in the 15,106.7 km total (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 4).
- Operational management focuses on six administered sectors (`✅ owner 06` Section 3.7).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-135, IS-136.

**Trap:** Presenting Afghanistan as an operationally managed border. The owner says it is claimed.

**Mains hook:** Use in border-count questions only.

**Back:** *"One hundred and six kilometres claimed, not managed: the seventh border counts on paper."*

### Flashcard IS-148 — Force-to-border mapping (BSF, ITBP, SSB, Assam Rifles)

**Front:** Which force guards each border?

**Definition (learn):** BSF guards the Pakistan and Bangladesh borders; ITBP the China border; SSB the Nepal and Bhutan borders; Assam Rifles the Myanmar border.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 4 and Section 6 (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF p. 127).
- One-force-one-border recommended by Kargil Review Committee and Group of Ministers (`✅ owner 06`).
- Multi-command gaps remain on the China border (IS-172).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-015, IS-149, IS-172.

**Trap:** Saying the principle is fully implemented. Owner 06 says it is partly implemented.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "One border, one force." Qualified conclusion: force mapping is simple on paper; the complexity is the second command.

**Back:** *"BSF on Pakistan and Bangladesh, ITBP on China, SSB on Nepal and Bhutan, Assam Rifles on Myanmar: four doors, one rule."*

### Flashcard IS-149 — Kargil Review Committee (1999) to Group of Ministers (2001) and the Godbole task force

**Front:** How did the Kargil Review Committee's recommendations reach border management?

**Definition (learn):** The Kargil Review Committee (1999) led to the Group of Ministers on Reforming the National Security System (2001), which had four task forces; its Task Force on Border Management was chaired by Madhav Godbole.

**Breakdown:**
- Recommendations: settle border disputes; create Department of Border Management; one-force-one-border; strengthen Coast Guard and marine police; accelerate border-area infrastructure (`✅ owner 06`, Singh PDF pp. 125-126).
- Godbole chair: `✅ owner 06` (⚠️ not verified in a fetched source).
- Kargil Review Committee 1999 (⚠️ year from memory).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1999; 2001.

**Links:** IS-015, IS-133, IS-150, IS-151.

**Trap:** Treating the GoM as a statute. It is a government reform body.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Reform after Kargil." Qualified conclusion: most elements remain partly implemented; the one completed element is the Bangladesh settlement.

**Back:** *"Ninety-nine's review, oh-one's ministers, Godbole's task force: the reform agenda for the border."*

### Flashcard IS-150 — Department of Border Management (MHA)

**Front:** What does the Department of Border Management do?

**Definition (learn):** An MHA department that manages international land and coastal borders, border policing and guarding, infrastructure (roads, fencing, floodlighting), and implements BADP.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 4 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 4).
- Created after the GoM recommendations (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated; creation year not fetched.

**Links:** IS-149, IS-151, IS-152.

**Trap:** Treating the department as a force. It is an administrative department.

**Mains hook:** Use to show the convergence mandate. Qualified conclusion: convergence needs sequencing to avoid overlapping schemes.

**Back:** *"The department runs the border's plan, not its patrols."*

### Flashcard IS-151 — Border Area Development Programme (BADP)

**Front:** What does BADP cover, and how is it funded?

**Definition (learn):** A village-level development programme for villages within 0-10 km of the international border, providing non-lapsable Special Central Assistance for infrastructure, livelihood, education and health.

**Breakdown:**
- 0-10 km (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 29).
- Non-lapsable Special Central Assistance (`✅ owner 06`).
- Not the same as BIM (IS-169).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-152, IS-169, IS-134.

**Trap:** Confusing BADP with BIM. Owner 06 flags it.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — BADP and BIM (PYQ 2024 Q19). Qualified conclusion: BADP builds village presence; its outcome depends on delivery and uptake.

**Back:** *"Ten kilometres of village, non-lapsable money: BADP holds the people."*

### Flashcard IS-152 — Border Infrastructure and Management (BIM) scheme

**Front:** What is the BIM scheme and what period does owner 06 state?

**Definition (learn):** A Central Sector umbrella scheme for roads, electricity and communication infrastructure along border areas.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 gives 2021-22 to 2025-26 per VisionIAS (`✅ owner 06`, PDF p. 30); ⚠️ period and allocation must be verified from the MHA Border Management-II annexure of 8 January 2026 (owner says so).
- Infrastructure focus, not village welfare (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2021-22 to 2025-26 (owner-stated, ⚠️).

**Links:** IS-151, IS-169, IS-153.

**Trap:** Quoting the period as current. Owner says verify.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — BIM. Qualified conclusion: infrastructure is a capability input; its effect depends on maintenance and use.

**Back:** *"Roads, power, signal along the line: BIM builds the path, not the patrol."*

### Flashcard IS-153 — Comprehensive Integrated Border Management System (CIBMS)

**Front:** What does CIBMS do?

**Definition (learn):** An integrated technology system using sensors, detectors, cameras and radar to close gaps in physical guarding.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 30).
- Technology complements people; it does not replace them (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 6).
- Detection is not interdiction (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated; ⚠️ year not fetched.

**Links:** IS-156 (grid protection), IS-152, IS-163.

**Trap:** Treating CIBMS as a complete border solution.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — technology in border management. Qualified conclusion: technology is a multiplier; human presence remains essential in difficult terrain.

**Back:** *"Sensors see, radars track, people stop: CIBMS is eyes, not hands."*

### Flashcard IS-154 — Integrated Check Posts (ICPs) and the Land Ports Authority of India (LPAI)

**Front:** What are ICPs and what does LPAI oversee?

**Definition (learn):** ICPs consolidate customs, immigration and regulatory facilities at designated land crossings; LPAI is the statutory body overseeing their construction, management and maintenance.

**Breakdown:**
- LPAI envisaged "on the lines of" the Airports Authority of India (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 29).
- Cabinet proposal for 13 new ICPs for SAARC, Thailand and Myanmar engagement (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 25).
- ⚠️ Not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-146, IS-096, IS-155.

**Trap:** Treating ICPs as security posts; they are trade and customs facilities.

**Mains hook:** Use in trade-and-security answers. Qualified conclusion: ICPs turn regulated trade into a legal alternative to informal crossing.

**Back:** *"Thirteen new check posts, one authority: the ports of a land border."*

### Flashcard IS-155 — Border Out Posts (BOPs)

**Front:** What are BOPs?

**Definition (learn):** Designated points on the international border through which cross-border movement takes place, provided with accommodation, logistics and combat infrastructure to show force against infiltrators and criminals while facilitating trade.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 29).
- Dual function: deterrence and trade (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-154, IS-148.

**Trap:** Treating BOP density as proof of sealed border.

**Mains hook:** Use in border-presence answers. Qualified conclusion: BOPs build presence; their value depends on patrols and cooperation.

**Back:** *"A post shows the flag and opens the trade: the Border Out Post is both the wall and the gate."*

### Flashcard IS-156 — Madhukar Gupta Committee: grid border protection vs linear security

**Front:** What did the Madhukar Gupta Committee recommend?

**Definition (learn):** Replacing "linear security" with "grid border protection" along the India-Pakistan border, and better coordination among BGF, police and intelligence.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 4 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 38).
- Grid implies a sensor and intelligence networked model (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 2).
- ⚠️ Committee year not fetched.

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-153, IS-159, IS-148.

**Trap:** Treating the grid as only a technology upgrade. Owner says it is a paradigm shift.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — way forward for the Pakistan border. Qualified conclusion: grid protection is more realistic on riverine and hilly stretches than a continuous fence.

**Back:** *"From a line to a net: the grid catches what the fence cannot."*

### Flashcard IS-157 — Border Roads Organisation, CPWD and State PWDs

**Front:** Which agencies build border roads?

**Definition (learn):** The Border Roads Organisation, CPWD and State PWDs execute phase-wise border road construction to address poor connectivity.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 47).
- Connectivity is a security and development input (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-152, IS-138.

**Trap:** Treating BRO and BIM as the same. BRO is an agency; BIM is a scheme.

**Mains hook:** Use in border infrastructure answers.

**Back:** *"BRO lays the road, the scheme pays for it, the State finishes the last mile."*

### Flashcard IS-158 — FMR: regulation vs community trade-off (2024 decision)

**Front:** What is the trade-off in the 2024 FMR scrapping decision?

**Definition (learn):** The decision reduces one channel of insurgent and narcotics misuse, but disrupts legitimate cross-border kinship and small-scale trade.

**Breakdown:**
- Decision announced 8 February 2024 (`✅ owner 06`; ⚠️ MHA page not fetched).
- Regulation gain vs livelihood cost (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 5).
- Announcement, formal notification and field implementation must not be collapsed (`✅ owner 06` Section 12).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 8 February 2024.

**Links:** IS-096, IS-146, IS-170 (community tools).

**Trap:** Presenting the decision as a costless security measure.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Border regulation and community livelihood." Qualified conclusion: trade-offs need risk-based legal mobility, not blanket bans alone.

**Back:** *"One channel closed, many kinship ties cut: the trade-off is the answer."*

### Flashcard IS-159 — Fencing is not sealing

**Front:** Why is fencing not the same as sealing a border?

**Definition (learn):** Fencing, floodlighting, patrol roads, BOP density and technology are separate, separately funded components. Riverine, hilly and habitation-adjacent stretches often cannot be fenced.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 7 (`✅ owner 06`).
- Grid rather than linear model for such stretches (IS-156).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-156, IS-153, IS-155.

**Trap:** Saying "fenced" means sealed.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on border sealing. Qualified conclusion: sealing requires components beyond fencing and an honest geography assessment.

**Back:** *"A fence is one stitch; sealing needs the patrol, the light, the sensor and the village."*

### Flashcard IS-160 — Three border categories: disputed, settled-by-instrument, porous

**Front:** Why does owner 06 add a third category to the disputed/porous binary?

**Definition (learn):** Disputed and undemarcated (China), settled by instrument (Bangladesh, through the 2014 award and 2015 LBA), and settled but porous (Pakistan and Bangladesh in regulation terms).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Advanced Section 2 (`✅ owner 06`).
- The Bangladesh case shows adjudication and constitutional amendment can close a boundary (`✅ owner 06`).
- Persistence of the China question is political will and asymmetry (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-138, IS-142, IS-143, IS-137.

**Trap:** Treating every boundary as the same problem.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Different borders demand different strategies." Qualified conclusion: the category decides the instrument.

**Back:** *"Dispute, settle, or porous: three categories, three tools."*

### Flashcard IS-161 — 2026 GS-III Q10: Ladakh, BADP, Operation Sadbhavana and Sixth Schedule demand

**Front:** What does the 2026 GS-III Q10 ask, and what is the answer spine?

**Definition (learn):** 2026 GS-III Q10 (10 marks, 150 words): discuss BADP and Army civic action as hearts-and-minds measures in Ladakh, and the demand for the Sixth Schedule for Ladakh, given Ladakh's position between China and Pakistan.

**Breakdown:**
- Provenance: OCR of question paper; supporting facts from a Vajiram and Ravi extract (July 2026); ⚠️ not fetched (owner-stated).
- Answer spine: strategic context; BADP; Army civic action (Operation Sadbhavana); Sixth Schedule demand (`✅ owner 06` Section 14).
- Ladakh fronts Pakistan (Kargil and AGPL) and China (Aksai Chin) (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2026 (paper).

**Links:** IS-162, IS-163, IS-151, IS-164.

**Trap:** Treating BADP and Operation Sadbhavana as the same programme. Owner says name both.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on Ladakh. Qualified conclusion: civic goodwill is a security priority, but it does not confer constitutional autonomy.

**Back:** *"Two hands for the hearts: the civil scheme and the Army's goodwill, and a Sixth Schedule still asked for."*

### Flashcard IS-162 — Operation Sadbhavana (Army civic action in Ladakh)

**Front:** What is Operation Sadbhavana and what does it do?

**Definition (learn):** An Indian Army civic-action programme in Ladakh offering goodwill schools, skill training, medical camps and small infrastructure.

**Breakdown:**
- About seven Army Goodwill Schools serving 2,200+ students; skill programmes; medical camps; self-defence training; small infrastructure (`✅ owner 06` Section 14; ⚠️ not fetched; some items reported 2026).
- Approximately ₹8.82 crore allocated in 2022-23 (`✅ owner 06`; ⚠️ dated figure; verify current allocation).
- Army-run, distinct from civilian BADP (`✅ owner 06` Section 14).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022-23 allocation; 2026 activities (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-161, IS-151, IS-164.

**Trap:** Treating it as a civil scheme. Also using the 2022-23 figure as current.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — civic action. Qualified conclusion: civic action builds trust; it is not a substitute for administrative presence.

**Back:** *"Goodwill schools, medical camps, looms and wells: the Army's civic hand in the cold."*

### Flashcard IS-163 — Sixth Schedule ADC vs Ladakh LAHDC (Autonomous Hill Development Councils)

**Front:** How do Sixth Schedule Autonomous District Councils differ from Ladakh's Hill Development Councils?

**Definition (learn):** Sixth Schedule councils are constitutionally protected, with legislative, judicial and financial powers. LAHDCs are statutory, created under the LAHDC Act, 1997, with administrative and developmental powers under UT control.

**Breakdown:**
- Sixth Schedule under Articles 244(2) and 275(1) (`✅ owner 06`; `✅ Wikipedia: Sixth Schedule` for the four States and council types).
- LAHDC Act, 1997 applied from 1 June 1995 (`✅ owner 06`; ⚠️ not fetched).
- July 2026 proposal: extend AHDCs under Section 3(1) LAHDC Act to all seven Ladakh districts (`✅ owner 06`; 📰 owner-stated, ⚠️ not fetched).
- Demand: Sixth Schedule status (land, demography, culture) (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1995; 1997; July 2026 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-078, IS-161, IS-162.

**Trap:** Saying Ladakh already has Sixth Schedule status through its Hill Councils. Owner 06 says false.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on Ladakh autonomy demand. Qualified conclusion: an extension of a statutory council is not Sixth Schedule status.

**Back:** *"Sixth Schedule is in the Constitution; Ladakh's hill councils are in an Act: upgrade asked, extension given."*

### Flashcard IS-164 — Vibrant Villages Programme (border blocks)

**Front:** What does the Vibrant Villages Programme cover?

**Definition (learn):** A programme introduced in the 2022 Budget covering border blocks in Arunachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand, Himachal Pradesh and Ladakh, combining infrastructure and livelihood.

**Breakdown:**
- Introduced in 2022 Budget (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 4, VisionIAS PDF p. 29).
- Separate from BADP (`✅ owner 06` Section 3.6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022 Budget.

**Links:** IS-151, IS-098, IS-161.

**Trap:** Confusing it with BADP.

**Mains hook:** Use as institutional convergence of development and guarding. Qualified conclusion: convergence needs sequencing.

**Back:** *"Five border states, one village ladder: development as the border's second line."*

### Flashcard IS-165 — DS-DBO road (2019): strategic road and travel time

**Front:** What does the DS-DBO road illustrate?

**Definition (learn):** The Daulat Beg Oldie-Shyok-Darbuk road, completed in 2019, cited as cutting travel time by 40% on a western-sector stretch.

**Breakdown:**
- Completed 2019 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 9).
- 40% travel time reduction (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2019.

**Links:** IS-157, IS-149, IS-158.

**Trap:** Treating the road as a civilian connectivity project. It is strategic.

**Mains hook:** Use in infrastructure-reform answers. Qualified conclusion: infrastructure is reform in practice, not only on paper.

**Back:** *"Two-nineteen road, forty per cent shorter: the strategic road is a troop-moving promise kept."*

### Flashcard IS-166 — 2020 GS-III route: Myanmar, Bangladesh and LoC comparison

**Front:** What is the answer route for the 2020 border question?

**Definition (learn):** 2020 GS-III (15 marks): security challenges along Myanmar and Bangladesh borders and the Pakistan Line of Control.

**Breakdown:**
- Three-column structure: Myanmar, Bangladesh, LoC (`✅ owner 06` Section 12).
- One neighbour-specific capability and one community safeguard per column (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2020.

**Links:** IS-146, IS-141, IS-136.

**Trap:** Merging the three into a generic porous-borders paragraph.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — the three-border comparison.

**Back:** *"Myanmar's terrain, Bangladesh's crowds, the LoC's line: three columns, not one porous wall."*

### Flashcard IS-167 — 2023 GS-III route: adversarial UAVs

**Front:** How should a hostile UAV be analysed in the 2023 question?

**Definition (learn):** A hostile UAV is a delivery vector, not a threat category. Payloads (surveillance, weapons or contraband) → vulnerabilities → capabilities → limitation.

**Breakdown:**
- 2023 GS-III: adversarial UAV threats across Indian borders (10 marks) (`✅ owner 06`, PYQ integration).
- Capabilities: layered radar and electro-optical detection, lawful counter-UAS, intelligence fusion, forensic chain (`✅ owner 06` Section 12).
- Do not invent incidents or interception counts (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2023.

**Links:** IS-153, IS-045 (UN technology), IS-044 (PRAHAAR drones).

**Trap:** Stating a trial as an achieved interception outcome.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks on drones. Qualified conclusion: detection and lawful response are capabilities; their outcomes need dated evidence.

**Back:** *"Payload, gap, sensor, verdict: the drone is a vector, not a villain."*

### Flashcard IS-168 — 2024 GS-III Q19 route: China and Pakistan border with BADP and BIM

**Front:** How should the 2024 Q19 be answered?

**Definition (learn):** 2024 GS-III Q19 (15 marks): examine conflicting issues and security challenges on the China and Pakistan borders, and describe BADP and BIM.

**Breakdown:**
- Separate China (disputed LAC) and Pakistan (segments) analyses (`✅ owner 06` Section 9).
- Contrast with Bangladesh settled instruments (`✅ owner 06`).
- BADP and BIM in separate boxes; cite the current MHA annexure for period and allocation (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** PYQ 2024 (`✅ owner 06`).

**Links:** IS-136, IS-138, IS-151, IS-152, IS-142.

**Trap:** One undifferentiated "China-Pakistan" paragraph.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — exact PYQ. Qualified conclusion: the borders differ; the schemes are inputs, the verdict is on delivery.

**Back:** *"China's line, Pakistan's segments, and two schemes boxed separately: Q19 rewards the split."*

### Flashcard IS-169 — BADP vs BIM distinction

**Front:** How do BADP and BIM differ?

**Definition (learn):** BADP is a village-level development programme (livelihood, health, education) for villages within 0-10 km. BIM is an infrastructure scheme (roads, electricity, communication). Both sit under the Department of Border Management.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 7 trap (`✅ owner 06`).
- BADP uses non-lapsable Special Central Assistance (IS-151).
- BIM period 2021-22 to 2025-26 per VisionIAS (IS-152; ⚠️ verify).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-151, IS-152, IS-150.

**Trap:** Merging them.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Distinguish BADP from BIM." Qualified conclusion: village welfare and infrastructure are different instruments with one ministry.

**Back:** *"BADP for the village, BIM for the road: two schemes, one department."*

### Flashcard IS-170 — Community-participative border management

**Front:** What community instruments does VisionIAS name for border management?

**Definition (learn):** Community-development by border guarding forces (Operation Sadbhavana-style), village defence or volunteer forces, and enhanced border trade, framed as reducing the pull toward illegal activity.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Advanced Section 2 (`✅ owner 06`, VisionIAS PDF p. 38).
- VDG as one instrument (IS-119).
- Cannot resolve disputed demarcation (`✅ owner 06` Section 6).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-162, IS-119, IS-158.

**Trap:** Treating community tools as dispute-resolution tools.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — community as a force multiplier. Qualified conclusion: community tools reduce pull; they do not settle a dispute.

**Back:** *"Livelihood, trade and volunteers: the community is the border's second guard."*

### Flashcard IS-171 — Unfinished one-force-one-border on the China border (Vikas Battalions and ITBP)

**Front:** Why does the one-force-one-border principle remain partially implemented on the China border?

**Definition (learn):** Vikas Battalions reporting to the Cabinet Secretariat in some sectors operate alongside ITBP reporting to MHA, making coordination difficult.

**Breakdown:**
- VisionIAS PDF pp. 4-5 (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 2).
- Persistent two decades after the Kargil recommendation (`✅ owner 06` Advanced Section 3).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner-stated.

**Links:** IS-148, IS-149, IS-138.

**Trap:** Saying one-force-one-border is complete.

**Mains hook:** 15 marks — "Critically examine why one force, one border remains incomplete." Qualified conclusion: reform is difficult to finish, not just slow to start.

**Back:** *"Two commands on one line: the Cabinet's battalions and the Ministry's ITBP make coordination the border's weak seam."*

### Flashcard IS-172 — Border management thesis (advanced Mains framework)

**Front:** What is the advanced central thesis for border management?

**Definition (learn):** Reform since Kargil diversified from pure guarding to guarding, regulation, development and bilateral mechanisms; its two hardest unresolved problems are one-force-one-border consolidation and durable China dispute resolution. Galwan, Tawang and the FMR decision show incomplete implementation.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Advanced Section 11 (`✅ owner 06`).
- Border-type-specific sequenced reform (`✅ owner 06`).
- Bangladesh shows the agenda is achievable (`✅ owner 06` Section 4).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-134, IS-139, IS-158, IS-171.

**Trap:** Reading incidents as architectural failure.

**Mains hook:** 20 marks — comparison of border types. Qualified conclusion: incomplete implementation is the diagnosis; border-type calibration is the remedy.

**Back:** *"Incomplete, not wrong: the architecture stands; the second command and the China line still wait."*

### Flashcard IS-173 — Border management as a convergence and scheme-overlap risk

**Front:** Why does scheme proliferation create a risk in border management?

**Definition (learn):** BADP, BIM, CIBMS, Vibrant Villages and others can overlap unless sequenced under the Department of Border Management's convergence mandate.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Advanced Section 6 (`✅ owner 06`).
- Convergence is a management task (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner synthesis.

**Links:** IS-150, IS-151, IS-152, IS-153, IS-164.

**Trap:** Counting schemes as coordinated outcomes.

**Mains hook:** 10 marks — "Schemes proliferate, outcomes lag." Qualified conclusion: convergence and sequencing are the test.

**Back:** *"Five schemes, one line: convergence is the discipline that stops them overlapping."*

### Flashcard IS-174 — MHA Border Management-II annexure (8 January 2026) as current source

**Front:** What is the current source for BIM, CIBMS and coastal-security status?

**Definition (learn):** The MHA Border Management-II annexure, dated 8 January 2026, is the owner's current anchor for scheme status, allocation and coverage.

**Breakdown:**
- Owner 06 Section 8 (`✅ owner 06`; 📰 owner-stated; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Use it rather than book or VisionIAS period figures (`✅ owner 06`).

**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 8 January 2026 (owner-stated).

**Links:** IS-152, IS-153, IS-169.

**Trap:** Quoting book-era BIM figures as current.

**Mains hook:** Use in any current-scheme answer.

**Back:** *"Dated January, the annexure decides the scheme's present tense."*

## INTERNAL SECURITY — Maritime, Cyber, Information Warfare, Terror Finance, Organised Crime, Forces

### GROUP — 07 Maritime and Coastal Security (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard IS-201 — Maritime security vs coastal security (concept)
**Front:** How do maritime security and coastal security differ, and which is the subset?
**Definition (learn):** Coastal security is a *subset* of maritime security. Coastal security guards the coastline and its territorial waters and maritime approaches against threats originating from the sea; maritime security is the broader domain covering the EEZ, the high seas and sea lanes of communication (SLOCs).
**Breakdown:**
- Coastal = coastline + territorial waters + near-shore approaches (Coast Guard and State Marine Police led).
- Maritime = EEZ + high seas + SLOCs (Navy led).
- Vulnerability profile: creeks, bays, backwaters, lagoons, estuaries, mudflats, small inhabited/uninhabited islands; no physical barrier; vital industrial and defence installations near the coast; volatile neighbours nearby.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 07 basic §1-§2 (✅ owner 07; source cited there: VisionIAS, *Security Challenges and Their Management in Border Areas*, PDF p. 31).
**Links:** IS-202 (zone ladder), IS-210 (three-tier architecture), IS-241 (layered design vs threat matrix).
**Trap:** Examiners treat "maritime" and "coastal" as non-interchangeable. Answering "maritime security = coastal security" loses the marks for jurisdiction.
**Mains hook:** Any "maritime and coastal security challenges" question (GS-III 2022, 2025) — open by drawing the subset relationship, then assign each threat to a zone. Conclusion: the distinction is operational, not semantic; the zone decides the lead agency.
**Back:** *"Coast is the Corner, Maritime the Whole Room."*

### Flashcard IS-202 — Maritime-zone ladder (12 / 24 / 200 nm / high seas)
**Front:** State India's maritime-zone ladder and the agency/law changes at each line.
**Definition (learn):** Under the Maritime Zones Act, 1976 (consistent with UNCLOS): territorial sea up to 12 nautical miles (full sovereignty); contiguous zone up to 24 nm; Exclusive Economic Zone (EEZ) up to 200 nm; high seas beyond.
**Breakdown:**
- 0-12 nm: territorial sea — full sovereignty; State Marine Police jurisdiction ends at 12 nm.
- 12-24 nm: contiguous zone — customs, fiscal, immigration and sanitary enforcement.
- 12-200 nm: EEZ — resource rights; Navy/Coast Guard surveillance to the EEZ limit.
- Beyond 200 nm: high seas — Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 applies here (including the EEZ).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Maritime Zones Act, 1976; UNCLOS (✅ owner 07 basic §2, §6; VisionIAS PDF p. 31-37). ✅ Verified in the owner ladder; statute text not fetched (⚠️ re-check against India Code before quoting section numbers).
**Links:** IS-203 (ICG), IS-208 (Marine Police), IS-221 (Piracy Act, high seas), IS-223 (coastline).
**Trap:** Piracy jurisdiction ≠ territorial jurisdiction. The EEZ is inside the piracy Act's reach but not the 12 nm sovereign zone. Do not state "EEZ = 12 nm" or "contiguous zone = territorial waters."
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to show that the lead agency and governing law change at each boundary (zone decides response). Qualify: a clean diagram does not remove operational overlap in a real incident.
**Back:** *"T-C-E-H: Territory 12, Contiguous 24, Economic 200, then High seas — The Coast Earns Heavily."*

### Flashcard IS-203 — Indian Coast Guard (ICG)
**Front:** When was the Indian Coast Guard established, under which Ministry, and what is its legal basis?
**Definition (learn):** ICG is the maritime law enforcement and search-and-rescue agency protecting India's maritime zones and national maritime interests; formally constituted under the Coast Guard Act, 1978 and operates under the Ministry of Defence.
**Breakdown:**
- Interim force: February 1977 (1 February 1977 per owner 07).
- Formal establishment: 18 August 1978 under the Coast Guard Act, 1978.
- Owner 07 describes it as the "fourth armed force of India." ⚠️ Wikipedia (fetched) confirms the Act and MoD but does not use the "fourth armed force" phrase in the visible text; cite the phrase only as the owner's wording.
- Mandate: territorial waters with vessels and aerial surveillance; coordination in the three-tier design.
- Helpline 1554 (owner 07 adv §4).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Coast Guard Act, 1978; Ministry of Defence; established 1 Feb 1977 (owner 07 basic §2; VisionIAS PDF p. 33). ✅ Wikipedia "Indian Coast Guard": interim Feb 1977, formal Aug 1978, Coast Guard Act 1978, operates under MoD; 42 Coast Guard stations (fetched).
**Links:** IS-204 (Act), IS-210 (three-tier), IS-213 (NCSMCS), IS-232 (single-authority question), IS-237 (helplines).
**Trap:** The ICG is not the Navy and not a State police force. Its Act (1978) is later than its interim formation (1977), so "established by Act of 1977" is wrong. Examiners use the 1 February 1977 / 1978 pair as a close-option distinction.
**Mains hook:** In "way forward" answers, the ICG is the candidate single coastal authority (owner 07 adv §2); qualify that the Coast Guard Act has "ambiguities" the owner says must be removed.
**Back:** *"Seventy-Seven Started, Seventy-Eight Sealed — 1977 interim, 1978 Act."*

### Flashcard IS-204 — Coast Guard Act, 1978
**Front:** What does the Coast Guard Act, 1978 do, and why does it matter for command clarity?
**Definition (learn):** The Act constituted the Indian Coast Guard as a statutory armed force under the Ministry of Defence, with duties for maritime-zone security and protection of maritime/national interests.
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory basis for the ICG (formal constitution, 18 August 1978).
- Owner 07 adv §2 and §6: the Act contains "ambiguities" that the VisionIAS way-forward section says must be removed so the Coast Guard can be the single coastal-security authority.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Coast Guard Act, 1978 (✅ owner 07 basic §2; Wikipedia fetched 2026).
**Links:** IS-203, IS-232, IS-210.
**Trap:** The Act does not, by itself, make the ICG the single coordinating authority for coastal security; the "single authority" is a recommendation in the source, not an enacted position (owner 07 adv §8).
**Mains hook:** Use the Act as the legal starting point for the "command unification" debate; qualify with the JOC/NCSMCS coordination that already exists.
**Back:** *"Eight-Seven-Eight: the Act that made the Coast a Guard."*

### Flashcard IS-205 — Maritime terrorism (definition)
**Front:** Define maritime terrorism as used in coastal-security literature.
**Definition (learn):** "The undertaking of terrorist acts and activities within the maritime environment, using or against vessels or fixed platforms at sea or in port... against coastal facilities or settlements" (VisionIAS, quoted in owner 07 basic §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Targets: vessels, fixed platforms (at sea or in port), coastal facilities, settlements.
- Legal anchor: SUA Act, 2002 (IS-220).
- Historical exemplars: 1993 Mumbai blasts via Raigad coast (IS-224); 26/11 sea route (IS-225).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 31 (owner 07 basic §2, ✅ owner 07). ⚠️ Definition is the owner's paraphrase-quotation; not checked against a primary treaty text.
**Links:** IS-206 (piracy), IS-220 (SUA Act), IS-224, IS-225.
**Trap:** Maritime terrorism is political/terror-motivated violence; piracy is "private ends" violence on the high seas. The two are governed by different statutes (SUA 2002 vs Piracy Act 2022).
**Mains hook:** Cite maritime terrorism as the reason sea-borne threats need coastal and maritime layers. Conclude with prevention (intelligence, surveillance) plus response capacity (interdiction).
**Back:** *"Terror at sea hits ships, platforms and shores: SUA, not Piracy."*

### Flashcard IS-206 — Piracy (definition and high-seas limit)
**Front:** What is piracy and where does it legally occur?
**Definition (learn):** Piracy, by definition, occurs on the high seas; the Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 defines it per convention language as "any illegal act of violence or detention or any act of depredation committed for private ends... directed on the high seas against another ship."
**Breakdown:**
- High-seas violence for private ends = piracy.
- Sunderbans-type shallow-water violence in India is analogous but not technically piracy (owner 07 basic §2, §7).
- Armed robbery in territorial waters is handled by ordinary criminal law.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 (IS-221); VisionIAS PDF p. 31 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-205, IS-221, IS-234.
**Trap:** "Any maritime violence = piracy" is the standard close-option trap. Also: India had no specific piracy provision in IPC or CrPC before 2022.
**Mains hook:** A "jurisdictional" answer: separate piracy (high seas/EEZ, 2022 Act) from territorial armed robbery (ordinary law). Qualify that the offence and location determine the law.
**Back:** *"High seas, private ends, ship targeted: that is piracy; shallow water is not."*

### Flashcard IS-207 — Five maritime threat categories
**Front:** List the five broad categories of maritime/coastal threat.
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS's five categories (owner 07 basic §3.1): maritime terrorism; piracy/armed robbery; smuggling/trafficking; infiltration/illegal migration/refugee influx; fishermen straying across maritime boundaries.
**Breakdown:**
- M-P-S-I-F: Maritime terrorism; Piracy/armed robbery; Smuggling/trafficking; Infiltration/illegal migration; Fishermen straying.
- Owner's 15-mark route (adv §12) expands to five threats incl. illegal fishing and port/coastal cyber disruption.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 31-32 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-205, IS-206, IS-226, IS-240, topic 08 (port cyber), topic 11 (narcotic trafficking).
**Trap:** Owner 07 basic and the 15-mark spine list different sets (basic = 5 categories; adv/Core = 5 threats including port cyber). Quote the source category list when asked "five threats" and say the Core spine adds cyber for a Mains answer.
**Mains hook:** Map each threat to the zone and agency that responds (zone decides lead). Conclude with prevention → preparedness → response → recovery.
**Back:** *"Terror, Piracy, Smuggling, Infiltration, Fishermen: T-P-S-I-F."*

### Flashcard IS-208 — Customs Marine Organisation (1974)
**Front:** What was the 1974 Customs Marine Organisation and why does it mark the start of the reactive chronology?
**Definition (learn):** The 1974 Customs Marine Organisation (CMO) was an anti-smuggling marine body that began the institutional build-out after rising smuggling.
**Breakdown:**
- 1974 CMO (anti-smuggling).
- Followed by 1977 ICG; 2005 Coastal Security Scheme; post-26/11 NC3I/JOCs; 2017 proposed Maritime Theatre Command; 2020 national maritime security coordinator (owner 07 basic §3.2).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** CMO 1974 (VisionIAS PDF p. 33, ✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-230 (reactive chronology), IS-203, IS-209.
**Trap:** The CMO is a Customs body, not the Coast Guard. Do not merge it with the 1977 ICG.
**Mains hook:** Use the CMO to show the architecture began with smuggling, not terror — a useful analytic point for "how threats changed".
**Back:** *"Seventy-Four Smugglers, Customs at sea."*

### Flashcard IS-209 — Coastal Security Scheme (2005) and Marine Police Force
**Front:** What is the Coastal Security Scheme and what did it create?
**Definition (learn):** The Coastal Security Scheme (CSS), implemented in phases by MHA's Department of Border Management, builds coastal police stations, check-posts, jetties, boats and surveillance for the Marine Police; it created the Marine Police Force in 2005-06 on a hub-and-spoke model with the ICG.
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 2005 (three-layered structure, owner 07 basic §3.2).
- Marine Police patrol territorial waters up to 12 nm.
- Hub-and-spoke model with ICG.
- Marine Police training institutes approved at Tamil Nadu and Gujarat (owner 07 adv §4 note).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Coastal Security Scheme (2005) — MHA DBM (VisionIAS PDF pp. 33-35, 47; ✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-210, IS-222 (Okha academy), IS-230.
**Trap:** The Coast Guard does not own the Marine Police; the State Marine Police is a State subject under this scheme. Do not state the Marine Police patrol the EEZ.
**Mains hook:** The CSS is the capacity-building answer for the close-coast layer; qualify that capacity gap (training, boats) persisted until 2026.
**Back:** *"Five-Oh-Five Scheme, Marine Police come alive within twelve."*

### Flashcard IS-210 — Three-tier coastal-security architecture
**Front:** Name the three operational tiers and their jurisdictions.
**Definition (learn):** Layered design: (1) Navy — EEZ and high seas patrol limits; (2) Coast Guard — territorial waters with vessels and aerial surveillance; (3) State Marine Police — close coastal patrolling within 12 nm.
**Breakdown:**
- Navy: EEZ/high seas.
- ICG: territorial waters.
- Marine Police: close coastal, up to 12 nm.
- Coordination layer above: NCSMCS (IS-213), JOCs (IS-212), NC3I (IS-211).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 35 (✅ owner 07 basic §3.3, §6).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-203, IS-208, IS-209, IS-211-IS-213, IS-232.
**Trap:** "The Navy is solely responsible for coastal security" is wrong. Each tier has a distinct role; the residual overlap is the structural weakness.
**Mains hook:** Use the three tiers to show layering, then show overlap as the reason for the "single authority" debate. Qualify: a simplified tier diagram does not remove operational overlap.
**Back:** *"Navy Outside, Coast Guard Middle, Marine Police Near Shore — N-C-M."*

### Flashcard IS-211 — NC3I (National Command, Control, Communication and Intelligence Network)
**Front:** What is NC3I and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** An over-arching coastal-security network that collates and disseminates vessel data to enhance maritime domain awareness.
**Breakdown:**
- Function: vessel data collation and dissemination.
- Built out in the post-26/11 multilayer expansion (owner 07 basic §3.2).
- Awareness asset, not interdiction asset (IS-231).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 35 (✅ owner 07); post-26/11 build-out.
**Links:** IS-212, IS-214, IS-231.
**Trap:** NC3I gives knowledge of what is at sea; it does not give boats, crew or boarding authority.
**Mains hook:** Cite NC3I as evidence of awareness-heavy investment; qualify with the need for interdiction capacity.
**Back:** *"NC3I sees the vessel; it does not board the vessel."*

### Flashcard IS-212 — Joint Operations Centres (JOCs), coastal security
**Front:** Where are the coastal JOCs and who mans them?
**Definition (learn):** Joint Operations Centres established after 26/11 at Mumbai, Visakhapatnam, Kochi and Port Blair, jointly manned 24x7 by the Navy, ICG and Marine Police.
**Breakdown:**
- Locations: Mumbai, Visakhapatnam, Kochi, Port Blair (4).
- Manning: Navy + ICG + Marine Police, 24x7.
- Clearest operational expression of the layered design (owner 07 adv §4).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Established after 2008 (post-26/11); VisionIAS PDF pp. 35-36 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-210, IS-211, IS-213, IS-225.
**Trap:** JOC count is four in the owner; do not add Chennai or Goa unless sourced. Close-option traps often swap Port Blair/Kochi.
**Mains hook:** Use JOCs as evidence of coordination in practice; qualify that JOCs solve coordination, not the single-authority question.
**Back:** *"Mumbai, Vizag, Kochi, Port Blair: four eyes on the coast, twenty-four hours."*

### Flashcard IS-213 — National Committee for Strengthening Maritime and Coastal Security (NCSMCS)
**Front:** What is the NCSMCS, who heads it, and how many agencies does it coordinate?
**Definition (learn):** The apex coordination body for maritime and coastal security, headed by the Cabinet Secretary, coordinating nearly 15 national and state agencies.
**Breakdown:**
- Head: Cabinet Secretary (owner 07 basic §4).
- Coordinates: nearly 15 agencies.
- Sits above the three operational tiers.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 35 (✅ owner 07 basic §4, adv §4).
**Links:** IS-210, IS-212, IS-232, IS-236 (national maritime security coordinator, 2020).
**Trap:** NCSMCS is a coordination committee, not an operational command. Do not describe it as a commander of the Navy or ICG. Its "headed by Cabinet Secretary" detail is a stock PYQ distractor.
**Mains hook:** Use NCSMCS to show coordination exists; qualify that the still-open question is command clarity, not the existence of a committee.
**Back:** *"Cabinet Secretary chairs; fifteen agencies sit: NCSMCS coordinates, it does not command."*

### Flashcard IS-214 — Coastal surveillance stack (CSN, AIS, VTMS)
**Front:** What are the main coastal-surveillance and vessel-tracking technologies?
**Definition (learn):** The Coastal Surveillance Network combines a static coastal-radar chain, Automatic Identification System (AIS) receivers, long-range tracking and day-night cameras; Vessel Traffic Management System (VTMS) radars are installed at major and minor ports.
**Breakdown:**
- CSN: coastal radar chain + AIS + long-range tracking + day/night cameras.
- AIS: automatic vessel identification (compulsory on larger vessels; owner 07 adv §4).
- VTMS: radars at major and minor ports.
- Fishing-craft RFID proposal and Distress Alert Transmitters (owner 07 adv §4).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 35-36 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-211, IS-231, IS-237.
**Trap:** Technology gives awareness, not interdiction; the technology depends on compliance, and small informal craft are hardest to track (adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Qualify technology-heavy solutions with community and human intelligence (fishermen as "ears and eyes").
**Back:** *"Radar sees, AIS names, VTMS guides the port."*

### Flashcard IS-215 — Integrated Underwater Harbour Defence and Surveillance System (IUHDSS)
**Front:** What is IUHDSS and where is it installed?
**Definition (learn):** The Navy's Integrated Underwater Harbour Defence and Surveillance System, installed at major naval harbours to detect underwater threats.
**Breakdown:**
- Installed by the Indian Navy at major naval harbours (owner 07 basic §3.6).
- Protects harbour approaches from underwater intrusion.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 35-36 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-216 (Sagar Prahari Bal), IS-217 (Sagar Kavach).
**Trap:** IUHDSS is a Navy harbour installation, not a State Marine Police system.
**Mains hook:** Cite in a "layered sensors" answer; qualify that harbour defence does not replace coastal patrol.
**Back:** *"Underwater eyes guard the naval harbour."*

### Flashcard IS-216 — Sagar Prahari Bal (2009)
**Front:** What is the Sagar Prahari Bal, when was it raised, and what does it protect?
**Definition (learn):** A Navy-raised force set up in 2009 to protect naval bases and adjacent vulnerable areas/points.
**Breakdown:**
- Raised: 2009 by the Indian Navy.
- Role: naval bases + adjacent vulnerable areas and points.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 35-36 (✅ owner 07 basic §3.6, §6).
**Links:** IS-215, IS-217, IS-225.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Sagar Prahari Bal (2009, Navy) with Operation Sagar Kavach (exercise, post-26/11).
**Mains hook:** Use as an example of a post-26/11 Navy response; qualify with the still-open single-authority debate.
**Back:** *"Two-Thousand-Nine, Sagar Prahari guards the naval base."*

### Flashcard IS-217 — Operation Sagar Kavach
**Front:** What is Operation Sagar Kavach and what does it test?
**Definition (learn):** An inter-agency coastal-security exercise (post-26/11) that tests coordination among the Navy, ICG, Marine Police and other agencies.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: exercise, not a permanent force.
- Tests: inter-agency coordination, JOC-level response.
- Owner 07 cites it as the post-26/11 exercise (basic §6).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 35-36 (✅ owner 07). ⚠️ Launch year not stated in owner; do not add a year without a dated source.
**Links:** IS-212, IS-216, IS-213.
**Trap:** An exercise tests coordination; it does not establish command authority.
**Mains hook:** Use as evidence of coordination testing; qualify that exercises do not replace command clarity.
**Back:** *"Sagar Kavach drills the shield, not the command chain."*

### Flashcard IS-218 — Information Fusion Centre-Indian Ocean Region (IFC-IOR)
**Front:** What is the IFC-IOR, where is it hosted, and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** A Navy-hosted information-sharing centre at Gurugram (since 2018) that shares vessel-of-interest information with International Liaison Officers from partner nations.
**Breakdown:**
- Host: Indian Navy, Gurugram; operational since 2018.
- Liaison officers from partner nations (12 per owner adv §4).
- Its diplomatic dimension belongs to IR; this topic treats its MDA function.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 36 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** IS-231, IS-232 (command), IS-219 (IMSS).
**Trap:** Do not present IFC-IOR as an internal-security body. IONS (Indian Ocean Naval Symposium) is a regional navy platform, not an internal agency (adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Use to show India's MDA extends beyond its own agencies; qualify that it generates awareness only.
**Back:** *"Gurugram gathers the Ocean's gossip — awareness, not action."*

### Flashcard IS-219 — Indian Maritime Security Strategy (IMSS), 2015
**Front:** What is the IMSS and what does it envisage?
**Definition (learn):** The Navy's strategy document (2015) envisaging coordination, sea-lane (SLOC) security, and multilateral engagement.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2015.
- Pillars named in owner: coordination, SLOC security, multilateral engagement.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 35 (✅ owner 07). The 2015 date is owner-given; ⚠️ not fetched from the primary strategy text.
**Links:** IS-218, IS-232 (resource conflict).
**Trap:** IMSS is a Navy doctrine, not a legal instrument; do not cite it as a statute.
**Mains hook:** Use to show Navy's blue-water emphasis; qualify with the resource argument that Navy assets are finite.
**Back:** *"Fifteen strategy: Coordinate, Protect lanes, Partner."*

### Flashcard IS-220 — SUA Act, 2002 (maritime navigation and fixed platforms)
**Front:** What does the SUA Act, 2002 cover and what treaty does it implement?
**Definition (learn):** The Suppression of Unlawful Acts against Safety of Maritime Navigation and Fixed Platforms on Continental Shelf Act, 2002, implements India's obligations under the SUA Convention and covers ship hijacking and violence against vessels and offshore platforms.
**Breakdown:**
- Long title: Suppression of Unlawful Acts against Safety of Maritime Navigation and Fixed Platforms on Continental Shelf Act, 2002.
- Covers: hijacking of ships; violence against vessels and offshore platforms.
- Predates the 2022 Piracy Act and complements it.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** SUA Act, 2002 (✅ owner 07 basic §2, cited as India Code). ⚠️ SUA Convention year (1988) is from memory; not fetched.
**Links:** IS-205, IS-221, IS-202.
**Trap:** The SUA Act is a maritime-terrorism statute (terror/unlawful acts), not a piracy statute. "Piracy law" = 2022 Act.
**Mains hook:** Use as the legal layer for maritime terrorism; qualify that the piracy and SUA statutes have different triggers.
**Back:** *"SUA is ship-and-platform sabotage; Piracy Act is the private-ends sea robbery."*

### Flashcard IS-221 — Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022
**Front:** What does the Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 do and why was it needed?
**Definition (learn):** A 2022 statute defining piracy per international-convention language, applying it to the high seas including the EEZ, providing Designated Courts, extradition to treaty partners, arrest/seizure only by authorised personnel and court-ordered disposal of seized property.
**Breakdown:**
- Definition: "any illegal act of violence or detention or any act of depredation committed for private ends... directed on the high seas against another ship."
- Scope: high seas including EEZ.
- Designated Courts: specified in consultation with the concerned High Court's Chief Justice.
- Extradition to treaty partners; authorised-personnel arrest; disposal of seized property by court order.
- Gap filled: India previously had no specific piracy provision in IPC or CrPC.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 (✅ owner 07 basic §2, §5; VisionIAS PDF p. 37).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-206, IS-220, IS-234.
**Trap:** "India always had a dedicated anti-piracy law" is wrong (enacted 2022). Territorial-water violence is not covered by the 2022 Act (adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Cite as evidence of a still-developing legal architecture; qualify with the three-statute design (Zones Act 1976, SUA 2002, Piracy Act 2022).
**Back:** *"Twenty-Twenty-Two: high seas, private ends, designated courts."*

### Flashcard IS-222 — National Academy of Coastal Policing, Okha (7 July 2026)
**Front:** What is the National Academy of Coastal Policing, when was it established, and what does it solve?
**Definition (learn):** A subordinate MHA office at Okha, established by official order dated 7 July 2026, providing specialised training to marine police personnel from nine coastal States, five Union Territories and central forces (BSF, CISF).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: order dated 7 July 2026 (owner 07 basic §4, §8 — 📰 current anchor).
- Trainees: nine coastal States; five UTs; central forces (BSF, CISF).
- Solves: marine-police training-capacity gap.
- Does NOT solve: single-authority / command-unification question.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** MHA order, 7 July 2026 (📰 owner 07; ⚠️ not independently fetched; re-verify with the MHA order before citing in an exam).
**Links:** IS-209, IS-232, IS-238, IS-239.
**Trap:** A 2026 training academy is not the answer to command clarity. Do not state that it resolves the Coast Guard/Navy/Marine Police jurisdiction question (adv §8).
**Mains hook:** The 2025 "way forward" can cite it; qualify that it is capacity, not command.
**Back:** *"Okha Trains the Marine Police; Command remains the open question."*

### Flashcard IS-223 — India's coastline and coastal States (7,516.6 km)
**Front:** What is India's coastline length including island territories, and how many coastal States are there?
**Definition (learn):** India's coastline is 7,516.6 km including island territories, spanning nine coastal States and coastal UTs.
**Breakdown:**
- Length: 7,516.6 km including island territories (owner 07 basic §3, §6; VisionIAS PDF pp. 4, 31).
- Nine coastal States: Gujarat, Maharashtra, Goa, Karnataka, Kerala, Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Odisha, West Bengal.
- Coastal UTs also included.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 4, 31 (✅ owner 07). ⚠️ Length is a dated source figure, not verified against a 2026 official release.
**Links:** IS-202, IS-209, IS-210.
**Trap:** Nine States is the count in the owner; do not add UTs to the State count. Coastline length must come with "including island territories".
**Mains hook:** Use coastline and State count to show scale of the task; qualify that scale drives the need for layered patrol.
**Back:** *"Nine States, Seven Thousand Five Hundred and Sixteen point Six km of shore."*

### Flashcard IS-224 — 1993 Bombay (Mumbai) serial blasts — coastal lesson
**Front:** What was the 1993 Mumbai blasts' sea-route link and why is it cited in coastal security?
**Definition (learn):** The 12 March 1993 serial blasts in Mumbai; RDX and arms were landed via the Raigad coast (Shekhadi), exposing coastal vulnerability.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 12 March 1993 (Wikipedia fetched, ✅ verified).
- Toll: 257 dead, about 1,400 injured (Wikipedia fetched; some sources differ).
- Smuggling: nearly 3,000 kg RDX landed at Shekhadi, Raigad, in February 1993 (Wikipedia fetched).
- Raigad customs and police officials were convicted of taking bribes (Wikipedia fetched).
- Trials: 2006 special (TADA) court tried 129 accused, 100 convicted; 2013 SC upheld Yakub Memon's death sentence; hanged 30 July 2015 (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1993 blasts (✅ owner 07 basic §5; VisionIAS PDF p. 31; ✅ Wikipedia "1993 Bombay bombings" fetched).
**Links:** IS-205, IS-208 (1974 CMO), IS-230 (reactive chronology), IS-225.
