**Trap:** Owner and Wikipedia both place landing at Raigad (Shekhadi); "Dighi" is not in the fetched text, so do not add it. Death toll varies by source (257 vs 317 with the Calcutta bombing).
**Mains hook:** Use the 1993 episode to show smuggling-to-terror linkage on the coast; qualify that the bribery angle is an insider-vulnerability lesson, not only a sea-patrol gap.
**Back:** *"Twelve March, Raigad's sand, three thousand kilos of RDX in hand."*

### Flashcard IS-225 — 26/11 (26 November 2008) sea-route attack
**Front:** How did the 26/11 attackers reach Mumbai and what did it change in coastal security?
**Definition (learn):** Ten Lashkar-e-Taiba attackers left Karachi on a Pakistani cargo vessel, hijacked the Indian trawler Kuber, and came ashore at Colaba in inflatable boats; the attacks ran from 26 to 29 November 2008.
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 26-29 November 2008.
- Attackers: ten (LeT per Wikipedia fetched).
- Route: Karachi → cargo vessel → trawler Kuber (four crew killed) → inflatable boats → Colaba.
- Toll: 175 dead (166 civilians, nine attackers); over 300 injured (Wikipedia fetched).
- Institutional change: JOCs (IS-212), NC3I, NCSMCS, national maritime security coordinator (2020).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 31, 35-36 (✅ owner 07); ✅ Wikipedia "2008 Mumbai attacks" fetched (toll and route).
**Links:** IS-212, IS-211, IS-213, IS-236, topic 02 (Terrorism).
**Trap:** "The mind-set that coastal security is not essential" changed only after 26/11 (owner 07 adv §2). Do not date the JOCs to 2005; they followed the 2008 attacks.
**Mains hook:** Use 26/11 as the reactive-institutionalisation exemplar (IS-230). Qualify: the sea-route vulnerability was known before 2008.
**Back:** *"Karachi to Colaba, a trawler hijacked, ten men, and a coast that woke up."*

### Flashcard IS-226 — Palk Bay / Sri Lanka fishermen dispute (settled boundary, contested practice)
**Front:** Why is the Palk Bay fishermen dispute a settled-boundary problem and not an unsettled one?
**Definition (learn):** The dispute concerns Indian fishermen straying across a settled maritime boundary (in VisionIAS's framing, refusal to recognise it) rather than a boundary yet to be delimited.
**Breakdown:**
- Settled: Palk Strait boundary (1974 agreement; 1976 agreement barred each side's fishermen from the other's EEZ).
- Practice: recurrent arrests and straying.
- Internal Security owns the coastal response (livelihood transition, deep-sea fishing shift); IR owns bilateral protest (adv §5).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 26 (✅ owner 07 adv §5); ✅ Wikipedia "Katchatheevu" fetched (1974 Indo-Sri Lankan maritime agreement; 1976 agreement).
**Links:** IS-227, IS-207, IS-233.
**Trap:** "Settled boundary, unsettled domestic politics" is a distinct category from "unsettled boundary" (adv §5). Examiners test this as the close-option distinction.
**Mains hook:** Use as an example of the fishermen-straying threat category; qualify with livelihood solutions and IR diplomacy.
**Back:** *"Settled on paper, strayed in practice: fish where the line says no."*

### Flashcard IS-227 — Katchatheevu (1974 cession, legacy politics)
**Front:** What happened at Katchatheevu in 1974 and why is it still politically salient?
**Definition (learn):** In 1974 India recognised Sri Lankan sovereignty over the uninhabited island through an Indo-Sri Lankan maritime agreement, which settled Palk Strait maritime boundaries.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1974.
- Island: uninhabited; 285 acres (115 ha); St Anthony's Shrine is its only structure (Wikipedia fetched).
- Agreement: Indo-Sri Lankan maritime agreement (1974); a 1976 agreement barred each side's fishermen from the other's EEZ (Wikipedia fetched).
- India's government said in 2014 that no territory was ceded; the area was disputed and never demarcated (Wikipedia fetched; government position).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1974 agreement; 1976 agreement (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 07 adv §5).
**Links:** IS-226, IS-228, IS-229.
**Trap:** The owner calls it a "1974 cession"; the fetched source notes India's own 2014 position of no territory ceded. Present both: legal/diplomatic settlement and domestic political framing.
**Mains hook:** "Settled boundary, unsettled domestic politics": show how boundary settlement can still generate security friction.
**Back:** *"Seventy-Four Settled, Twenty-Fourteen Disputed in Delhi's Ledger."*

### Flashcard IS-228 — India–Bangladesh maritime boundary (2014 UNCLOS arbitral award)
**Front:** How was the India–Bangladesh maritime boundary settled?
**Definition (learn):** The India–Bangladesh maritime boundary was delimited by a 2014 UNCLOS arbitral award (owner 07 adv §5; VisionIAS PDF p. 21).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: third-party arbitration under UNCLOS (both parties submitted).
- Year: 2014.
- Owner 07 notes VisionIAS p. 31 is stale on Bangladesh (still says undelimited).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2014 arbitral award (✅ owner 07 adv §5; ⚠️ not fetched from the PCA award text).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-229, IS-230.
**Trap:** Owner 07 basic §7 lists the Bangladesh boundary as settled but the source text at p. 31 says undelimited. Answer with the settled position and note the stale sentence.
**Mains hook:** Use to show third-party settlement works; qualify that Sir Creek is the unresolved segment.
**Back:** *"Two-Fourteen, Bangladesh boundary drawn by an arbitral pen."*

### Flashcard IS-229 — Sir Creek (India–Pakistan, unsettled maritime segment)
**Front:** What is Sir Creek, why is it disputed, and what turns on it?
**Definition (learn):** Sir Creek is a 96 km tidal creek in the Indus delta marshlands separating Gujarat (India) from Sindh (Pakistan), flowing into the Arabian Sea; the dispute is thalweg (mid-channel, India) versus green line (eastern flank, Pakistan).
**Breakdown:**
- Length: 96 km (Wikipedia fetched).
- India: thalweg principle; cites 1914 resolution para 10, 1924 mid-channel pillars, 1925 map.
- Pakistan: green line; cites 1914 resolution para 9; argues thalweg applies to non-tidal rivers.
- 1968 tribunal (Rann of Kutch) gave India 90% and Pakistan 10% of the larger claims; Sir Creek elements remain (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 1914 resolution; 1924 pillars; 1925 map; 1968 tribunal (✅ Wikipedia fetched). ⚠️ EEZ quantum: owner 07 cites "roughly 250 square miles" (VisionIAS PDF p. 11); fetched Wikipedia gives no figure and says "several thousand square kilometres". Do not quote either without flagging the conflict.
**Links:** IS-228, IS-226, topic 06 (land-border dimension).
**Trap:** Sir Creek is the unsettled maritime segment, not the Bangladesh boundary. The EEZ figure conflicts across sources: flag ⚠️.
**Mains hook:** Use to show that unsettled boundaries create a permanent patrolling ambiguity; qualify the area figure.
**Back:** *"Thalweg pulls one way, green line the other: Sir Creek stays unsettled."*

### Flashcard IS-230 — Reactive institutionalisation chronology (coastal security)
**Front:** Trace the reactive chronology behind India's coastal-security architecture.
**Definition (learn):** Each major coastal-security institution followed a specific vulnerability episode rather than anticipatory planning.
**Breakdown:**
- 1974 CMO (anti-smuggling) → 1977 ICG → 2005 CSS (three layers) → post-26/11 NC3I/JOCs (2008+) → 2017 proposed Maritime Theatre Command → 2020 national maritime security coordinator → 2022 Anti-Maritime Piracy Act → 2026 Okha Academy.
- Pattern: vulnerability episode → institution (owner 07 adv §2).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 33, 35 (✅ owner 07 basic §3.2; adv §2-§3).
**Links:** IS-208, IS-209, IS-211, IS-212, IS-225, IS-236.
**Trap:** Do not say the architecture was built proactively. The "mind-set" changed only after 26/11 (adv §8).
**Mains hook:** Central analytic thesis for "reactive, not anticipatory": explain why gaps recur. Conclude with sustained anticipatory planning.
**Back:** *"Smuggling, Ship, Shore, Strike: every stone laid after a wound."*

### Flashcard IS-231 — Awareness vs interdiction (maritime domain awareness)
**Front:** Distinguish maritime domain awareness (MDA) from interdiction capability.
**Definition (learn):** MDA assets (radar, AIS, NC3I, IFC-IOR, VTMS) answer "what is out there?"; interdiction assets (boats, sea-days, trained crew, boarding authority) answer "can we reach it and act lawfully?"
**Breakdown:**
- Awareness: sensors, databases, networks.
- Interdiction: vessels, crew, sea-days, boarding teams.
- India's post-26/11 build-out has been awareness-heavy (adv §2).
- A detection without a reachable response is a logged track, not a security outcome.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 07 basic §7; adv §2, §8 (⚠️ analytical inference of owner).
**Links:** IS-211, IS-214, IS-218, IS-232.
**Trap:** MDA and interdiction are not the same thing. The owner's advanced trap: "Expanding surveillance coverage automatically expands the ability to act" is false.
**Mains hook:** Use in 20-mark comparison answers; qualify that capability gaps (boats, crew) are often the binding constraint.
**Back:** *"Radar says what; boats say can we."*

### Flashcard IS-232 — Single coastal authority and the Navy-eased-out debate
**Front:** What is the still-open command-unification question in coastal security?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS's way-forward section recommends designating the Coast Guard as the single authority for coastal security, removing ambiguities from the Coast Guard Act, integrating State police in detection and capture at sea, and easing the Navy out of coastal tasking so it can focus on blue-water capability.
**Breakdown:**
- Recommendation 1: Coast Guard = single coastal-security authority.
- Recommendation 2: remove ambiguities from the Coast Guard Act, 1978.
- Recommendation 3: State police integrated in detection and capture at sea.
- Recommendation 4: ease the Navy out of coastal responsibilities (quote per owner adv §2, PDF p. 37).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 37 (✅ owner 07 adv §2, §7). Status: unresolved per source (adv §7).
**Links:** IS-203, IS-204, IS-210, IS-213, IS-222, IS-231.
**Trap:** The 2026 Okha academy does not resolve this. The Navy-eased-out point is a resource argument, not only a tidiness argument (adv §2).
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark "critically examine the single-authority recommendation": present for (single chain, speed) and against (blue-water needs, State police role, legal change); conclude with a calibrated hybrid (designated lead + common protocol).
**Back:** *"One Coast Guard, one chain, the Navy freed for the blue."*

### Flashcard IS-233 — Fishermen as "ears and eyes" (asset vs vulnerability)
**Front:** Why are fishermen both an intelligence asset and a security vulnerability?
**Definition (learn):** Trained volunteer fishermen groups are the "ears and eyes" of coastal security; the same population has been a vulnerability (2008-era risk that straying Indian-registered boats could carry arms, explosives and operatives).
**Breakdown:**
- Asset: volunteer monitoring groups; multi-purpose fishermen ID cards; uniform boat licensing; GPS/transponder tracking (VisionIAS PDF p. 36).
- Vulnerability: Indian-registered boats could be misused (Singh PDF p. 122, owner 07 adv §2).
- Engagement design decides which.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 36; Singh PDF p. 122 (✅ owner 07 adv §2).
**Links:** IS-214, IS-226, IS-237.
**Trap:** Do not treat every fishing craft as hostile; monitoring must preserve livelihood and due process (basic §4 table).
**Mains hook:** Use in "way forward" as community policing; qualify with livelihood and rights.
**Back:** *"Ears and eyes on the water, but never suspect every boat."*

### Flashcard IS-234 — Piracy jurisdiction boundary (high seas vs territorial waters)
**Front:** Which law governs violence in India's territorial waters versus the high seas?
**Definition (learn):** The Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 applies to the high seas including the EEZ; violence in territorial or internal waters (Sunderbans-type incidents) falls under ordinary criminal law.
**Breakdown:**
- High seas + EEZ: piracy Act.
- Territorial/internal waters: ordinary law.
- Terror-linked sea acts: SUA Act, 2002 (IS-220).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Anti-Maritime Piracy Act, 2022 (✅ owner 07 adv §5).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-206, IS-221.
**Trap:** The piracy Act's jurisdiction must not be applied automatically to territorial-water violence (basic §12 claim table).
**Mains hook:** Use to show the three-statute architecture (Zones 1976, SUA 2002, Piracy 2022).
**Back:** *"High seas: Piracy Act. Our waters: ordinary law."*

### Flashcard IS-235 — Maritime Theatre Command (2017, proposed)
**Front:** What was the 2017 proposal for a Maritime Theatre Command?
**Definition (learn):** A proposed integrated maritime command structure recorded in the owner's chronology as proposed in 2017.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2017 (proposed, not confirmed as established).
- Listed in owner 07 basic §3.2 chronology.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 33 (✅ owner 07 — "proposed").
**Links:** IS-230, IS-232.
**Trap:** Proposed ≠ operational. Do not present it as an established command.
**Mains hook:** Cite as one of the command-unification debates; qualify it as a proposal.
**Back:** *"Seventeen proposed, not yet commanded."*

### Flashcard IS-236 — National maritime security coordinator (2020)
**Front:** When was the first national maritime security coordinator appointed?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's chronology records the first national maritime security coordinator as appointed in 2020.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2020 (owner 07 basic §3.2).
- Appointment of a coordinator is coordination-oriented, not an operational command.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 33 (✅ owner 07). ⚠️ Office holder not named in owner; not verified.
**Links:** IS-213, IS-230, IS-232.
**Trap:** A coordinator is not the Coast Guard single authority; the coordination/command gap remains.
**Mains hook:** Use as evidence of coordination reform; qualify with the command-unification question.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Twenty coordinates; the commander is still unnamed."*

### Flashcard IS-237 — Coastal helplines and fishermen registration measures
**Front:** Which coastal helplines and registration measures are named in the owner?
**Definition (learn):** Coastal-security helplines: 1554 (Indian Coast Guard) and 1093 (Marine Police); fishermen registration includes AIS transponders on larger vessels, RFID proposals for smaller craft, colour-coded registration, Distress Alert Transmitters.
**Breakdown:**
- Helplines: 1554 ICG; 1093 Marine Police (owner 07 adv §4).
- AIS on larger vessels; RFID proposal for smaller ones.
- Distress Alert Transmitters.
- Converts a vulnerable population into a monitored, semi-formal intelligence layer (VisionIAS PDF p. 27).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 27 (✅ owner 07 adv §4). ⚠️ Helpline numbers not independently verified.
**Links:** IS-203, IS-209, IS-214, IS-233.
**Trap:** Helpline 1554 is ICG, 1093 is Marine Police; do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** Use under community-layer answers; qualify with privacy and livelihood.
**Back:** *"Fifteen-Fifty-Four calls the Coast Guard; Ten-Ninety-Three calls the Marine Police."*

### Flashcard IS-238 — PYQ 2022 GS-III Q10 (maritime challenges and organisational initiatives)
**Front:** What is the 2022 GS-III Q10 demand and how should it be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Verbatim PYQ demand (owner 07 basic §12, §Historical PYQ table): "Maritime security challenges and organisational procedural initiatives India" — Discuss · 10 marks · 150 words.
**Breakdown:**
- Structure: define maritime/coastal distinction → zone ladder → Navy/ICG/Marine Police map → JOCs, NC3I, radar/AIS/VTMS, Sagar Kavach → one legal distinction (Piracy 2022 vs SUA 2002) → qualification (awareness is not interdiction).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022 GS-III Q10 (✅ owner 07 historical PYQ integration; routed ledger `_PYQ-ROUTING-MAINS-GS3-GS4-2018-2023.md`).
**Links:** IS-201, IS-210, IS-212, IS-221, IS-231.
**Trap:** A 10-mark answer must not drift into a full threat catalogue; keep the organisational spine.
**Mains hook:** Model answer follows the owner's 10-mark spine; qualified conclusion: awareness plus interdiction plus legal clarity.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Two's ten: define, map, link, qualify."*

### Flashcard IS-239 — PYQ 2025 GS-III Q20 (sea trade, challenges, way forward)
**Front:** What is the 2025 GS-III Q20 demand and what answer route does the owner set?
**Definition (learn):** Verbatim: "Why is maritime security vital to protect India's sea trade? Discuss maritime and coastal security challenges and the way forward." (Discuss · 15 marks · 250 words).
**Breakdown:**
- Route: trade-dependence context (cite only verified figures) → maritime vs coastal + zone ladder → five threats → three-tier architecture + post-26/11 institutions → legal layer (1976/2002/2022) → way forward: Okha Academy (2026), single-authority question, capacity-building.
- 20-mark variant spine: MDA vs interdiction vs cyber resilience (owner 07 adv §12).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2025 GS-III Q20 (✅ owner 07 PYQ integration; routed ledger `_PYQ-ROUTING-MAINS-GS3-GS4-2024-2025.md`).
**Links:** IS-201, IS-207, IS-210, IS-221, IS-222, IS-232, IS-240.
**Trap:** The "way forward" should show prevention, preparedness, response, recovery, not "more patrols".
**Mains hook:** Use the sea-trade premise; conclude with a calibrated reform route.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Five's trade question: sea-lanes sold, shore defended, reform in order."*

### Flashcard IS-240 — Sea-trade and hydrocarbon dependence claim (⚠️ dated share)
**Front:** What proportion of India's trade and hydrocarbon requirement moves by sea, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 07 quotes VisionIAS: "more than 90 percent of India's trade is through sea routes" and "more than 80% of our hydrocarbon requirement is ferried through sea route" (PDF p. 37).
**Breakdown:**
- Source claim: >90% trade; >80% hydrocarbon by sea.
- Status: source-cited proportions, not a dated official statistic (owner 07 basic §3.0).
- Action: confirm against a current Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways or Navy release before quoting in exam.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 37 (✅ owner 07, flagged ⚠️).
**Links:** IS-239, IS-223.
**Trap:** Do not state these percentages as a verified current official statistic. Quote as source-cited.
**Mains hook:** Use as context in the 2025 answer; qualify as a source-cited figure.
**Back:** *"Ninety for trade, eighty for fuel: quote the source, not the sum."*

### Flashcard IS-241 — Maritime and coastal layering: who handles which threat (comparison)
**Front:** Which agency leads which threat across zones, and what is the common weakness?
**Definition (learn):** A comparative grid of zone × agency × threat that shows lead responsibility changes with location; the common weakness is overlap at the margins.
**Breakdown:**
- 0-12 nm: Marine Police (close coastal) + ICG (territorial) — infiltration, smuggling, straying fishermen.
- 12-200 nm EEZ: ICG and Navy surveillance — smuggling, illegal fishing, terror transit.
- High seas / SLOCs: Navy; piracy under the 2022 Act.
- Common weakness: command ambiguity at the margins; awareness not matched by interdiction.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 07 basic §1-§3, adv §2-§3 (⚠️ comparison grid authored from owner text).
**Links:** IS-202, IS-210, IS-231, IS-232.
**Trap:** A tiered diagram is a simplification. Real incidents need a single protocol, not three separate lead claims.
**Mains hook:** Use in 20-mark comparison: state zones, leads and gaps; conclude with command clarity plus capability.
**Back:** *"Zone decides lead; overlap decides the failure."*

### GROUP — 08 Cyber Security, CII and Cybercrime (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard IS-242 — Cybercrime vs cyber warfare vs cyber terrorism (classification)
**Front:** Distinguish cybercrime, cyber warfare and cyber terrorism.
**Definition (learn):** Cybercrime = use of cyberspace to commit crime against an individual or corporate entity; cyber warfare = state-initiated internet-based force as an instrument of policy to sabotage or spy on another nation; cyber terrorism = terrorist activity through cyberspace by an organisation working independently of a nation-state.
**Breakdown:**
- Target: cybercrime → individuals/corporates; cyber warfare → states; cyber terrorism → state and society, with terror intent.
- Actor: cybercrime → criminals; cyber warfare → state; cyber terrorism → non-state.
- India's statutory hook for cyber terrorism: Section 66F (IS-243).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh PDF pp. 99, 101 (✅ owner 08 basic §2). Definitions are owner-sourced; ⚠️ not separately fetched.
**Links:** IS-243, IS-256 (incident/crime/operation split), IS-262.
**Trap:** The owner's two-way split is cybercrime vs cyber warfare; cyber terrorism is a third category. "Hacking by a state" is not cyber terrorism; "hacking by a terror group" is cyber terrorism only if terror intent is present.
**Mains hook:** "Distinguish cybercrime, cyber warfare and cyber terrorism" (15 marks) — use the three-way split, then the institution that answers each. Qualify with attribution difficulty.
**Back:** *"Crime hits people, War hits states, Terror hits minds with intent."*

### Flashcard IS-243 — Section 66F, IT Act 2000 (cyber terrorism offence)
**Front:** What does Section 66F of the IT Act, 2000 penalise and with what punishment?
**Definition (learn):** Section 66F (inserted by the 2008 amendment) defines cyber terrorism: denial of access, unauthorised access or introduction of contaminants with intent to threaten India's unity, integrity, security or sovereignty or to strike terror; and unauthorised access to restricted information likely to injure sovereignty, friendly relations, public order or State security. Punishable with imprisonment for life.
**Breakdown:**
- Acts: denial of access; unauthorised access; contaminant introduction.
- Intent: threaten unity, integrity, security, sovereignty; strike terror.
- Restricted information limb: injury to sovereignty, friendly relations, public order, security.
- Punishment: imprisonment for life (owner 08 basic §2, §6).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act, 2000 s.66F (inserted 2008) (✅ owner 08; Singh PDF pp. 101-102). ⚠️ Not fetched from India Code in this pass; section and punishment are the owner's statement.
**Links:** IS-242, IS-263 (66A struck down), IS-248 (69/69A/69B), topic 02.
**Trap:** 66A ≠ 66F. 66A (offensive messages) was struck down in 2015; 66F (cyber terrorism) remains in force. Examiners swap them.
**Mains hook:** Use to show India has a statutory cyber-terror offence; qualify that the offence is prosecuted through BNS plus IT Act post-2024.
**Back:** *"Sixty-Six F for Force of terror; Sixty-Six A was struck, F stands."*

### Flashcard IS-244 — Critical Information Infrastructure (CII) and Section 70, IT Act 2000
**Front:** Define CII under Section 70 and what a "protected system" is.
**Definition (learn):** Section 70 defines CII as computer resources whose incapacitation or destruction would have a debilitating impact on national security, economy, public health or safety. The appropriate government may declare a resource a "protected system", with penal consequences for unauthorised access.
**Breakdown:**
- Criterion = consequence of incapacitation (not sector or technology alone).
- Sectors: energy, transport, banking/finance, telecom, defence, space, law enforcement, government, public health, water, e-governance (Singh PDF p. 105).
- Mechanism: notification as protected system.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act, 2000 s.70 (✅ owner 08 basic §1-§2; Singh PDF p. 106; VisionIAS PDF p. 3).
**Links:** IS-245 (NCIIPC), IS-246 (CERT-In), IS-257 (interdependence), IS-244 vs IS-245 distinct.
**Trap:** Sector membership alone does not establish notified CII status (owner 08 basic §12 table). A CII is defined by consequence, not by the technology.
**Mains hook:** "Consequence-led" prioritisation; qualify that notification, not definition, makes an asset protected.
**Back:** *"Seventy defines the Critical; a protected system is the notified one."*

### Flashcard IS-245 — NCIIPC (National Critical Information Infrastructure Protection Centre)
**Front:** What is NCIIPC, under which body and which section?
**Definition (learn):** NCIIPC is the specialised nodal agency for CII protection, a unit under the National Technical Research Organisation (NTRO), under Section 70A of the IT Act.
**Breakdown:**
- Parent: NTRO.
- Statutory base: Section 70A.
- Functions (VisionIAS PDF p. 23): identification of all CII elements; strategic leadership and coherence across government; coordinating, sharing, monitoring, collecting, analysing and forecasting national-level threats to CII.
- Plus malware analysis, cyber forensics, CII-owner facilitation (Singh PDF pp. 110-111).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act s.70A; NTRO (✅ owner 08 basic §4; adv §4).
**Links:** IS-244, IS-246, IS-265.
**Trap:** NCIIPC is protective (CII); CERT-In is incident-response. They are not the same body.
**Mains hook:** Use to show dual-institution architecture; qualify with notified-asset coverage.
**Back:** *"NTRO's NCIIPC guards the notified crown jewels."*

### Flashcard IS-246 — CERT-In (Indian Computer Emergency Response Team)
**Front:** What is CERT-In, under which section and ministry, and when was it set up?
**Definition (learn):** CERT-In is India's national nodal agency for cyber incident evaluation, prediction, alerts and emergency response, constituted under Section 70B of the IT Act, 2000, under MeitY.
**Breakdown:**
- Established: 19 January 2004 (Wikipedia fetched).
- Parent ministry: MeitY (originally under the Ministry of Communications and Information Technology).
- Statute: IT Act s.70B.
- Functions: incident response, alerts, audits and empanelment of audit organisations (adv §4).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act s.70B; 19 Jan 2004 (✅ Wikipedia "Indian Computer Emergency Response Team" fetched; ✅ owner 08 basic §4, VisionIAS PDF p. 27).
**Links:** IS-245, IS-247, IS-265.
**Trap:** CERT-In's 2022 six-hour rule is incident reporting, not CII protection. Do not call CERT-In the CII nodal agency.
**Mains hook:** Use CERT-In for response and recovery; qualify that response does not equal attribution.
**Back:** *"Oh-Four CERT: seventy-B responds; seventy-A protects."*

### Flashcard IS-247 — CERT-In Directions, 28 April 2022 — six-hour reporting
**Front:** What do the CERT-In Directions of 28 April 2022 require?
**Definition (learn):** Covered entities must report specified cyber incidents to CERT-In within six hours of noticing them or being informed of them.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 28 April 2022 (directions; FAQs cite 28.04.2022).
- Requirement: six hours for specified incidents.
- Later FAQs narrowed the scope to high-severity incidents (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** CERT-In Directions, 28 April 2022 (📰 owner 08 basic §4, §8; ✅ Wikipedia fetched for the six-hour rule and FAQ narrowing).
**Links:** IS-246, IS-256 (incident vs crime).
**Trap:** An incident report is not a registered crime; incident volumes are not crime statistics (owner 08 basic §7). Do not state the six-hour rule without noting the FAQ narrowing.
**Mains hook:** Use to show prevention-through-reporting; qualify that reporting volume is not a proxy for risk.
**Back:** *"Six hours to tell CERT, not six days to hide it."*

### Flashcard IS-248 — Interception, blocking and traffic-monitoring powers (IT Act ss. 69, 69A, 69B)
**Front:** What are the powers in Sections 69, 69A and 69B of the IT Act, 2000?
**Definition (learn):** S.69: interception, monitoring or decryption of information in the interest of sovereignty, integrity, defence, security, friendly relations, public order or incitement to a cognisable offence; S.69A: blocking public access to information; S.69B: monitoring and collecting traffic data for cyber-security purposes. Each is subject to rules.
**Breakdown:**
- 69: interception, monitoring, decryption.
- 69A: blocking.
- 69B: traffic data monitoring.
- All require procedure prescribed by rules.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act, 2000 ss.69, 69A, 69B (✅ owner 08 basic §2; ⚠️ not fetched from India Code).
**Links:** IS-243, IS-253 (Telecom Act), topic 09 (social media blocking).
**Trap:** 69 (interception) is not 69A (blocking) and 69B (traffic data). Quote the section that matches the power.
**Mains hook:** Naming the section shows prevention-vs-response split in law; qualify with privacy and procedural safeguards.
**Back:** *"Sixty-Nine listens, Sixty-Nine-A blocks, Sixty-Nine-B counts the traffic."*

### Flashcard IS-249 — Cyber Swachhta Kendra (Botnet Cleaning and Malware Analysis Centre)
**Front:** What is the Cyber Swachhta Kendra and what is its role?
**Definition (learn):** The Botnet Cleaning and Malware Analysis Centre identifies botnet infections in India, alerts users and provides free tools for cleaning and securing end-user systems.
**Breakdown:**
- Role: prevention and hygiene (not response).
- Output: alerts to users; free cleaning and security tools.
- Distinct from CERT-In (response) and NCIIPC (CII protection).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 23, 27 (✅ owner 08 basic §4, adv §4).
**Links:** IS-246, IS-245, IS-264 (CyberDome, citizen-level hygiene).
**Trap:** Cyber Swachhta Kendra is a hygiene institution, not a CERT. Do not call it a CII protection body.
**Mains hook:** Use under "prevention and resilience" as citizen-level defence; qualify with user adoption.
**Back:** *"Swachhta cleans botnets; it does not command a response."*

### Flashcard IS-250 — I4C (Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre), 1930 helpline and cybercrime reporting portal
**Front:** What is I4C, which ministry runs it, and what citizen-facing tools does it have?
**Definition (learn):** I4C is MHA's coordination platform for cybercrime prevention, reporting, investigation support and capacity building; citizen-facing components include the National Cybercrime Reporting Portal and the financial-fraud helpline 1930.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: MHA.
- Functions: prevention, reporting, investigation support, capacity building.
- Helpline 1930 (financial cyber-fraud); national cybercrime reporting portal.
- Distinct from CERT-In and NCIIPC.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** I4C under MHA; helpline 1930 (✅ owner 08 basic §4, §6). ⚠️ Owner flags verification of current component list; not fetched.
**Links:** IS-246, IS-256, IS-267 (BNS).
**Trap:** Do not confuse 1930 (financial fraud) with 112 or 1077. The portal is for reporting; the crime is investigated by State police with I4C support.
**Mains hook:** Use to show the crime chain (report → investigate → prosecute); qualify with victim redress and State police capacity.
**Back:** *"Nineteen-Thirty for the money trail; the portal for the crime."*

### Flashcard IS-251 — National Cyber Security Policy, 2013
**Front:** What is the National Cyber Security Policy 2013 and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2013 national policy framework for cyber security; the owner treats it as the notified national policy unless a replacement is officially notified.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2013.
- Status: remains the notified national policy (owner 08 basic §4, ⚠️ inference).
- Singh's 2013 targets are book-period claims; verify current status.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NCSP 2013 (Singh PDF pp. 107-109; ✅ owner 08 basic §4). ⚠️ Current status not fetched.
**Links:** IS-252, IS-262, IS-270, IS-271.
**Trap:** Owner adv §1 calls the 2013 policy "largely aspirational" in objectives; do not describe it as superseded without a notified replacement.
**Mains hook:** The 2022 PYQ asks whether a comprehensive national strategy exists; qualify that a 2013 policy and sectoral measures are not a single integrated strategy.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Thirteen policy, aspirational frame: strategy still not whole."*

### Flashcard IS-252 — National Digital Communications Policy, 2018
**Front:** What does the NDCP 2018 cover and how is it different from the cyber policy?
**Definition (learn):** A telecom policy setting communication-network security and "digital sovereignty" objectives.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2018.
- Scope: telecom and communication networks, not general cyber security.
- Objectives: network security; digital sovereignty (VisionIAS pp. 22-23).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** NDCP 2018 (VisionIAS pp. 22-23; ✅ owner 08 basic §4).
**Links:** IS-251, IS-253, IS-259.
**Trap:** NDCP is a telecom policy, not a cyber-security policy (owner 08 basic §4).
**Mains hook:** Use in the 5G/telecom answer; qualify that telecom policy and cyber policy are distinct instruments.
**Back:** *"Telecom policy, not cyber policy: digital sovereignty in the network."*

### Flashcard IS-253 — Telecommunications Act, 2023
**Front:** What does the Telecommunications Act, 2023 replace and what powers does it give to government?
**Definition (learn):** The Telecommunications Act, 2023 (Act No. 44 of 2023) replaces the Indian Telegraph Act, 1885 (and per the infobox, the Indian Wireless Telegraphy Act, 1933); it lets the government take over, manage or suspend telecom services and networks for national security reasons, and provides interception and decryption provisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Passed: Lok Sabha 20 Dec 2023; Rajya Sabha 21 Dec 2023.
- Assent: 25 December 2023 (Act No. 44 of 2023).
- Replaces: Indian Telegraph Act 1885; Indian Wireless Telegraphy Act 1933 (infobox).
- Powers: take over, manage, suspend telecom services; suspend or prohibit equipment from certain countries or persons; interception and decryption.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Telecommunications Act, 2023 (✅ Wikipedia fetched; owner 08 basic §4, §6).
**Links:** IS-248, IS-252, IS-254, topic 09.
**Trap:** The owner's claim "Section 20 provides interception" is ⚠️ — Wikipedia did not give section numbers; do not cite s.20 without India Code confirmation. Do not say it "replaced the IT Act".
**Mains hook:** Use as the network-level statute; qualify that three statutes (IT Act, Telecom Act, DPDP) have different regulators.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-Eighty-Five telegraph gives way to twenty-three telecom."*

### Flashcard IS-254 — Digital Personal Data Protection (DPDP) Act, 2023 — salient features
**Front:** What are the salient features of the DPDP Act, 2023?
**Definition (learn):** India's comprehensive personal-data-protection statute, which sets consent, notice and data-fiduciary obligations, creates the Data Protection Board and gives special protections to children.
**Breakdown:**
- Enacted: Lok Sabha 7 Aug 2023; Rajya Sabha 9 Aug 2023; assent 11 Aug 2023 (Wikipedia fetched).
- Data Protection Board: s.18; adjudicator of disputes between data principals and fiduciaries.
- Children: special protections for those under 18 (fetched).
- Penalties: a Schedule-based scale; Wikipedia fetched reads "minimum penalty for breach is 50 crore INR" — ⚠️ conflict with commonly reported schedule maxima (e.g. ₹250 crore); do not quote a penalty figure until checked against the Act's Schedule.
- Consent manager registration: from 13 Nov 2026 (IS-255).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** DPDP Act, 2023 (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 08 basic §4, §12).
**Links:** IS-255, IS-256, IS-266, IS-272.
**Trap:** DPDP is a privacy/data-governance statute, not CII protection or cybercrime. It is not India's complete cyber framework (owner 08 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** 2024 PYQ route: context → features → phased commencement; close by separating privacy governance from security.
**Back:** *"Consent, Board, Children, Fiduciary: DPDP guards data, not the grid."*

### Flashcard IS-255 — DPDP Rules, 2025 — phased commencement dates
**Front:** What are the commencement dates of the DPDP Act and Rules?
**Definition (learn):** Phased commencement: 13 November 2025 (Board provisions and certain sections; G.S.R. 843(E) commencement notification); 13 November 2026 (Consent Manager registration rules, Rule 4); 13 May 2027 (remaining core compliance rules).
**Breakdown:**
- 13 Nov 2025: sections 2, 18-26, 35, 38-43, 44(1),(3) per Wikipedia; Rules 1, 2 and 17-21 per owner.
- 13 Nov 2026: s.6(9) and s.27(1)(d); Rule 4 (Consent Managers).
- 13 May 2027: remaining provisions (notice/consent, fiduciary obligations, security, children, cross-border transfer).
- Gazette: G.S.R. 846(E) for the Rules (owner 08 basic §4); ⚠️ Gazette number from owner, not confirmed by fetch.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 13 Nov 2025 / 13 Nov 2026 / 13 May 2027 (✅ Wikipedia fetched dates; 📰 owner 08 basic §8).
**Links:** IS-254, IS-256.
**Trap:** "Fully in force" on notification (13 Nov 2025) is wrong; use provision-wise dates. Owner adv §9 insists on dated sources.
**Mains hook:** "Notification ≠ enforcement" — use the three-date ladder to show phased rights-vs-readiness design.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Five notices, Twenty-Six registers, Twenty-Seven rules bite."*

### Flashcard IS-256 — Incident vs cybercrime vs information operation (three chains)
**Front:** Why must a cyber answer separate incident, crime and information operation?
**Definition (learn):** A technical incident (CERT-In reportable) ends in containment and recovery; a cybercrime (penal offence) ends in investigation and prosecution; an information operation (perception-shaping campaign) ends in attribution, platform action or counter-messaging.
**Breakdown:**
- Incident → CERT-In → containment, patching, recovery.
- Crime → State police + I4C → BNS + IT Act prosecution.
- Information operation → intelligence, diplomacy → attribution, platform action, counter-messaging (topic 09).
- The same event can be all three; institutions and remedies differ.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 08 basic §3.1, adv §2 (⚠️ analytical split authored from owner text).
**Links:** IS-246, IS-250, IS-242, IS-267, topic 09 (IS-281 onwards).
**Trap:** Incident counts and crime statistics are published by different bodies and are not interchangeable.
**Mains hook:** Use as the structural frame of any "cyber resilience" answer (20 marks). Qualify that improvement in one chain does not mean improvement in the others.
**Back:** *"Incident heals, crime punishes, operation persuades."*

### Flashcard IS-257 — CII interdependence and three amplifiers
**Front:** Why does a CII disruption cascade, and what are the three amplifiers?
**Definition (learn):** CII sectors are interdependent, so a disruption cascades across them. VisionIAS names three amplifiers: interconnectedness of sectors, proliferation of exposure points, and concentration of assets (PDF p. 31).
**Breakdown:**
- Interdependence: energy, transport, banking, telecom, defence, space, government, health, water, e-governance.
- Amplifiers: (1) interconnectedness; (2) proliferation of exposure points; (3) concentration of assets.
- Cascade: VisionIAS PDF p. 3.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 3, 31; Singh PDF p. 105 (✅ owner 08 basic §3.3).
**Links:** IS-244, IS-245, IS-258, IS-259.
**Trap:** Interdependence is a feature, not a defect; the amplifier answer is about exposure, not sector size.
**Mains hook:** Use in the threat-vulnerability-consequence answer; qualify with sector-level CERTs.
**Back:** *"Link, Expose, Concentrate: three amplifiers of cascade."*

### Flashcard IS-258 — Internal vs external threat sources (CII)
**Front:** What are the internal and external threat sources to CII?
**Definition (learn):** Internal threats: insiders with access or inside knowledge causing intentional or negligent harm. External threats: hackers, organised cyber-criminals, terrorist groups, foreign-government agents, non-state actors.
**Breakdown:**
- Internal: insider (intentional or negligent).
- External: hackers; organised cybercriminals; terrorist groups; foreign-government agents; non-state actors.
- Source: Singh PDF p. 106.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh PDF p. 106 (✅ owner 08 basic §3.4).
**Links:** IS-257, IS-262, IS-264.
**Trap:** Negligent insiders count as internal threats; not every internal threat is malicious.
**Mains hook:** Use in threat classification; qualify that insider controls and procurement are part of resilience.
**Back:** *"Insiders harm by access; outsiders harm by reach."*

### Flashcard IS-259 — 5G attack-surface shift (200 times more attack vectors)
**Front:** Why does 5G expand the attack surface, per the security-agency assessment cited by VisionIAS?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS records an assessment that 5G has "200 times more attack vectors" than 4G, because switching moves from centralised hardware to "distributed, software-defined digital routing" on common internet protocols with shared infrastructure and AI-managed networks.
**Breakdown:**
- Shift: centralised hardware → distributed software-defined routing.
- Common protocols and shared infrastructure.
- Early-generation AI for network management: a controller of managing software controls the network.
- Risk: "potential for mass failure across multiple linked-networks" (PDF pp. 21-22).
- Responses named: indigenous equipment, in-India data storage, shared operator-level security responsibility.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 21-22 (✅ owner 08 basic §3.7, adv §2, §7). ⚠️ The "200 times" is a cited assessment, not a verified primary figure.
**Links:** IS-252, IS-260, IS-261.
**Trap:** The "200 times" figure is an attributed assessment; present it as "cited by VisionIAS".
**Mains hook:** Use as an architectural change (perimeter → software supply-chain problem); qualify that responses are industrial and regulatory.
**Back:** *"Five-G, two-hundred vectors: software is the new frontier."*

### Flashcard IS-260 — Huawei/ZTE equipment share (20%) vs Indian manufacturers (3%)
**Front:** What supply-chain figure does VisionIAS cite for telecom equipment?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS (PDF p. 28): Chinese manufacturers such as Huawei and ZTE have supplied about 20% of telecom equipment while Indian manufacturers have about 3% of the market.
**Breakdown:**
- Foreign share: about 20% (Huawei, ZTE).
- Indian share: about 3%.
- Illustration of import-dependence risk.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 28 (✅ owner 08 adv §2, §8). ⚠️ Dated only by the source; not a current official figure.
**Links:** IS-261, IS-259, topic 10 (Economy/IR adjacent).
**Trap:** Owner adv §8: do not say India's telecom equipment supply is predominantly domestic. The figure is a cited, persisting vulnerability.
**Mains hook:** Use under supply-chain constraint; qualify with economic dimensions (Economy topic) and trusted-source rules.
**Back:** *"Twenty per cent from Huawei and ZTE, three from home: a supply gap."*

### Flashcard IS-261 — Telecom equipment certification and trusted-source procurement
**Front:** What measures were announced to ensure imported communication equipment is secure?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS records announced measures including mandatory local certification and an equipment testing laboratory to ensure imported equipment is free of vulnerabilities (PDF p. 23). Owner adds that India has since operated a trusted-source regime under a National Security Directive on the Telecommunication Sector (⚠️ verify designating authority).
**Breakdown:**
- Mandatory local certification.
- Equipment testing laboratory.
- Trusted-source regime (⚠️ not verified in this pass).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 23 (✅ owner 08 adv §4); National Security Directive on the Telecom Sector ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** IS-260, IS-259, IS-253.
**Trap:** A certification regime is not a complete supply-chain strategy; owner adv §6 says it needs a longer domestic manufacturing strategy.
**Mains hook:** Use as the security-institutional response to supply-chain risk; qualify with industrial policy.
**Back:** *"Certify locally, test in the lab, trust the source."*

### Flashcard IS-262 — Attribution difficulty as structural constraint
**Front:** Why does attribution difficulty limit deterrence in cyberspace?
**Definition (learn):** Singh's point (PDF pp. 102-103): cyberspace allows attackers to cover tracks and mislead targets into believing the attack came from elsewhere, which makes retaliation a weak deterrent.
**Breakdown:**
- Feature of the domain itself: disguised attackers.
- Consequence: retaliation-based deterrence is weak.
- Implication: resilience and rapid recovery take priority.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh PDF pp. 102-103 (✅ owner 08 adv §2).
**Links:** IS-242, IS-256, IS-265, IS-270.
**Trap:** Attribution difficulty is not only a capacity gap; more funding does not remove it. Do not claim attribution from a technical incident alone.
**Mains hook:** "Attribution difficulty makes deterrence limited; resilience first" (15 marks). Qualify with diplomatic and legal attribution routes.
**Back:** *"Disguised attacker, false flag: retaliation is a weak deterrent."*

### Flashcard IS-263 — Section 66A struck down (Shreya Singhal, 2015)
**Front:** Which judgment struck down Section 66A of the IT Act, and on what ground?
**Definition (learn):** Shreya Singhal v. Union of India (2015) struck down Section 66A as unconstitutionally vague restriction on free speech.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2015.
- Ground: vagueness; unconstitutional restriction on free speech.
- Lesson: enforcement power requires constitutional calibration; carried into DPDP's safeguards (adv §5).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Shreya Singhal v. Union of India (2015) (✅ owner 08 basic §7, adv §7).
**Links:** IS-243, IS-248, IS-254.
**Trap:** 66A (struck down) vs 66F (cyber terrorism, in force). Do not cite 66A as current law.
**Mains hook:** Use to show constitutional calibration of cyber law; qualify with national-security exceptions.
**Back:** *"Sixty-Six A went, Sixty-Six F stays for terror."*

### Flashcard IS-264 — CyberDome Project (Kerala Police model)
**Front:** What is CyberDome and what does the 2019 PYQ ask?
**Definition (learn):** CyberDome is a State-level public-private cyber-security and cybercrime support initiative (owner names Kerala Police) providing specialist forensics, research, awareness and police capacity.
**Breakdown:**
- Model: State public-private collaboration.
- Utility: forensics, research, awareness, police capacity.
- Limit: not a substitute for national incident response or ordinary police investigation.
- PYQ: 2019 GS-III (10 marks) "What is CyberDome Project? Explain how it can be useful in controlling internet crimes in India."
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2019 GS-III Q10 (✅ owner 08 basic §9, historical PYQ table). ⚠️ "Kerala Police" attribution from owner text; not fetched.
**Links:** IS-250, IS-256, IS-268.
**Trap:** CyberDome is State-level; do not present it as national architecture.
**Mains hook:** Use as a state-level example; qualify with scalability and privacy.
**Back:** *"Kerala's dome watches crime; the nation needs more than a dome."*

### Flashcard IS-265 — Sectoral CERTs (CSIRT-Fin, CSIRT-Power)
**Front:** What are sectoral CERTs and what examples does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** Sector-specific incident-response teams coordinating with CERT-In, reflecting the need for sector-level response capacity; owner names CSIRT-Fin and CSIRT-Power.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: CSIRT-Fin (finance), CSIRT-Power (power/energy).
- Role: sector-level response and coordination with CERT-In.
- Distinct from national CERT-In and NCIIPC.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 27 (✅ owner 08 adv §4).
**Links:** IS-246, IS-245, IS-257.
**Trap:** Sectoral CERTs are not CII protection nodal agencies; NCIIPC is.
**Mains hook:** Use for interdependence answers; qualify with inter-sector coordination.
**Back:** *"Sector CERTs answer for their own pillars."*

### Flashcard IS-266 — Audit-mandate model (CERT-In and NCIIPC sectoral audits)
**Front:** What is the audit-mandate model and why is it preventive?
**Definition (learn):** CERT-In and NCIIPC jointly conduct sectoral cyber-security audits (power/energy, transport, BFSI) as a preventive, not only reactive, tool; CERT-In empanels audit organisations.
**Breakdown:**
- Audits: sectoral (power, transport, BFSI).
- Empanelment: CERT-In empanels audit organisations.
- Shift: from aspirational 2013 objectives to measurable compliance verification.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 08 adv §2, §4 (📰 current-anchor — any count must be sourced from a CERT-In report; ⚠️ not verified).
**Links:** IS-246, IS-245, IS-251.
**Trap:** Do not state an audit count without a linked official report; rising audit counts do not mean rising risk (adv §8).
**Mains hook:** Use as evidence of institutional maturation; qualify with capacity and private-operator coordination.
**Back:** *"Audit before the breach: prevention by paperwork and proof."*

### Flashcard IS-267 — Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS) replaces IPC from 1 July 2024
**Front:** Since when do BNS provisions govern cyber-enabled offences, and what did they replace?
**Definition (learn):** Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 replaced the Indian Penal Code, 1860, with effect from 1 July 2024; cyber-enabled offences are charged under BNS read with IT Act offences.
**Breakdown:**
- Enacted: 2023 (assent 25 Dec 2023, Wikipedia fetched).
- Commencement: 1 July 2024.
- Replaces: IPC 1860.
- Cyber terrorism still under IT Act s.66F.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** BNS 2023; effective 1 July 2024 (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 08 basic §7).
**Links:** IS-243, IS-250, IS-256.
**Trap:** A 2024 "cybercrime under IPC" option is wrong for an event after 1 July 2024.
**Mains hook:** Use to show the penal framework (BNS + IT Act); qualify that offences before 1 July 2024 are governed by the IPC.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-Sixty gives way to Twenty-Three, live from July Twenty-Four."*

### Flashcard IS-268 — PYQ 2020 GS-III Q9 (types of cybercrime and countermeasures)
**Front:** What does the 2020 GS-III cyber PYQ demand, and how should a 10-mark answer run?
**Definition (learn):** Owner's routed demand: "Types of cyber crimes and countermeasures against menace" (Discuss · 10 marks · 150 words).
**Breakdown:**
- Classify cybercrime by target/method: fraud, malware/ransomware, identity/data abuse, intrusion.
- Match each with prevention, reporting, investigation and victim recovery.
- Countermeasures: CERT-In, I4C, 1930, sectoral CERTs.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2020 GS-III Q9 (✅ owner 08 historical PYQ table, Core §12).
**Links:** IS-242, IS-250, IS-246.
**Trap:** A list of crime types without a countermeasure map scores lower.
**Mains hook:** Use classification + countermeasure matrix; qualified conclusion: prevention and reporting, plus investigation capacity.
**Back:** *"Classify the crime, match the counter."*

### Flashcard IS-269 — PYQ 2021 GS-III Q10 (cross-border cyber attacks and internal security)
**Front:** What does the 2021 cross-border cyber attack PYQ demand?
**Definition (learn):** Analyse the impact of cross-border cyber attacks on India's internal security (10 marks, 150 words).
**Breakdown:**
- Context: threat–vulnerability–consequence.
- Distinguish espionage, cybercrime, cyber terrorism and asserted state operation.
- Map CERT-In, NCIIPC, sectoral CERTs, I4C, State police.
- Do not claim attribution from a technical incident alone.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2021 GS-III Q10 (✅ owner 08 historical PYQ table; Core §12).
**Links:** IS-262, IS-256, IS-258.
**Trap:** Cross-border origin is not proven by an IP trail; attribution is a judgement.
**Mains hook:** Use attribution difficulty as the qualifier; conclude with resilience and recovery.
**Back:** *"Cross-border strikes: analyse, distinguish, map, and refuse easy blame."*

### Flashcard IS-270 — PYQ 2022 GS-III Q19 (cyber security elements and national strategy)
**Front:** What does the 2022 GS-III cyber PYQ demand and what is its test?
**Definition (learn):** "What are the different elements of cyber security? Keeping in view the challenges in cyber security, examine the extent to which India has successfully developed a comprehensive National Cyber Security Strategy." (Examine · 15 marks · 250 words; owner routes it as Q19.)
**Breakdown:**
- Elements of cyber security (confidentiality, integrity, availability as per owner's CIA triad, adv §Core).
- Challenges: attribution, supply chain, federal/private coordination.
- Test: comprehensive strategy = integrated policy plus implementation; 2013 policy is not a strategy by itself.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2022 GS-III Q19 (✅ owner 08 historical PYQ table; stem verified against scan).
**Links:** IS-251, IS-262, IS-266, IS-257.
**Trap:** Listing agencies is not assessing comprehensiveness.
**Mains hook:** Qualified conclusion: strong institutions, but strategy integration and supply-chain are unresolved.
**Back:** *"Elements, challenges, extent: the strategy test in twenty-two."*

### Flashcard IS-271 — PYQ 2024 Q10 (DPDP Act context and salient features)
**Front:** What is the 2024 DPDP PYQ demand and its answer route?
**Definition (learn):** "Describe the context and salient features of the Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023." (10 marks.)
**Breakdown:**
- Context: long-pending comprehensive data-protection framework after IT Rules-based privacy provisions.
- Features: consent, Data Protection Board, fiduciary obligations, penalties, children's data, cross-border transfer.
- Status: phased commencement (13 Nov 2025 / 13 Nov 2026 / 13 May 2027).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2024 Q10 (📰 owner 08 basic §9, adv §10; ✅ Wikipedia DPDP fetched for dates).
**Links:** IS-254, IS-255, IS-272.
**Trap:** Do not state the Rules were fully operational on notification.
**Mains hook:** Structure by category; qualify with rights-vs-readiness trade-off.
**Back:** *"Context, features, status: DPDP in three beats."*

### Flashcard IS-272 — DPDP phased-compliance trade-off (rights vs readiness)
**Front:** What trade-off does the DPDP phased-compliance design reflect?
**Definition (learn):** The 18-month runway for core compliance provisions prioritises industry readiness over immediate full enforcement of data-subject rights (owner adv §2, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Benefit: compliance readiness and orderly industry adoption.
- Cost: delayed full data-subject rights until 13 May 2027.
- Consent Manager regulated intermediary created from 13 Nov 2026.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** DPDP Act/Rules (✅ owner 08 adv §2, §6; dates ✅ Wikipedia fetched).
**Links:** IS-254, IS-255, IS-273.
**Trap:** A phased regime is not a design flaw; it is a stated trade-off. Do not gloss it as neglect.
**Mains hook:** "Rights-security balance" 15 marks; qualify with enforcement capacity.
**Back:** *"Readiness first, rights in full: May Twenty-Seven."*

### Flashcard IS-273 — Consent Manager registration (from 13 November 2026)
**Front:** What is a Consent Manager and when does its registration start?
**Definition (learn):** A regulated intermediary through which data principals give, manage or withdraw consent; registration rules (Rule 4) begin 13 November 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Start: 13 Nov 2026 (owner 08 basic §4).
- New regulated category: institutional design choice with compliance-industry implications.
- Relevant provision: s.6(9) effective 13 Nov 2026 (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** DPDP Rules 2025 Rule 4 (📰 owner 08); s.6(9) (✅ Wikipedia fetched).
**Links:** IS-255, IS-272.
**Trap:** Consent Manager registration is not the same as the Board being operative (13 Nov 2025).
**Mains hook:** Use under data-governance design; qualify with intermediary accountability.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Six Consent Manager registers the choice."*

### Flashcard IS-274 — Cyber insurance (PYQ 2020 Prelims)
**Front:** What does cyber insurance cover, and what does the 2020 Prelims PYQ test?
**Definition (learn):** Cyber insurance is a contractual risk-transfer tool covering defined financial-loss or cost exposure from cyber incidents; it does not prevent attacks.
**Breakdown:**
- Covers: defined financial loss and cost exposure.
- Does not: prevent an attack; does not remove vulnerability.
- Prelims PYQ: 2020 Q60 "Cyber insurance coverage benefits for individuals in India" (official key not held locally; owner declines to infer an answer).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2020 Prelims GS-I Q60 (✅ owner 08 historical PYQ table; key unavailable).
**Links:** IS-275, IS-276.
**Trap:** Do not infer an answer letter. Do not say insurance prevents an attack (owner Core §12 table).
**Mains hook:** Use as one tool in the risk layer; qualify that insurance is risk transfer, not security.
**Back:** *"Insurance pays the loss; it does not stop the hack."*

### Flashcard IS-275 — WannaCry, Petya and EternalBlue (PYQ 2018 Prelims)
**Front:** What are WannaCry, Petya and EternalBlue, and what does the 2018 Prelims test?
**Definition (learn):** WannaCry and Petya are ransomware/malware families; EternalBlue is an exploit that spread them via a Windows vulnerability. The 2018 Prelims PYQ asks about these terms as used in technology news.
**Breakdown:**
- Exploit: EternalBlue (vulnerability exploit).
- Malware/ransomware: WannaCry, Petya.
- Prelims test: statement-level distinction exploit vs malware vs ransomware.
- Official key not held locally.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** 2018 Prelims GS-I Q58 (✅ owner 08 historical PYQ table; key unavailable). ⚠️ Technical attribution of EternalBlue beyond owner text not fetched.
**Links:** IS-274, IS-262.
**Trap:** Exploit ≠ malware ≠ ransomware. Patching removes the exploit route; it does not undo damage already done.
**Mains hook:** Use under patch-and-preparedness; qualify with supply-chain and operator responsibility.
**Back:** *"Exploit opens the door, malware walks in, ransom holds the house."*

### Flashcard IS-276 — Three-column cyber resilience framework (20-mark comparison)
**Front:** How should a 20-mark "assess cyber resilience" answer be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Three linked but separate columns: (i) CII/CERT-In/NCIIPC prevention, detection, response and recovery; (ii) cybercrime/I4C/State-police investigation and victim redress; (iii) data governance/DPDP rights and compliance. Assess using CIA triad, CII interdependence, supply-chain/skill/operator constraints.
**Breakdown:**
- Column 1: incident chain.
- Column 2: crime chain.
- Column 3: data-governance chain.
- Verdict: resilience and recovery are more defensible than sweeping attribution claims.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 08 basic §12 (⚠️ answer architecture authored from owner text).
**Links:** IS-256, IS-257, IS-262, IS-272.
**Trap:** DPDP is not a security statute; do not merge column 3 into column 1.
**Mains hook:** Use as 20-mark template; qualified verdict (resilience over retaliation, privacy without converting DPDP into a security law).
**Back:** *"Incident, crime, data: three columns, never one."*

### Flashcard IS-277 — Telecom and CII security debate: resilience vs retaliation (concept)
**Front:** Why is resilience preferred to retaliation as the core cyber posture?
**Definition (learn):** Given attribution difficulty and supply-chain dependency, India's posture must emphasise resilience and rapid recovery (CERT-In's role) at least as much as retaliation-based deterrence (owner adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Constraint 1: attribution difficulty (structural).
- Constraint 2: import-dependent supply chain (industrial).
- Constraint 3: federal/private coordination.
- Output: recovery-first posture; longer-term supply-chain strategy.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 08 adv §1, §6, §11 (⚠️ analytical inference).
**Links:** IS-262, IS-260, IS-266, IS-276.
**Trap:** Do not present resilience as a substitute for an offensive capability assessment; it is a posture choice given structural limits.
**Mains hook:** "Attribution limits deterrence" 15-mark answer; calibrated way-forward.
**Back:** *"Recover first, deter second, attribute carefully."*

### GROUP — 09 Social Media, Encrypted Messaging and Information Warfare (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard IS-278 — Social media's dual character (positive vs negative use)
**Front:** What is the dual character of social media in internal security?
**Definition (learn):** Social media is internet-based communication that lets people "create, share and exchange" content directly, in real time and two-way, unlike one-way, media-house-controlled traditional media. The same speed, reach and decentralisation produce both positive and negative effects.
**Breakdown:**
- Positive: social awareness, cheap/fast communication, activism, debate, breaking media monopoly, participatory democracy (Singh PDF p. 118).
- Negative: riots, misinformation, terrorism/anti-national activity, false-opinion building, addiction, communal incitement (Singh PDF p. 117).
- Same property (speed, reach, decentralisation) drives both.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh PDF pp. 117-118 (✅ owner 09 basic §2-§3).
**Links:** IS-279, IS-281, IS-293, IS-310.
**Trap:** Do not present social media only as a threat; the answer must show the dual use and then the calibrated response.
**Mains hook:** "Distinguish positive and negative uses" (10 marks); qualify that the answer is about the response to the negative use, not restriction of the positive.
**Back:** *"Same speed for the awareness and the rumour: dual use, one pipe."*

### Flashcard IS-279 — Misinformation vs disinformation vs malinformation
**Front:** Distinguish misinformation, disinformation and malinformation.
**Definition (learn):** Misinformation = false content shared without intent to harm; disinformation = false content created and spread deliberately to deceive; malinformation = genuine information used out of context to cause harm.
**Breakdown:**
- Truth value: false (mis, dis); true (mal).
- Intent: no intent (mis); deliberate (dis, and malinformation's harm intent).
- Remedies: correction and literacy (mis); attribution and platform action (dis); contextual counter-speech (mal; takedown of true content raises the sharpest free-speech objection).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Owner 09 basic §2 (⚠️ analytical definitions authored from owner text; not fetched).
**Links:** IS-281, IS-280, IS-282.
**Trap:** The three terms are not synonyms; only disinformation is an adversarial information operation (owner 09 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Use to tighten "how do fake news threaten internal security" answers; qualify that malinformation is the hardest to remedy without rights costs.
**Back:** *"Mis is careless, Dis is designed, Mal is true but turned."*

### Flashcard IS-280 — Hybrid warfare: the five domains
**Front:** Name the five hybrid-warfare domains identified by VisionIAS.
**Definition (learn):** Hybrid warfare fuses conventional and unconventional instruments of power and subversion. VisionIAS names five domains: political (interference in political activity), technological (using tech capability to harm), military (IEDs, guerrilla tactics), economic (supply-chain disruption, counterfeit currency), social (exploiting social issues via propaganda).
**Breakdown:**
- P-T-M-E-S: political, technological, military, economic, social.
- Social domain is where fake news and identity-conflict tactics sit.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 18-19 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.2, §6).
**Links:** IS-281, IS-296, IS-278.
**Trap:** Hybrid warfare is not the same as cyber warfare (IS-242); it includes non-cyber domains.
**Mains hook:** Use to show information operations are one domain in a wider hybrid campaign.
**Back:** *"Political, Tech, Military, Economy, Social: P-T-M-E-S."*

### Flashcard IS-281 — Fake news (definition and types)
**Front:** Define fake news as a security threat.
**Definition (learn):** False or misleading information presented as authentic news to misinform, push an agenda or cause confusion; can be state-sponsored, commercially driven, or satire misused as fact (VisionIAS PDF p. 27).
**Breakdown:**
- Sources: state-sponsored; commercial; satire misused.
- Effect: confusion, panic, communal violence.
- Remedy triad: legal, platform, education (IS-313).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 27 (✅ owner 09 basic §2).
**Links:** IS-279, IS-293, IS-294, IS-295.
**Trap:** "Fake news" as a catch-all term blurs three distinct objects; use IS-279.
**Mains hook:** "How does fake news threaten internal security" — use Muzaffarnagar, North-East exodus and Delhi metro rumour as evidence.
**Back:** *"Fake news wears news clothes to make people act."*

### Flashcard IS-282 — IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, 2021
**Front:** What are the 2021 IT Rules, when did they commence, and what did they supersede?
**Definition (learn):** The Information Technology (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, 2021 are framed under Section 87 of the IT Act, 2000; they replaced the Information Technology (Intermediaries Guidelines) Rules, 2011.
**Breakdown:**
- Commenced: 25 February 2021 (Wikipedia fetched).
- Statutory base: s.87 (owner says s.87(2); Wikipedia fetched gives "section 87"; ⚠️ subsection not confirmed).
- Compliance deadline for intermediaries: 25 May 2021 (Wikipedia fetched).
- Architecture: Rules 3 (due diligence), 4 (significant social media intermediaries), 7 (safe harbour).
- Owner notes the 2021 Rules combined draft 2018 rules and the OTT/digital-media code (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Rules 2021 (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 09 basic §4).
**Links:** IS-283, IS-284, IS-285, IS-286, IS-299-IS-301.
**Trap:** The Rules are subordinate legislation under the IT Act, not an amendment to the Act. Do not date the Rules to 2022 or 2026.
**Mains hook:** Use as the platform-accountability framework; qualify that enforcement works through safe-harbour loss, not penalties.
**Back:** *"Twenty-One Rules, Eleven Rules superseded, Eighty-Seven their parent."*

### Flashcard IS-283 — Rule 3: due diligence and grievance redressal
**Front:** What does Rule 3 of the 2021 Rules require from intermediaries?
**Definition (learn):** Rule 3 sets due-diligence obligations and grievance-redressal duties for all intermediaries, including takedown on a lawful order and user grievance process.
**Breakdown:**
- Due diligence: publish rules and user agreements; act on lawful orders.
- Grievance redressal: Grievance Officer; time-bound resolution.
- Rule 3(1)(d): removal on a court order or appropriate-government notification (owner 09 basic §3.6).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Rule 3 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.6, §4).
**Links:** IS-282, IS-287, IS-286, IS-300.
**Trap:** A due-diligence duty is not the same as a particular takedown order (owner 09 basic §12 table).
**Mains hook:** Use to show platform accountability runs through defined legal process.
**Back:** *"Rule Three: diligence before the delete button."*

### Flashcard IS-284 — Rule 4(1): compliance officers for significant social media intermediaries
**Front:** Which compliance officers must a significant social media intermediary appoint under Rule 4(1)?
**Definition (learn):** Rule 4(1) requires a significant social media intermediary to appoint a Chief Compliance Officer, a Nodal Contact Person and a Resident Grievance Officer.
**Breakdown:**
- Chief Compliance Officer (CCO).
- Nodal Contact Person (for law enforcement coordination).
- Resident Grievance Officer (RGO).
- Wikipedia fetched confirms Rule 4(1)(a)-(c) roles.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Rule 4(1) (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 09 basic §3.6, §4).
**Links:** IS-282, IS-283, IS-285.
**Trap:** Rule 4(1) is about officers, not about traceability; traceability is Rule 4(2) (IS-285).
**Mains hook:** Use as concrete compliance layer; qualify with the grievance-appeal design (IS-300).
**Back:** *"Four-One: Chief, Nodal, Resident — three officers answer for the platform."*

### Flashcard IS-285 — Rule 4(2): first-originator traceability for messaging services
**Front:** What does Rule 4(2) require, and why is it contested?
**Definition (learn):** Rule 4(2) requires a significant social media intermediary providing messaging services to enable identification of the first originator of information, on a court order or a Section 69A competent-authority order, for specified serious offences. The rule is limited to India; a first originator abroad is treated as the first originator within India (Wikipedia fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Trigger: court order or s.69A competent authority order.
- Scope: specified serious offences.
- Demand: identify the first originator (metadata attribution), not decrypt content.
- Dispute: platforms say attribution at scale in end-to-end encryption needs architectural change; WhatsApp filed suit on 25 May 2021 (Wikipedia fetched).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Rule 4(2) (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 09 basic §3.3, adv §2); ⚠️ litigation status not re-verified.
**Links:** IS-282, IS-286, IS-304, IS-305, IS-310.
**Trap:** Rule 4(2) ≠ breaking encryption; it is a metadata identification demand. State the rule, state the objection, do not declare a winner.
**Mains hook:** Use in the encrypted-messaging answer: "architectural, not evidentiary" dispute; qualify that the rule is notified and contested.
**Back:** *"Four-Two finds the first hand, not the message in it."*

### Flashcard IS-286 — Rule 7: loss of Section 79 safe harbour
**Front:** What is the sanction for non-compliance under Rule 7?
**Definition (learn):** Under Rule 7, failure to observe due diligence costs the intermediary its Section 79 safe harbour, exposing it to liability for third-party content.
**Breakdown:**
- Sanction: loss of s.79 immunity (not a content-specific penalty).
- Consequence: liability for third-party content.
- Design effect: incentive for over-removal (adv §6).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Rule 7; IT Act s.79 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.3, §6; ✅ owner 09 adv §4).
**Links:** IS-283, IS-285, IS-307.
**Trap:** Rule 7 is indirect enforcement via safe-harbour loss, not a fine. Do not describe it as a penal provision.
**Mains hook:** "Efficient but blunt" enforcement; qualify with transparency reporting.
**Back:** *"Seven removes the shield, not the speech."*

### Flashcard IS-287 — Section 69A (content blocking) and Rule 3(1)(d)
**Front:** What does Section 69A allow, and how does Rule 3(1)(d) connect to it?
**Definition (learn):** Section 69A of the IT Act allows blocking of public access to information. Rule 3(1)(d) of the 2021 Rules provides removal on a court order or an appropriate-government notification.
**Breakdown:**
- Content level lever: s.69A (blocking).
- Rule 3(1)(d): removal trigger.
- Procedural safeguards required by Shreya Singhal (IS-290).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** IT Act s.69A; Rule 3(1)(d) (✅ owner 09 basic §3.3; ⚠️ not fetched from India Code).
**Links:** IS-248, IS-283, IS-290, IS-302.
**Trap:** 69A is a government blocking route; GAC (IS-300) is a user-appeal route against platform decisions; do not conflate.
**Mains hook:** Name 69A at the content level; qualify with process and review.
**Back:** *"Sixty-Nine-A blocks at source; Rule Three-One-D removes on order."*

### Flashcard IS-288 — Telecommunications Act, 2023: network-level lever
**Front:** Which law governs network-level interception and suspension of message transmission?
**Definition (learn):** The Telecommunications Act, 2023 governs network-level interception and suspension of message transmission on specified public-emergency/public-safety grounds, replacing the Indian Telegraph Act, 1885.
**Breakdown:**
- Replaces: Indian Telegraph Act 1885 (and Wireless Telegraphy Act 1933 per infobox).
- Powers: take over, manage, suspend telecom services; suspend equipment; interception and decryption (IS-253).
- Owner's s.20 reference is ⚠️ unverified.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Telecommunications Act, 2023 (✅ Wikipedia fetched; ✅ owner 09 basic §3.5).
**Links:** IS-253, IS-289, IS-248.
**Trap:** The IT Act governs content and platforms; the Telecom Act governs network suspension. Keep them distinct.
**Mains hook:** Use as the shutdown lever; qualify with Anuradha Bhasin.
**Back:** *"Telecom Act holds the network; IT Act holds the content."*

### Flashcard IS-289 — Anuradha Bhasin v. Union of India (2020) — internet suspension standard
**Front:** What did Anuradha Bhasin (2020) hold on internet suspension?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court held that indefinite internet suspension is impermissible; suspension orders must be published and are judicially reviewable; restrictions must satisfy proportionality and be periodically reviewed.
**Breakdown:**
- Indefinite suspension: impermissible.
- Publication: orders must be published.
- Review: judicially reviewable; periodically reviewed.
- Standard: necessity and proportionality.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Anuradha Bhasin v. Union of India (2020) (✅ owner 09 basic §2, §6; ⚠️ Wikipedia page 404 in this pass — not independently fetched).
**Links:** IS-288, IS-291, IS-315.
**Trap:** A legal power to suspend is not proof a shutdown is necessary; "shutdown as default" is the trap (owner 09 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** "Proportionality, not prohibition" (15 marks); qualify that shutdowns can be short-term containment only.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Twenty: no endless silence, proof published, review always."*

### Flashcard IS-290 — Shreya Singhal (2015) and the blocking procedure
**Front:** What did Shreya Singhal (2015) require for blocking orders?
**Definition (learn):** Shreya Singhal v. Union of India (2015) struck down Section 66A and required written reasons, hearing and review-committee process for any blocking order (as per owner 09 adv §8 and the PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**
- Struck down: s.66A (vagueness; free speech).
- Blocking standard: written reasons; process; review committee.
- Standard against which Sahyog portal is criticised (IS-302).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Shreya Singhal v. Union of India (2015) (✅ owner 09 basic §4, adv §8; ✅ owner 08 basic §7).
**Links:** IS-263, IS-287, IS-302.
**Trap:** The blocking-procedure requirement is a Shreya Singhal holding; 66A is a separate holding. Do not cite 66A as usable.
**Mains hook:** Use to anchor the platform-vs-state boundary; qualify that the review-committee design sits in the blocking rules.
**Back:** *"Shreya: strike the vague, write the reasons."*

### Flashcard IS-291 — Christchurch Call to Action (India signatory)
**Front:** What is the Christchurch Call and has India signed it?
**Definition (learn):** An international declaration against terrorist and violent extremist content online; VisionIAS records India as a signatory (PDF p. 28).
**Breakdown:**
- Aim: eliminate terrorist and violent-extremist content online.
- Status: India has signed (owner 09 basic §6).
- Dimension: IR owns diplomacy; Internal Security owns domestic accountability.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF pp. 23, 28 (✅ owner 09 basic §4, §6). ⚠️ Signing date not stated in owner; not fetched.
**Links:** IS-292, IS-297, IS-289.
**Trap:** Christchurch Call is a declaration, not a treaty; do not present it as binding law.
**Mains hook:** Use to show international cooperation; qualify with extraterritorial limits (IS-297).
**Back:** *"Christchurch Call: declared, not enforced by a court."*

### Flashcard IS-292 — UNSC Counter-Terrorism Committee Delhi Declaration (new and emerging technologies)
**Front:** What is the UNSC CTC Delhi Declaration?
**Definition (learn):** A declaration of the UN Security Council Counter-Terrorism Committee on countering terrorist use of new and emerging technologies, cited by VisionIAS (PDF p. 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Body: UNSC Counter-Terrorism Committee.
- Subject: terrorist use of new and emerging technologies.
- Status in owner: international partnership reference.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 23 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.3). ⚠️ Year and text not fetched.
**Links:** IS-291, IS-288.
**Trap:** Do not treat it as an enforceable Indian statute.
**Mains hook:** Use under international level of response.
**Back:** *"Delhi declares: new tech, old terror, shared response."*

### Flashcard IS-293 — 2013 Muzaffarnagar riots and the fake video
**Front:** How did fake online content feature in the 2013 Western UP riots?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS (PDF p. 28) records that a 2013 fake WhatsApp-shared video contributed to Western Uttar Pradesh communal riots that killed 62 and displaced 50,000.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2013.
- Mechanism: fake video shared via WhatsApp, identity-conflict amplification.
- Toll: 62 killed; 50,000 displaced (owner-cited).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 28 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.3). ⚠️ Toll and displacement figures are source-cited, not verified in this pass.
**Links:** IS-281, IS-294, IS-295, IS-312.
**Trap:** A single fake post does not prove violence; the case is an illustration of amplification (owner 09 basic §12).
**Mains hook:** Use under "fake news threat to public order"; qualify with root-cause (identity conflict) and rumour-as-catalyst.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Thirteen video, sixty-two lost, fifty thousand moved."*

### Flashcard IS-294 — 2012 North-East exodus panic (SMS/MMS messages)
**Front:** What happened in the 2012 North-East exodus from southern cities?
**Definition (learn):** Provocative SMS/MMS and internet messages triggered a real exodus of North-East people from southern cities under fear of attack (Singh PDF p. 48, cross-referenced to topic 04).
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2012.
- Mechanism: SMS/MMS/internet messages.
- Effect: mass movement from southern cities.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** Singh PDF p. 48 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.3; topic 04).
**Links:** IS-293, IS-281, IS-309.
**Trap:** Owner uses the case as an identity-conflict example, not a standalone communalism topic (owner 09 basic §7).
**Mains hook:** Use as a fake-content-to-displacement example.
**Back:** *"Twenty-Twelve messages, a city emptied in fear."*

### Flashcard IS-295 — March 2020 Delhi metro rumour
**Front:** What social-turmoil effect did a March 2020 false rumour cause?
**Definition (learn):** VisionIAS (PDF p. 27) cites a March 2020 false rumour of violence in Delhi that caused temporary metro-station shutdowns.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: March 2020.
- Effect: temporary metro-station shutdowns.
- Category: law-and-order disruption via rumour.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** VisionIAS PDF p. 27 (✅ owner 09 basic §3.4). ⚠️ Not independently verified.
**Links:** IS-281, IS-293, IS-310.
